
class Component extends DCLogic {
  // ══ PROVENANCE ═════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
  // Read this before building anything from this file.

  // so the header is the part that can go stale — and it did, on 2026-09-01,

  // disagree you are holding the wrong file — stop") would have halted a build
  // on a perfectly good file. The instruction stands for a MISSING or OLDER
  // stamp; it does not apply when the dated blocks inside the file prove it is
  // newer than its own header. The content is the fact; the header is a label.

  // WHY THIS STAMP EXISTS. File transport between the design side and the build
  // side is by hand, so a stale copy is always possible. On 2026-08-31 a build
  // session read a superseded ATH template for an hour, and three tests broke
  // because they hardcoded a drop directory that had moved. Neither was
  // detectable from inside the file.
  //
  // SO: compare this DROP line against project_state/design/CURRENT_DROP.json
  // before you build. If they disagree, you are holding the wrong file — stop.
  // Never hardcode a drop directory; read the pointer.

  // ONE SOURCE OF TRUTH. There is no readable design project; the zip is the
  // channel and this stamp is how you tell whether your copy is current. If you
  // are ever handed two files that both claim to be the drop, the one whose

  // ══ RULINGS THAT OVERRIDE THIS FILE'S OWN EARLIER DRAWINGS ══════════════
  // Written 2026-08-31 after a build-side parity sweep found four places where
  // this file still carried behaviour Tom had already overruled — so both sides
  // could cite an authority and a "fidelity pass" would have restored the

  // These are settled. DO NOT re-derive them from older frames or specs:
  //
  //  1. NO IMAGE PLACEHOLDERS (Tom, 2026-08-25). An artist with no photograph
  //     is NOT on the wall. The hatched "no photo yet" tile and the
  //     "40 more pending →" footer this file used to draw are GONE. Counts
  //     count what is shown.
  //  2a. [SUPERSEDED 2026-09-01] "HOVER AUDIO DEFAULTS OFF (Tom,
  //      2026-08-19)." REPLACED BY RULING 7 — the default is per view, not

  //      not the transport switch drawn here." OVERTURNED BY RULING 6. The

  //      because 2b is what a session cited when it removed the switch, and a
  //      reader who finds that citation needs to land on this line.
  //  3. LABEL NAV follows Tom's 2026-08-18 order, and RADIO IS NORTHERN SPY
  //     ONLY — not "every label except NNA" as this file used to say.
  //  4. THE ATH ARTISTS INDEX IS THE BY-DROP PAGE (Tom, 2026-08-25, "the
  //     originals win"). The shared-mosaic version below is superseded.
  //  5. CATALOGUE IS 5 ACROSS ON EVERY DESKTOP WIDTH, 2 on a phone. The
  //     "5 / 7 / 8 across" line was stale prose; the code was right.
  //
  //  6. THE TRANSPORT'S HOVER-AUDIO SWITCH STAYS (Tom, 2026-09-01), and so does
  //     the curator map's corner switch. Both are live and correct. This
  //     OVERTURNS 2b. Hidden at narrow only. ⚠ THE REASON GIVEN HERE — "a
  //     phone has no hover to arm" — IS SUPERSEDED (2026-09-10): touch is the
  //     phone's hover, see touchHoverBridge(). The control stays out of the
  //     390px header for ROOM, and lives in the ☰ sheet instead; the mode
  //     itself is fully available there. Placement, not capability.
  //
  //  7. HOVER AUDIO'S DEFAULT IS PER VIEW (Tom, 2026-09-01), replacing 2a:
  //     ON in any starmap view · OFF in grid view · ALWAYS ON on the ATH
  //     curator map, which keeps its own corner switch. A grid has play
  //     buttons, so nothing is lost by starting it quiet; the star field has
  //     no other way to be heard. A visitor's own toggle always wins.
  //
  //  8. THE BROADCAST TIGHT CUT DOES NOT SURVIVE REAL DATA (2026-09-01).
  //     I cut the strand rows at tight because every one of them read
  //     'Episode title pending' — filler, and a card carries fine without
  //     filler. The feed is now wired and those rows are real, dated,
  //     tappable episodes, so the premise is gone. Deleting three real
  //     episodes is not the same act as deleting three placeholders.
  //       narrow → 2 rows.  tight → 1 row, NOT 0.
  //     One row keeps the feed present, keeps the numbered-row affordance,
  //     and spends one row of height. Zero says the show has no back
  //     catalogue. The 3→2 step at narrow stands on its own: a third row is
  //     a third of the card whether or not it is filler.
  //     GENERAL RULE: where I cut something because it was a placeholder,
  //     the cut expires when the real thing lands. Where I cut for space,
  //     it does not. If a cut's reason is content, ask me again once the
  //     data is real — do not carry it over silently.
  //
  //  9. THE ATH MAP'S NEXT-PANEL NAV IS NOT FULL-WIDTH (2026-09-01). It was
  //     left:0/right:0 and swallowed the corner hover-audio switch, which
  //     rulings 6 and 7 both protect. A labelled control gets a labelled-
  //     sized hit box; an invisible full-bleed target that eats a sibling is
  //     wrong regardless of who was drawn first. See panelNavStyle.
  //
  // ── WHAT THIS BLOCK CANNOT DO ─────────────────────────────────────────────
  // On 2026-09-01 a build session deleted the transport switch, citing ruling 2b,
  // a caption of mine and a comment of mine. Every citation was accurate and in
  // order. Tom reversed it the same day. Two lessons, both bigger than the
  // switch:
  //
  //   A COMMENT IS NOT A GUARD. Prose loses to a fresh instruction every time.
  //   The build side has pinned the switch with a RED TEST that quotes Tom's
  //   ruling in its failure message, and that is the only mechanism either side
  //   has found that holds. Anything here that must not be removed should be
  //   pinned that way on their side, not merely asserted in this file.
  //
  //   AN OUT-OF-DATE AUTHORITY IS WORSE THAN NONE. This block carried Tom's
  //   2026-08-19 decision and not his 2026-09-01 one, so following it
  //   faithfully produced the wrong build — and produced it confidently,
  //   with citations. Keeping this list current is the whole of its value.
  //
  // Where a comment elsewhere in this file disagrees with the list above, the
  // list wins and the comment is a leftover — say so rather than building it.
  // ═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
  // ⚠ DECLARED BEFORE `state`, AND THAT ORDER IS LOAD-BEARING (2026-09-09,
  // code audit part 1). Class fields initialise in declaration order, and
  // `state = this.arrival(…)` is itself a field — so with ROUTES declared
  // below it, `this.ROUTES` was undefined inside arrival() and every
  // host-published __DC_ROUTE threw in the constructor. Moved, not guarded:
  // a `|| []` here would have turned a hard failure into a silent one, which
  // is the same trade the DEFAULT_JOURNAL lookup made and lost (see
  // arrival()'s article branch).

  // ── THE CLOSED ROUTE LIST (2026-09-07, §4-1) ──────────────────────────────
  // Every route this file draws, and nothing else. It exists because the host
  // is about to stop slicing 31 copies and start publishing window.__DC_ROUTE
  // into ONE served file: a route arriving from outside has to be checked
  // against something, and a list is the only honest check. An unknown value is
  // IGNORED — the baked route stands — rather than rendered as a blank page.
  //
  // 'notfound' and 'error' are here as designed surfaces (§4-4, §4-5), not as
  // states: the host routes to them the same way it routes to anything else.
  ROUTES = [
    'star', 'landing', 'catalogue', 'artists', 'artist', 'record', 'radio', 'tour', 'news', 'about', 'group',
    // ⚠ portal-category WAS DRAWN AND NOT LISTED (2026-09-09, code audit §6).
    // isPortalCategory renders a real surface — data-surface="portal-category",
    // reachable from the route picker — but the route was missing from this
    // list, and this list is a CLOSED CHECK: an inbound __DC_ROUTE that is not
    // in it is ignored. The host stages the route, so a live category URL would
    // have been refused at the door and landed on the label's landing instead.
    // Listing it makes the address legal; nothing links to it until Tom rules
    // on whether category pages are real URLs.
    'portal-category',
    'ath-map', 'ath-about', 'ath-artists', 'ath-all-releases', 'ath-release', 'ath-artist',
    'ath-journal', 'ath-article', 'ath-subscribe', 'ath-broadcast', 'ath-track',
    'cls', 'cls-pr', 'cls-radio', 'cls-management', 'cls-licensing', 'cls-distribution',
    'cls-fulfillment', 'cls-manufacturing',
    'notfound', 'error'
  ];

  // ── THE CLOSED VIEW LIST (2026-09-09) ─────────────────────────────────────
  // What the Starmap's column can show — the counterpart to ROUTES, and here for
  // the same reason: goToNav takes a view from outside and the column has no
  // default branch, so an unknown value drew nothing at all. Declared above
  // `state` for the same field-order reason ROUTES is.
  VIEWS = ['releases', 'artists', 'results', 'album', 'tour', 'news', 'about', 'radio', 'ath-curator'];

  state = this.arrival({ view: 'releases', hoverRow: null, hoverStar: null, lock: null, prev: null, open: true,
            queue: false, qshuffle: false, qhover: null, qplaying: 1, qover: false,
            theme: 'light', vp: 0, label: 0, avail: 1180, started: false,
            // ── GRID IS THE ARRIVAL, EVERYWHERE (2026-09-09, Tom) ────────────
            // "I want all the pages, all the sites, to default to grid view and
            // hover audio, and then people can switch from there." The star map
            // was the arrival for the whole file; it is now a mode the visitor
            // chooses, reached from the View toggle, the hamburger or /map. The
            // label landing is the grid front page — the same route the Grid
            // toggle returns you to from the star field.
            route: 'landing',
            // null, not a name. It was 'Jeremy Cunningham' — the mock's first
            // artist — which leaked onto every non-artist route on the live site
            // as the one mock name left after real data was injected. Resolved
            // to PAIRS[0][0] at first use via curArtist(), so it is always a real
            // name from whatever data is present.
            artist: null, sort: 0,
            // The Clandestine enquiry form: which categories are open, which
            // questions are ticked. Both empty until someone engages — the page
            // is quiet by default and the form is the one thing that unfolds.
            clsFormOpen: false, clsSvc: {}, clsPick: {}, clsFields: {}, clsSent: false, clsSending: false, clsFail: false,
            // Live window size, site mode only. Null in the tool, where VPS
            // supplies the viewport.
            winW: null, winH: null,
            // WHAT IS PLAYING, AND WHETHER IT IS PAUSED (2026-09-01).
            // The grid's ▶ set `started: true` and nothing else, so the button
            // had no state to show: it stayed ▶ forever, and because it only
            // rendered on hover it vanished the moment the cursor left — a
            // record could be playing with nothing on the page saying which.
            // npPlay is the record the visitor pressed play on; paused is the
            // transport's own state. Both are null/false until someone plays.
            npPlay: null, paused: false,
            // RULING 7 (Tom, 2026-09-01) — the default is PER VIEW, not global.
            // null means the visitor has not touched the switch, so the view
            // decides; true/false is their own choice and it sticks. See
            // hoverAudioOn below.
            hoverAudio: null,
            // Ported with playExt / imgAR (2026-09-09): the sounding external
            // episode, and the tick that re-renders once a measured image ratio
            // lands. Both null/0 until something happens, per the same rule as
            // npPlay above — the honest placeholder for "has this occurred?" is no.
            extPlay: null, arTick: 0,
            // Which curator-map circle was pressed, so its ❙❙ is its own. null
            // until someone presses one, like npPlay above.
            mapSeat: null,
    // ── GUIDE (2026-09-10) ──────────────────────────────────────────────
    // guideIntro  the arrival orientation is up
    // guideStep   0 nothing · 1 VIEW lit · 2 AUDIO lit · 3 actions
    // guideTip    the id of the one context tip on screen, or null
    // ⚠ NONE OF THIS IS READ FROM STORAGE HERE. localStorage is touched in
    // componentDidMount, never in a class-field initialiser — `state` is
    // itself a field, and this file has already been bitten once by a field
    // reading something declared below it (arrival() and ROUTES).
    guideIntro: false, guideStep: 0, guideTip: null, guideAt: null, xpTick: 0 });

  // Superseded by INDEX below, which carries the same 3a values plus the
  // measured aspect ratio each mosaic tile is laid out from.

  // A designed page used as a deck panel must fit ONE screen: measure the body
  // after paint and scale it down just enough, so nothing scrolls and nothing
  // is cut off. Runs after every render, since React rewrites the transform.
  // The measured centring correction, shared by both centred branches of the
  // fit ladder (2026-09-05). See the call site for why measuring beats
  // reasoning about the padding box.
  //
  // ⚠ THE RECTS ARE IN SCREEN PIXELS AND THE TRANSFORM IS IN LAYOUT PIXELS.
  // The tool scales the whole artboard (0.75 at the 1180 stage), so applying a
  // screen-space delta directly undershot by exactly that factor — 12–15px of
  // residual on desktop while the unscaled phone stage measured 0. Dividing by
  // the stage scale is what makes the correction exact at every width; it is
  // the same screen-vs-layout confusion that made the See-our-work jump land
  // 1300px short, so it is worth recognising on sight.
  // The tool scales the artboard, so a client rect is in screen px while a
  // style value is in layout px. Anything that measures and then writes a
  // length must divide by this (2026-09-06) — the same trap that made the
  // card-centring correction undershoot and the See-our-work jump land short.
  _stageK(el) {
    const stage = el && el.closest ? el.closest('[data-surface]') : null;
    const box = stage || (el && el.offsetParent);
    if (!box || !box.offsetHeight) return 1;
    const k = box.getBoundingClientRect().height / box.offsetHeight;
    return (k > 0.05 && k < 20) ? k : 1;
  }
  _centreIn(panel, el) {
    const pr = panel.getBoundingClientRect();
    const r = el.getBoundingClientRect();
    if (!r.height) return;
    const stage = panel.offsetHeight ? (pr.height / panel.offsetHeight) : 1;
    const k = (stage > 0.05 && stage < 20) ? stage : 1;
    // ── THE NAV BAND IS RESERVED, AT EVERY SIZE (2026-09-06, Tom) ───────────
    // ⚠ THIS IS THE RULE THAT KEEPS BEING RE-DERIVED WRONG. Read it before
    // changing anything in this routine.
    //
    // The panel's padding-bottom IS the deck's nav band — the "BROADCAST ↓"
    // cue that takes you to the next card. It is not slack. The fit ladder
    // already subtracts it when sizing (see `avail`), but this centring pass
    // measured against the panel's FULL rect at width, so it translated every
    // card ~39px back down INTO the reserve the fit had just protected. Two
    // passes disagreeing, and the fit's reserve lost.
    //
    // Measured at 1180 before this: the drops card ended 50px above the panel
    // floor against a cue that needs 50 — zero slack, so any window height
    // where a card grew a few pixels put content over the button. That is the
    // report, twice.
    //
    // The band is now excluded AT EVERY WIDTH and never by less than
    // CUE_RESERVE, so the guarantee does not depend on a padding value someone
    // may tune later. Content scales; the button's margin is absolute.
    const cs = getComputedStyle(panel);
    const chrome = k * Math.max(parseFloat(cs.paddingBottom) || 0, 56);
    const delta = (((pr.top + (pr.bottom - chrome)) / 2) - ((r.top + r.bottom) / 2)) / k;
    if (Math.abs(delta) < 0.5) return;
    // Composed BEFORE the scale, so the translate is read in unscaled layout
    // space. Appending it instead meant the card's own scale multiplied it —
    // a 1.35 card overshot by 35% and read 14px out, a 1.5 card by half and
    // read 17px out, while the unscaled 1180 and phone stages were exact. The
    // order of a transform list is the bug you cannot see in the value.
    const sc = el.style.transform ? ' ' + el.style.transform : '';
    el.style.transform = 'translateY(' + delta.toFixed(2) + 'px)' + sc;
  }
  fitPanels() {
    const pane = document.querySelector('.sa-scroll');
    if (pane && pane.clientHeight) this._athPane = pane;
    // ── The deck as CARDS ────────────────────────────────────────────────
    // Every panel is stacked in the same place; only the active one is visible
    // and interactive. Nothing scrolls, so you can never sit between two ideas.
    if (this._isDeck && pane) {
      const active = this.state.panel || 0;
      for (let i = 0; i < pane.children.length; i++) {
        const c = pane.children[i];
        const d = i - active;
        // !important, because three of these cards take their style from a
        // template hole: React rewrites that style attribute on every render and
        // would otherwise wipe these writes, freezing the deck on one card.
        const set = (prop, val) => c.style.setProperty(prop, val, 'important');
        set('position', 'absolute');
        set('left', '0'); set('right', '0'); set('top', '0'); set('bottom', '0');
        set('height', 'auto');
        set('transition', 'opacity 380ms cubic-bezier(.4,0,.2,1), transform 380ms cubic-bezier(.4,0,.2,1)');
        set('opacity', d === 0 ? '1' : '0');
        set('transform', d === 0 ? 'none' : 'translateY(' + (d > 0 ? 26 : -26) + 'px)');
        set('pointer-events', d === 0 ? 'auto' : 'none');
        set('z-index', d === 0 ? '2' : '1');
      }
      pane.scrollTop = 0;
    }
    // ── Vertical centring of the map, DERIVED ────────────────────────────
    // The canvas box is not the composition: Volume One's band carries a top
    // margin and Volume Two's names hang below its last face row, so the box
    // centre sits below the visual centre. Measure the real cluster extents
    // against the panel's content box — whose padding mirrors the header bar
    // and the floating transport — and converge the offset to equal gaps.
    const mc = document.querySelector('.ath-map-canvas');
    const restingMap = this.state.pillHover == null && !this.state.sel
      && !(this.state.selTrio && this.state.selTrio.length);
    if (mc && mc.offsetParent && restingMap) {
      const panel = mc.parentElement;
      const lockKey = String(this.state.vp) + ':' + Math.round(panel.clientHeight);
      if (this._mapYKey === lockKey) {
        // Already resolved for this viewport — apply the held value and do not
        // measure again, so returning to rest never re-adjusts. NOT an early
        // return: the .ath-fit-inner pass below still has to run, or a locked
        // viewport revisiting the map route leaves every panel unscaled.
        if (this._mapY != null) mc.style.setProperty('--mapy', this._mapY.toFixed(1) + 'px');
      } else {
      const pr = panel.getBoundingClientRect();
      const pcs = getComputedStyle(panel);
      const topEdge = pr.top + parseFloat(pcs.paddingTop || 0);
      const botEdge = pr.bottom - parseFloat(pcs.paddingBottom || 0);
      let t = Infinity, b2 = -Infinity, found = 0;
      const spans = mc.querySelectorAll('span');
      for (let i = 0; i < spans.length; i++) {
        const x = spans[i], xs = getComputedStyle(x);
        if (xs.position !== 'absolute' || xs.display !== 'flex' || x.children.length !== 2) continue;
        const r2 = x.getBoundingClientRect();
        if (!r2.height) continue;
        if (r2.top < t) t = r2.top;
        if (r2.bottom > b2) b2 = r2.bottom;
        found++;
      }
      if (found > 4) {
        // page px → stage px, since the whole stage is scaled to the viewport
        const stage = panel.closest('[data-theme]');
        const sc = stage ? (stage.getBoundingClientRect().width / (stage.offsetWidth || 1)) : 1;
        const delta = (((botEdge - b2) - (t - topEdge)) / 2) / (sc || 1);
        if (Math.abs(delta) > 0.6) {
          this._mapY = (this._mapY || 0) + delta;
          mc.style.setProperty('--mapy', this._mapY.toFixed(1) + 'px');
          // Converge: writing the property does not re-render, so run the pass
          // again on the next frame until the two gaps agree. Bounded, so a
          // layout that cannot settle can never spin.
          this._mapPass = (this._mapPass || 0) + 1;
          if (this._mapPass < 8) requestAnimationFrame(() => this.fitPanels());
        } else {
          // Converged: hold this value for this viewport.
          this._mapPass = 0;
          this._mapYKey = lockKey;
        }
      }
      }
    }
    const inners = document.querySelectorAll('.ath-fit-inner');
    for (let i = 0; i < inners.length; i++) {
      const el = inners[i];
      const panel = el.parentElement;
      if (!panel) continue;
      // FITTED: deck cards, and the card-shaped routes off the deck. A LIST
      // route keeps its own scroll and is never fitted — see _fitRoute.
      //
      // This used to test _isDeck alone, which made "every ATH page is an app
      // screen" true only on the deck: Subscribe on its own URL centred and then
      // scrolled, because nothing scaled it. The fit-or-scroll ladder below
      // already handles the case where a card cannot shrink far enough, so the
      // only thing that was missing was permission to try.
      if (!this._fitRoute) { el.style.transformOrigin = ''; el.style.transform = ''; el.style.height = ''; panel.style.overflowY = ''; continue; }
      const cs = getComputedStyle(panel);
      // The nav band is reserved before anything is sized — see _centreIn's
      // note. Never less than 56, so a card can be scaled down but the button's
      // margin cannot be scaled away (2026-09-06, Tom).
      const avail = panel.clientHeight - parseFloat(cs.paddingTop || 0) - Math.max(parseFloat(cs.paddingBottom || 0), 56) - 8;
      if (avail <= 0) { el.style.transform = ''; continue; }
      el.style.transform = '';
      el.style.height = '';
      const h = el.scrollHeight;
      const want = avail / h;
      // FIT OR SCROLL, never crop: shrink only as far as the type stays
      // readable, and if a card still will not fit, let that one card scroll
      // inside itself rather than hiding content with no way to reach it.
      // The floor is lower on a SHORT viewport — an SE leaves ~483px of card
      // where a 390×844 leaves ~740, and 0.80 of the phone type is still well
      // above the readable minimum. These cards explain the whole series, so
      // holding all their information matters more than the last 6%.
      const floorK = panel.clientHeight < 560 ? 0.8 : 0.86;
      // A TRANSFORM DOES NOT CHANGE LAYOUT HEIGHT. Flex centring places the child
      // by its unscaled height, so a scaled card was centred as though it were
      // still full size and its top half was pushed above the panel and clipped
      // — unreachable, since the panel does not scroll on this branch. Pin the
      // element's layout height to the height actually painted, and the centred
      // box becomes the box you can see.
      // ── A CARD MAY GROW, NOT ONLY SHRINK (2026-09-04, Tom: at 16" and 20"
      // "the card content is too small … shrunk in the middle") ──────────────
      // This ladder only ever scaled DOWN, so a card authored at the 1180
      // measure kept that measure at 1728 and 2048 and sat as a small block in
      // the middle of a very large screen. A card that FITS with room to spare
      // now scales UP to take the room — the authored composition enlarged, not
      // a re-layout — bounded three ways:
      //   · the height available (never taller than its panel),
      //   · the panel's width (never wider, so nothing reaches the chrome),
      //   · CEIL, because past about a quarter again the type stops being the
      //     size it was drawn at and starts being a zoom.
      // Cards that already fill their panel compute ≈1 and do not move, which
      // is why the curator map and the drops card look exactly as before. The
      // map is excluded outright: its canvas does its own geometry and a
      // transform over that would fight the measured centring above.
      // The ceiling is per size, because the cards' own measures are narrow —
      // a text column is 400–700px whatever the screen, so the wrapper's
      // max-width is never what binds; the ceiling is. 1.35 puts a 700px card
      // at ~945 on a 16", and 1.5 puts it at ~1050 on a 20", which reads as a
      // page rather than a token. Past that the type is a zoom, not a size.
      const CEIL = panel.clientWidth >= 2000 ? 1.5 : 1.35;
      const growable = panel.clientWidth > 1400 && !el.querySelector('.ath-map-canvas');
      if (h <= avail) {
        const roomW = el.offsetWidth ? (panel.clientWidth - 8) / el.offsetWidth : 1;
        const up = Math.min(avail / h, roomW, CEIL);
        if (growable && up > 1.02) {
          // ── WHY THIS CARD WAS NEVER CENTRED (2026-09-05, Tom, third report) ──
          // The two halves of the r9 pass fought each other, and I fixed each
          // half twice without ever seeing the interaction:
          //
          //   · transform-origin is 'top center', so a scaled card paints
          //     DOWNWARD from its layout top;
          //   · el.style.height was pinned to the SCALED height (h × up).
          //
          // Flex then centred a box of height h×up whose paint BEGAN at that
          // box's top and ran h×up further down — so the painted card sat half
          // a scale-height below the centre of what had been measured: hard
          // against the top, with all the surplus dumped underneath. Exactly
          // the screenshot. Neither half is wrong alone; together they
          // double-count the growth, which is why fixing either one in
          // isolation changed nothing.
          //
          // Growing needs neither. CENTER origin makes the scale expand
          // symmetrically about the flex-centred box, and the layout height is
          // left at h so flex centres the thing that is actually painted.
          // \`up\` is already bounded by avail / h, so the painted card can
          // never exceed the panel and nothing is clipped.
          //
          // The shrink branches below keep the top origin and the pinned
          // height, which is correct THERE: scaling down, the unscaled h
          // exceeds avail, and pinning is what stops the top being pushed above
          // the panel and clipped (r9's own note, still true).
          el.style.transformOrigin = 'center center';
          el.style.transform = 'scale(' + up.toFixed(4) + ')';
          el.style.height = '';
        } else {
          el.style.transformOrigin = '';
          el.style.transform = '';
          el.style.height = '';
        }
        panel.style.overflowY = 'hidden';
        panel.style.alignItems = 'center';
        // ── AND THEN FORCE IT (2026-09-05, Tom: "force it to be centred") ─────
        // Flex centres within the panel's CONTENT box. This panel has
        // padding-bottom: 78px (clearance for the deck's nav) and no padding
        // top, so "centred" landed ~39px above the centre a person sees — the
        // residual that survived the origin fix, constant across every card
        // because it is geometry rather than content.
        //
        // Arguing about whether the content box is the right frame is how this
        // stayed open across three reports. It is not: a card in a full-bleed
        // panel should read as centred IN THE PANEL. So the pass measures what
        // it actually painted and corrects the difference — which also absorbs
        // any future padding, chrome or scale change without another visit.
        this._centreIn(panel, el);
      }
      else if (want >= floorK) { el.style.transformOrigin = ''; el.style.transform = 'scale(' + want.toFixed(4) + ')'; el.style.height = Math.round(h * want) + 'px'; panel.style.overflowY = 'hidden'; panel.style.alignItems = 'center'; this._centreIn(panel, el); }
      // A CENTRED flex child that overflows is clipped at its TOP and cannot be
      // scrolled back to — which is what cut the eyebrow off the broadcast card
      // on a phone. Once the panel scrolls it stops centring.
      else { el.style.transformOrigin = ''; el.style.transform = 'scale(' + floorK + ')'; el.style.height = Math.round(h * floorK) + 'px'; panel.style.overflowY = 'auto'; panel.style.alignItems = 'flex-start'; }
    }
  }

  // ⚠ THE ONLY componentDidUpdate IN THIS CLASS. A second definition anywhere
  // below silently REPLACES this one — no error, no warning — and the first
  // thing to stop happening is the ATH card fit pass, which every centred card
  // depends on. It happened on 2026-09-03: the star bridge added its own, and
  // Broadcast and Subscribe went from scale(0.909)/scale(0.911) to unscaled and
  // clipped on every in-app navigation. componentDidMount still fits once at
  // 60ms, so a hard reload into a card route looked correct while navigating to
  // one did not — which is why it survived a check.
  // ANYTHING THAT NEEDS AN UPDATE HOOK GOES IN HERE, AS A CALL.
  // ── THE HOST HEARS EVERY NAVIGATION (2026-09-03) ─────────────────────────
  // Explore, the nav, Grid|Map, a cover, a star: anything that changes WHERE
  // the visitor is fires window.__DC_ON_NAV with one tuple, after commit. The
  // host uses it to re-scope data (INDEX/PAIRS for a new label), mirror the
  // URL, and re-frame the field. One channel, so the host never has to watch
  // st.label or diff renderVals to find out.
  //
  //   window.__DC_ON_NAV = ({ label, route, view, record, artist, ath }) => …
  //
  //   label   'northern-spy' | 'nna-tapes' | 'egghunt' | 'whited-sepulchre' | 'across-the-horizon'
  //   route   the current route string · view  the star column's view
  //   record  PAIRS index of the locked/opened record, or null
  //   artist  the artist page's name, or null · ath  true on any ath-* route
  //
  // Fires only when one of those changes; hover never fires it. Not fired on
  // first paint — the host set that state itself through the arrival keys.
  // ── THE TRANSPORT BRIDGE · window.__DC_ON_TRANSPORT ────────────────────
  // The site owns audio; this file owns every play control. Whenever what is
  // playing changes, the host hears ONE tuple:
  //
  //     window.__DC_ON_TRANSPORT = ({ playing, rec, track, queueIndex, shuffle,
  //                                    hoverAudio, hoverRec }) => { … }
  //
  //   ⚠ SEVEN FIELDS, NOT FIVE (corrected 2026-09-09, code audit part 1). The
  //   last two are V2's, and they are the two hover mode depends on:
  //     hoverAudio — is hover audio armed at all
  //     hoverRec   — the record under the cursor, or null
  //   The tuple publishes in BOTH modes. Hover mode removes the transport BAR,
  //   never the channel — a host reading only the first five cannot sound a
  //   preview, which is the whole of hover mode.
  //
  //   · playing    — true while sound should be coming out. false when paused
  //                  or before the first press. (started && !paused)
  //   · rec        — PAIRS index of the record whose audio is wanted, or null.
  //                  The same derivation the transport bar shows: a hovered
  //                  star wins (hover audio), then the record the visitor
  //                  pressed play on, then the locked record.
  //   · track      — 0-based track index within that record, or null when the
  //                  design has not chosen one (the host plays track 0).
  //   · queueIndex — position in the queue when the queue panel is open and a
  //                  row is current, else null.
  //   · shuffle    — the queue's shuffle flag.
  //
  // Fires after commit, only when the tuple changes; never on first paint.
  // Same shape and rules as __DC_ON_NAV. The host drives the real player from
  // it the way the field follows __DC_ON_STAR_STATE; a slow or absent host
  // cannot stall the design.
  // ── ONE PLAY METHOD (§62) ─────────────────────────────────────────────────
  // Every ▶ / ❙❙ in the file calls this. It is the only writer of started /
  // paused / npPlay, so _playTuple() always sees the truth and the transport
  // bar, the album panel, the hero, the catalogue card and the queue can never
  // disagree about what is playing.
  //   play(rec)          — start rec (PAIRS index), or resume it if it is the
  //                        current record and paused; pressing ▶ on the record
  //                        that is already playing pauses it.
  //   play()             — no record: resume/start whatever is current
  //                        (lock, hover, or the last played).
  //   togglePlay()       — the transport bar: pause if playing, else play().
  // ── WHAT THIS PAGE'S PLAY BUTTON IS ABOUT (2026-09-04) ───────────────────
  // The album page's control read its state from st (recPlayLabel) and acted
  // through startPlayback; ATH's acted through the same startPlayback but wore
  // a LITERAL label — '▶︎ Play the track'. So ATH played and paused correctly
  // and said "Play" throughout: the behaviour was right and invisible, which
  // is worse than a broken button because the visitor presses it again.
  //
  // The cause is that the subject was resolved in ONE place (startPlayback)
  // and the state was tested in ANOTHER (recPlayLabel's own npPlay compare).
  // Two resolutions of "which record is this page about" can always drift, so
  // there is one now, and both the action and the label read it.
  //   { rec, track } — track is undefined where the page is about a whole
  //   release rather than one track of it.
  pageSubject(route, st, athArtWho) {
    if (route === 'ath-artist') {
      const rec = athArtWho ? this.recFor(athArtWho) : null;
      return { rec: rec, track: undefined };
    }
    if (route === 'ath-release' || route === 'ath-track') {
      const d = this.athDrop(st).r, i = st.trackIdx == null ? 0 : st.trackIdx;
      const name = ([d.curator].concat(d.guests || []))[i] || d.curator;
      // dropRowRec, so an invited artist with no record of their own resolves
      // through the drop — the same resolver the drop's own rows use.
      return { rec: this.dropRowRec(d, name), track: i };
    }
    if (String(route).indexOf('ath-') === 0) {
      const d = this.athDrop(st).r;
      return { rec: d ? this.recFor(d.curator) : null, track: undefined };
    }
    // The album page and the star drawer: the page is about the locked record,
    // and the whole release rather than one track of it. recIdx first, then
    // lock, then whatever the transport holds — the same order play() uses to
    // decide what a bare press means.
    const rec = st.recIdx != null ? st.recIdx : (st.lock != null ? st.lock : (st.npPlay != null ? st.npPlay : null));
    return { rec: rec, track: undefined };
  }
  // Is that subject the thing sounding right now? The album page's own test,
  // lifted verbatim so the two pages cannot answer it differently.
  // ⚠ AND THE SUBJECT IS OFTEN NULL, WHICH IS THE OTHER HALF OF THE BUG.
  // An ATH drop's artist frequently has no record under their own name, so the
  // subject resolves to null, startPlayback falls through to play() with no
  // argument, and the transport RESUMES WHATEVER IT HELD. That is why music
  // started but the label never moved: the control was playing one thing and
  // describing another. A control that cannot name its subject must at least
  // describe what it actually did, so a null subject is read against the
  // transport's own record — the thing the press really acted on.
  subjectPlaying(subject, st) {
    if (!st.started || st.paused) return false;
    // Started and not paused IS the transport running — npPlay may be null
    // when the record could not be named, and the audio is the host's either
    // way. Requiring npPlay here is what kept the label frozen on a drop whose
    // artist has no record of their own.
    if (!subject || subject.rec == null) return true;
    const sameTrack = subject.track == null || subject.track === (st.trackIdx == null ? 0 : st.trackIdx);
    return st.npPlay === subject.rec && sameTrack;
  }
  // ── A PRESS WRITES TO THE QUEUE (2026-09-09, Tom) ────────────────────────
  // "When you hit play on something, it's in the queue. I should be able to look
  // at the queue and see the one playing… it holds the history of what's playing
  // and what's played."
  //
  // So the queue here is not a to-play list, it is a RECORD OF LISTENING: what
  // is sounding now sits in it, and everything played before it stays. Pressing
  // play with an empty queue leaves exactly one row.
  //
  // The design keeps its own list because QUEUE is a HOST table and this file
  // cannot write to one. qPlayed is appended here, merged with whatever the host
  // published (see qList), and deduped on title + artist so a repeat press moves
  // nothing — the row is already there and the playing mark simply lands on it.
  queuePlayed(rec, track) {
    if (rec == null) return;
    const p = (this.data('PAIRS') || [])[rec] || [];
    const rows = this.recTrackRows(p) || [];
    const t = rows[track == null ? 0 : track] || [];
    const title = t[0] || p[1] || '';
    const who = t[3] || p[0] || '';
    if (!title) return;
    // "6:12" → 372. No duration → null, never 0: a zero here would print 0:00
    // in the row, which is the broken clock rule 2 names.
    const secs = (() => {
      const m = String(t[1] || '').match(/^(\d+):(\d{2})$/);
      return m ? (+m[1] * 60 + +m[2]) : null;
    })();
    const prev = this.state.qPlayed || [];
    const already = prev.some(r => r[0] === title && r[1] === who);
    if (already) return;
    this.setState({ qPlayed: prev.concat([[title, who, secs, true, rec, (track == null ? 0 : track)]]) });
  }

  // ── A CUED RECORD, SO THE BAR AND THE QUEUE ARE NEVER EMPTY (2026-09-10,
  // Tom: "if somebody switches between hover and player… it should just load
  // up the song. It doesn't mean play it… It should never be an empty queue.
  // It should just grab a song from the page it's on. Or… the newest three
  // release singles") ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────
  // CUED IS NOT PLAYING and nothing here starts audio: this returns ROWS, the
  // same [title, who, secs, live, rec, track] shape queuePlayed appends, and
  // the callers only ever DISPLAY them. st.started stays false, so the control
  // still reads ▶︎ and the scrubber still reads nothing.
  //
  // It is also not a stand-in (rule 2). Every row is a real catalogue record
  // resolved through PAIRS and REC_TRACKS — the same readers the tracklist
  // uses — so the bar cannot name a track the record does not have. Where a
  // duration is missing it stays null, never 0.
  //
  // BOTH OF TOM'S ANSWERS, IN ONE ORDER, because they are the same answer at
  // two scopes: the page's own record first (what you are looking at), then
  // the label's hero set, which IS the newest three. On the landing those are
  // the same record, so the list is the three heroes with the one you are on
  // at its head.
  cueSet(st) {
    const s = st || this.state;
    const out = [], seen = {};
    const add = (rec) => {
      if (rec == null) return;
      const p = (this.data('PAIRS') || [])[rec];
      if (!p) return;
      const t = (this.recTrackRows(p) || [])[0] || [];
      const title = t[0] || p[1] || '';
      const who = t[3] || p[0] || '';
      const k = title + '\u0000' + who;
      if (!title || seen[k]) return;
      seen[k] = 1;
      const m = String(t[1] || '').match(/^(\d+):(\d{2})$/);
      // [6] = CUED. The queue is HISTORY on this site — a press writes a row —
      // so a seeded row must say it has not been played yet, or the panel
      // claims three listens that never happened. One extra element rather
      // than a parallel list: qRows already walks this shape and the flag
      // rides with the row through the merge.
      out.push([title, who, m ? (+m[1] * 60 + +m[2]) : null, true, rec, 0, true]);
    };
    add(s.recIdx != null ? s.recIdx : null);
    (this.heroSet(s.label) || []).forEach(hz => { if (hz && !hz.pending) add(hz.rec); });
    return out;
  }

  play(rec, track) {
    const st = this.state;
    // ══ HOVER MODE HAS NO PLAYER (2026-09-09, Tom: "a song is playing and I
    // can't stop it… I'm not hovering on anything") ═══════════════════════════
    // The two modes are exclusive, and the exclusion was only ever drawn: every
    // play CONTROL is hidden by playCtl in hover mode, but play() itself still
    // accepted a call — from a tracklist row's click, a cover, a queue action —
    // and set started/npPlay. That is a sound with no control anywhere on the
    // page to stop it, because hover mode removes the transport too.
    //
    // Guarding HERE closes the class the same way the null-record guard below
    // does: the mode is the one authority, and no caller has to remember it.
    // The sound in hover mode is the preview under the cursor, published as
    // hoverRec/hoverTrack and stopped by moving away — which is the whole idea.
    if (this.hoverArmed(st)) return;
    // Into the queue on the way through — one call site, because play() is the
    // funnel every control already goes through (see the guard below).
    if (rec != null) this.queuePlayed(rec, track);
    if (rec != null) this.xpMark('queue.first');
    const cur = st.npPlay != null ? st.npPlay : (st.lock != null ? st.lock : null);
    if (rec == null) rec = cur;
    // ══ NO RECORD, NO PLAYBACK · THE FUNNEL REFUSES (2026-09-09) ═════════════
    // ⚠ THIS IS THE ROOT OF EVERY WRONG-ATTRIBUTION BUG FOUND TODAY, and it was
    // patched caller-by-caller seven times before being fixed here. play() took
    // a null record and still set started: true with npPlay left null; npRec
    // then resolved `npPlay ?? lock ?? 0` and named RECORD 0. So a press with no
    // identity produced a transport confidently reading "Passing Fires · Jeremy
    // Cunningham", a ⋮ offering "Open the record" on an unrelated album, and — on
    // ath-map, where no bar paints — a wrong state that only surfaced on leaving
    // the map.
    //
    // Guarding HERE closes the class rather than its instances: every remaining
    // `else this.play()` becomes a no-op automatically. That is athShownDrops
    // (6 rows), athDrops (2) and athLatestDrop (2) — three more callers I would
    // otherwise have found one at a time.
    //
    // A BARE RESUME STILL WORKS: rec has already fallen back to `cur`
    // (npPlay ?? lock), so a paused record resumes as before — cur is non-null
    // exactly when something is holding the transport. This refuses only the
    // case where nothing has an identity, which is the case that was lying.
    if (rec == null) return;
    // A record the site marked hasAudio:false (PAIRS[i][5]) is never played.
    if (rec != null) { const p = this.data('PAIRS')[rec]; if (p && p[5] === false) return; }
    const sameTrack = track == null || track === (st.trackIdx == null ? 0 : st.trackIdx);
    const playingThis = st.started && !st.paused && st.npPlay === rec && rec != null && sameTrack;
    if (playingThis) { this.setState({ paused: true }); return; }
    const switching = rec != null && rec !== st.npPlay;
    // One setState: record, track and play state land together, so the row
    // that lights is the row that was pressed, on the same render.
    //
    // ── AND THE CURATOR-MAP SEAT IS RELEASED HERE (2026-09-09, third pass) ──
    // mapSeat was written by the map's own two buttons and by nothing else, so
    // it outlived every other press: play(3) then play(1) from a cover brought
    // Clarice Jensen's circle back to ❙❙ for audio nobody started there. In the
    // collision pairs it was worse — press Grey Mcmurray, then press play on
    // Clarice's cover, and GREY's circle claimed the track. The npPlay guard in
    // mapSeatOn caught the first case and not the second, because npPlay was
    // right and the seat was stale.
    //
    // THIS IS THE FUNNEL: covers, track rows, the drawer and the transport all
    // arrive here, and the map's buttons are the only presses that should own a
    // seat. So any press through play() drops it, and the map's own handlers
    // re-claim it immediately AFTER calling play — the order matters, and
    // claiming first is what this replaces.
    // ⚠ AND IT RELEASES THE EXTERNAL EPISODE. There is ONE speaker and TWO
    // funnels — this one for records, playExt for broadcast episodes — and each
    // kept its own ownership marker without clearing the other's. So
    // started/paused/npPlay/mapSeat/extPlay could all be simultaneously
    // consistent with two different things sounding, and every reader needed its
    // own guard against the other funnel. Three rounds of stale-attribution bugs
    // came out of that, each fixed at a reader.
    //
    // Fixed at the CAUSE instead: the two funnels are now mutually exclusive at
    // the source. Starting a record drops extPlay; starting an episode drops
    // npPlay and mapSeat. Nothing downstream has to know the other exists —
    // which is why mapSeatOn does not gain an !extPlay clause and the broadcast
    // row does not gain an !npPlay one.
    this.setState({ started: true, paused: false, mapSeat: null, extPlay: null, npPlay: rec == null ? st.npPlay : rec, trackIdx: track != null ? track : (switching ? 0 : st.trackIdx) });
  }
  // ── ACTIONS THE SITE PERFORMS · window.__DC_ON_ACTION (§65) ──────────────
  // The file has no cart store, no queue store, no playlists, no outbound
  // links. Every control that MEANS one of those emits an intent and the site
  // acts on it, then re-injects the affected table (CART, QUEUE) so the file
  // shows the result. Fires synchronously on the click, once, with:
  //
  //   { action, rec, track, format, source }
  //   action — 'cart.add' | 'cart.remove' | 'cart.changeFormat' | 'cart.checkout'
  //          | 'queue.add' | 'queue.playNext' | 'queue.remove' | 'queue.clear'
  //          | 'queue.addArtist' | 'queue.played' | 'playlist.add'
  //          | 'constellation.add'
  //          | 'open.bandcamp' | 'open.spotify' | 'open.apple' | 'open.youtube'
  //          | 'open.instagram' | 'open.site' | 'share.copy' | 'notify.restock'
  //          | 'broadcast.play' | 'broadcast.open' | 'form.submit'
  //   ⚠ THE LIST ABOVE IS THE WHOLE VOCABULARY, and it was four names short
  //   until 2026-09-09 (code audit part 7): broadcast.play, broadcast.open,
  //   queue.played and form.submit were all being emitted against a comment that
  //   read as a closed enumeration. Three DETAIL keys were missing with them —
  //   `kind` and `row` (which broadcast row), and `ext` (the external audio id).
  //   A host switching on this list dropped them silently.
  //   rec    — PAIRS index, or null when the action is not about one record
  //   track  — 0-based track index for track-level actions, else null
  //   format — the BUY row index for cart actions ('LP', 'CS' … by position), else null
  //   source — which control: 'catalogue' 'album' 'record' 'hero' 'transport'
  //            'queueRow' 'cartRow' 'artistTile' 'track' 'buySheet'
  //
  // The design keeps doing its own part (closing the menu, opening the cart or
  // queue panel so the visitor sees where the thing went). The site does the
  // rest. A missing handler is harmless: the panel still opens, nothing is added.
  // The two-second "copied" confirmation, one place. The host does the
  // clipboard write — it owns the URL — and this is the design's receipt.
  confirmCopy(where) {
    this.setState({ copied: where });
    clearTimeout(this._copyT);
    this._copyT = setTimeout(() => this.setState({ copied: null }), 2000);
  }
  emit(action, detail) {
    if (typeof window === 'undefined' || typeof window.__DC_ON_ACTION !== 'function') return;
    const d = Object.assign({ action: action, rec: null, track: null, format: null, source: null }, detail || {});
    try { window.__DC_ON_ACTION(d); } catch (e) { /* the host's problem, not the design's */ }
  }
  togglePlay() {
    const st = this.state;
    if (st.started && !st.paused) this.setState({ paused: true });
    else this.play();
  }
  // ── IS HOVER AUDIO ARMED? · THE ONE RESOLUTION (2026-09-07, Tom) ──────────
  // "I want to switch hover audio to default on all pages: artist pages and
  // release pages. Those are where it works. You can use it anywhere. It's
  // already on for ATH. Just put it on for the labels, so right away it's on.
  // People can see the visualization and turn it off."
  //
  // So the default is ON, everywhere, full stop. The old per-route split —
  // star field and ATH armed, grid and the flat pages quiet — came from the
  // 2026-09-01 reasoning that a grid has play buttons so nothing is lost by
  // starting silent. That was true when the switch was hard to find; it is not
  // the design any more. The Audio button is in the chrome of every route, the
  // meter shows the state without being asked, and turning it off is one hover
  // away. Arriving armed is now the better default because the visitor can SEE
  // that it is armed.
  //
  // THE VISITOR'S OWN CHOICE STILL WINS, unchanged: st.hoverAudio null means
  // untouched (so this default applies), true/false is theirs and sticks across
  // every route.
  //
  // ⚠ THIS IS THE ONLY READER OF THAT QUESTION, and that is the point of it
  // existing. There were THREE copies — _playTuple's hoverAudioNow0,
  // renderVals' hoverAudioNow, and the hoverAudioOn key — "kept in step by
  // name", which is the arrangement this file's own rule calls out as always
  // drifting. They HAD drifted: the third listed only ath-map and star, so the
  // AUDIO the switch reported disagreed with the audio actually armed on the
  // drops card and the volumes page. Same defect class as the play labels
  // (r16). One method, three callers, no way to disagree.
  // ── IS HOVER AUDIO ARMED? · THE ONE RESOLUTION (2026-09-07) ────────────────
  // THE VISITOR'S OWN CHOICE ALWAYS WINS: st.hoverAudio null means untouched, so
  // the default below applies; true/false is theirs and sticks across every
  // route.
  //
  // ⚠ THIS IS THE ONLY READER OF THAT QUESTION. There were THREE copies "kept in
  // step by name", which is the arrangement this file's own rule calls out as
  // always drifting — and they HAD drifted: one listed only ath-map and star
  // while the audio was armed on the drops card and the volumes page too, so the
  // switch could read Off over audible sound. Same defect class as the play
  // labels (r16). One method, three callers, no way to disagree.
  //
  // ⚠ SUPERSEDED 2026-09-09 — READ THE BODY, NOT THIS TABLE. Everything from
  // here to the routing table below is the 09-07 per-surface rule, kept as
  // lineage. hoverArmed now returns TRUE for every surface above 700 ("all the
  // pages, all the sites, default to grid view and hover audio"), and a phone
  // still resolves to player outright. The live rule is at HOVER IS THE DEFAULT
  // EVERYWHERE, twenty lines down.
  //
  // ⚠ REVERSED, DELIBERATELY (2026-09-07, Tom, same day): "I want to go back to
  // hover audio off for grid mode, except for across the horizon, which should
  // still be on."
  //
  // Earlier today this returned true unconditionally. That is narrowed rather
  // than undone: the visitor can still SEE the state on every route, which is
  // what made the wider default defensible — but whether a LABEL's flat pages
  // should make sound before being asked to is a different question, and the
  // answer is no.
  //
  //   ACROSS THE HORIZON → ON, on every one of its surfaces. The series is
  //     curated listening; moving across the faces and covers IS the way
  //     through it, and it has been armed there since r2f.
  //   ANY STAR FIELD → ON. The field is the instrument.
  //   EVERYTHING ELSE → OFF. A grid has play buttons; nothing is lost by
  //     starting quiet, and the Audio button is one hover away.
  //
  // ATH IS ANSWERED BY labelKeyOf, NOT BY THE ROUTE — rules 1 and 2. An ATH
  // visitor on `catalogue` or `artist` is still on ATH's site and still gets
  // armed audio; deriving this from an `ath-` route prefix would be the launch
  // blocker's exact shape, one surface further out.
  hoverArmed(st) {
    const s = st || this.state;
    // ── AND HOVER IS OFF ON A PHONE AGAIN (2026-09-14, Tom: "no map and
    // hover, just to make it simpler to display and more consistent") ───────
    //
    // ⚠ THIS REVERSES THE 09-10 REVERSAL, and I am stating that plainly rather
    // than quietly re-adding a deleted line. On 09-10 the argument was that a
    // phone has no POINTER but a finger held on a cover is the same gesture,
    // so touchHoverBridge() supplied the translation and the block came out.
    // That reasoning still holds on its own terms — the bridge works.
    //
    // What changed is not the mechanism, it is the SHAPE OF THE PHONE PRODUCT.
    // With the map gone, hover's best surface goes with it: the gesture's whole
    // point was exploring a sky with your finger. On a grid the same gesture
    // competes with tap-to-open and with scrolling, and it leaves the phone
    // with two audio modes where one is now clearly better. Tom is choosing
    // consistency over a feature, which is a product call and not a bug.
    //
    // touchHoverBridge() IS LEFT IN PLACE and is inert, because it self-gates
    // on hoverArmed. If the map ever returns to the phone, deleting this block
    // is the whole of what brings hover back with it.
    if (this._narrowNow()) return false;
    // ── V2 · A PHONE IS ALWAYS PLAYER MODE, AND IT WINS OUTRIGHT ────────────
    // ⚠⚠ THIS WHOLE HEADING AND ITS REASONING ARE SUPERSEDED (2026-09-10) AND
    // KEPT ONLY AS HISTORY — the block it justified is gone; see the REVERSED
    // note below the paragraphs. A phone is NOT forced into Player any more.
    // Read the reversal, not this. ⚠⚠
    //
    // ⚠ THIS FIXES A BUG I SHIPPED AND THEN DOCUMENTED AS A DECISION. The note
    // on audioWrapStyle read "the mode there is whatever the surface's default
    // is, and the play controls follow it" — which, on a device with no hover,
    // is the fault stated as if it were reasoning.
    //
    // What it produced: ATH defaults to hover, so EVERY phone visitor to ATH —
    // and every phone visitor who opened a star view — arrived in hover mode
    // where hover can never fire, with the play buttons removed by the mode, no
    // transport, and the switcher hidden below 700 (14c). Nothing on the page
    // could start audio by any means, and there was no way out. In the shipped
    // file this could not happen: hover audio on/off never removed the play
    // controls, so a phone always had them. V2's mode is what made the gap
    // reachable, so V2 is where it closes.
    //
    // BEFORE the explicit-choice check, deliberately. A visitor who chose hover
    // on a desktop and then opened the same site on a phone must not inherit a
    // mode that physically cannot work there — a choice that cannot fire is not
    // a choice worth honouring, and honouring it costs them all playback. The
    // desktop choice is untouched and returns the moment the viewport does.
    //
    // ⚠ REVERSED (2026-09-10, Tom: "we want hover to work on mobile. For album
    // covers, putting your finger on the album cover plays the track. It's
    // simple: it's a hover. For stars, you can do a short press to listen
    // quickly and a longer press to lock it").
    //
    // The block above was sound reasoning from a false premise: that a phone
    // HAS no hover. It has no POINTER — but a finger held on a cover is the
    // same gesture with the same meaning, and the same beginning, middle and
    // end. What was missing was never the capability, only the translation.
    // touchHoverBridge() supplies it, so the reason to force Player has gone
    // and forcing it now costs phone visitors the better half of the site.
    //
    // The rest of this method is unchanged and still governs: an explicit
    // choice wins, and the per-surface default decides the rest.
    if (s.hoverAudio !== null && s.hoverAudio !== undefined) return s.hoverAudio !== false;
    // ── HOVER IS THE DEFAULT EVERYWHERE (2026-09-09, Tom) ───────────────────
    // "All the pages, all the sites, default to grid view and hover audio, and
    // then people can switch from there." The per-surface default is gone: it
    // meant the same visitor met two different sites depending on where they
    // landed, and the mode is a preference, not a property of a page. One
    // answer, one switch, and the switch is right there in the header.
    //
    // ⚠ The phone exception ABOVE stands and is not a leftover: a device with
    // no hover cannot start audio in hover mode, and this default would strand
    // every phone visitor exactly as it did before (see the note above it).
    // Narrow resolves to player; every other viewport arrives in hover.
    return true;
  }
  // ══ V2 · TWO WAYS TO LISTEN, AND THE SITE PICKS A SIDE (2026-09-07) ═══════
  // ⚠ V2 ONLY. The shipped file has no mode: it has hover audio ON or OFF, and
  // the play controls are always there. This is the idea being tried.
  //
  // Tom: "a play/queue mode vs mouseover mode site wide… in hover mode there
  // would be no transport, no play buttons anywhere, and in play mode the play
  // buttons would appear."
  //
  // THE MODE IS THE SAME STATE THE SWITCH ALREADY HELD. hoverArmed() above has
  // always answered "is hover audio on", and "on" already meant "you listen by
  // moving" while "off" meant "you listen by pressing". So mode is not a new
  // axis bolted beside it — it is that axis, named honestly and given its
  // consequences. One state, one reader, no way for the two to disagree:
  //
  //   HOVER MODE  hoverArmed() true   → no transport, no play buttons, no queue
  //   PLAY MODE   hoverArmed() false  → the player, the buttons and the queue
  //
  // ⚠ THE SECOND HALF OF THIS PARAGRAPH IS SUPERSEDED (2026-09-09). It read
  // "ATH and the star fields arrive in HOVER mode, the labels' flat pages arrive
  // in PLAY mode" — there is no per-surface default any more: every surface
  // arrives in HOVER, and a phone in PLAYER. The mode is a preference, not a
  // property of a page. What still holds is the sentence above it: mode is the
  // axis hoverArmed has always answered, named honestly.
  //
  // audioMode() is the name to read in new code; hoverArmed() stays because
  // forty call sites and three drops of comments use it, and renaming it would
  // be a diff nobody could review.
  audioMode(st) { return this.hoverArmed(st) ? 'hover' : 'play'; }
  // ══ WHAT SOUNDS IN HOVER MODE · THE CLOSED LIST (2026-09-09, Tom) ═════════
  // The instruction, so it is in the file and not only in a message:
  //
  //   COVERS         every album cover, everywhere one is drawn — the catalogue
  //                  grid, the drawer's release rows, the artist page's
  //                  discography, the ATH drops and volumes covers, the stars.
  //                  catHover = the record.
  //   TRACK ROWS     every tracklist row, on the album page and in the column.
  //                  catHover = the record AND hoverTrack = the row, so the
  //                  preview is THAT track's sample and not the record's first.
  //   CURATOR MAP    a face is a person on a drop and keeps its preview.
  //
  //   ⚠ NOT ARTIST PHOTOGRAPHS. The artists wall previewed that artist's newest
  //   record on hover and Tom removed it (same message, revising himself): "a
  //   photograph is not a record". The tile still lights and still carries its
  //   menu; it makes no sound.
  //
  // And the rule that makes the mode real, which was drawn but not enforced
  // until today: PLAY() REFUSES IN HOVER MODE. Every play control is hidden
  // there, but play() itself still accepted a call, so a row's click could start
  // a record with no control anywhere on the page able to stop it. Switching
  // INTO hover mode also stops whatever the player was doing, and the tuple's
  // playing/rec/track go null — the live fields are hoverAudio / hoverRec /
  // hoverTrack, and they clear the moment the cursor leaves.
  // ── ONE SIDE STYLE FOR BOTH MODE TOGGLES (2026-09-09) ─────────────────────
  // View and Audio are the same control twice, so their sides come from one
  // function rather than two copies of the same object. When they were buttons
  // with panels the duplication was survivable; side by side and permanently
  // open, a 1px difference between them would be the first thing you see.
  //
  // 24px inside a 28px seg (2px padding each side). The held side is a gold
  // TINT, never a slab — the 2026-09-01 ruling that governs every two-state
  // control in this chrome.
  // ⚠ snug ARRIVES AS AN ARGUMENT, IT IS NOT CLOSED OVER (2026-09-09). This is
  // a class method, so it cannot see renderVals()'s locals — an earlier pass read
  // `snug` here directly and threw ReferenceError on every render, blanking the
  // page. Passed in rather than re-derived: a second `W < 1300` inside this
  // method would be a second authority for one width decision, which is the
  // thing this file's one-authority rule exists to prevent.
  // ══ PORTED FROM V1 · 2026-09-09 ══════════════════════════════════════════
  // V2 was forked from V1 late on 09-07 and V1 kept working for a day and a
  // half. These are the pieces of that work with no V2 equivalent, moved over
  // whole rather than re-derived — the comments come with them, because their
  // reasoning is the record of why each exists.
  //
  // ⚠ DELIBERATELY NOT PORTED: the queue's explain-once cursor tooltip
  // (queueCoachOpen, _qc* , _stagePt, queueCoach* keys) and hoverAudioPaused.
  // Tom, 2026-09-09: "get rid of the tooltips altogether for now… there are no
  // play buttons in hover mode now, so you can't just hit a play button and it
  // switches. There's no need to tell people that."
  //
  // He is right and it is the mode that makes it right: the tooltip existed to
  // explain a collision — a press taking the floor from an armed hover — and in
  // V2 that collision cannot happen. Hover mode has no play button to press,
  // and player mode has no hover to interrupt. PLAYER MODE IS EXCLUSIVE, so the
  // two audio systems are never live at once and there is nothing to announce.
  //
  // hoverAudioPaused goes with it for the same reason: it published "hover is
  // armed but standing down", a state that no longer exists. The tuple's own
  // `hoverAudio` field already carries the answer — false in player mode, true
  // in hover mode — so the mode IS the flag. Engineering read hoverAudioPaused
  // as of r62 and should stop; it will not appear in this drop's tuple.
  // ── IS HOVER AUDIO ARMED? · THE ONE RESOLUTION (2026-09-07, Tom) ──────────
  // "I want to switch hover audio to default on all pages: artist pages and
  // release pages. Those are where it works. You can use it anywhere. It's
  // already on for ATH. Just put it on for the labels, so right away it's on.

  //

  //
  // ⚠ THIS IS THE ONLY READER OF THAT QUESTION, and that is the point of it
  // existing. There were THREE copies — _playTuple's hoverAudioNow0,
  // renderVals' hoverAudioNow, and the hoverAudioOn key — "kept in step by
  // name", which is the arrangement this file's own rule calls out as always
  // drifting. They HAD drifted: the third listed only ath-map and star, so the
  // AUDIO the switch reported disagreed with the audio actually armed on the
  // drops card and the volumes page. Same defect class as the play labels
  // (r16). One method, three callers, no way to disagree.
  // ── IS HOVER AUDIO ARMED? · THE ONE RESOLUTION (2026-09-07) ────────────────
  // THE VISITOR'S OWN CHOICE ALWAYS WINS: st.hoverAudio null means untouched, so
  // the default below applies; true/false is theirs and sticks across every
  // route.
  //
  // ⚠ THIS IS THE ONLY READER OF THAT QUESTION. There were THREE copies "kept in
  // step by name", which is the arrangement this file's own rule calls out as
  // always drifting — and they HAD drifted: one listed only ath-map and star
  // while the audio was armed on the drops card and the volumes page too, so the
  // switch could read Off over audible sound. Same defect class as the play
  // labels (r16). One method, three callers, no way to disagree.
  //
  // ⚠ SUPERSEDED 2026-09-09 — READ THE BODY, NOT THIS TABLE. Everything from
  // here to the routing table below is the 09-07 per-surface rule, kept as
  // lineage. hoverArmed now returns TRUE for every surface above 700 ("all the
  // pages, all the sites, default to grid view and hover audio"), and a phone
  // still resolves to player outright. The live rule is at HOVER IS THE DEFAULT
  // EVERYWHERE, twenty lines down.
  //
  // ⚠ REVERSED, DELIBERATELY (2026-09-07, Tom, same day): "I want to go back to
  // hover audio off for grid mode, except for across the horizon, which should
  // still be on."
  //
  // Earlier today this returned true unconditionally. That is narrowed rather
  // than undone: the visitor can still SEE the state on every route, which is
  // what made the wider default defensible — but whether a LABEL's flat pages
  // should make sound before being asked to is a different question, and the
  // answer is no.
  //
  //   ACROSS THE HORIZON → ON, on every one of its surfaces. The series is
  //     curated listening; moving across the faces and covers IS the way
  //     through it, and it has been armed there since r2f.
  //   ANY STAR FIELD → ON. The field is the instrument.
  //   EVERYTHING ELSE → OFF. A grid has play buttons; nothing is lost by
  //     starting quiet, and the Audio button is one hover away.
  //
  // ATH IS ANSWERED BY labelKeyOf, NOT BY THE ROUTE — rules 1 and 2. An ATH
  // visitor on `catalogue` or `artist` is still on ATH's site and still gets
  // armed audio; deriving this from an `ath-` route prefix would be the launch
  // blocker's exact shape, one surface further out.
  // ── THE PHOTO'S OWN RATIO, MEASURED ONCE (2026-09-07) ─────────────────────
  // Tom, on the journal article: "I just don't understand what this big space
  // is under the photo." It is the unused bottom of the box: a declared 1/1
  // against a 1.43 photograph leaves 138px of real layout height, and no
  // background-image div can know better — contain honours the photo and the
  // rest is still box.
  //
  // The two ways out were both closed. A declared ratio is a guess unless the
  // data states it (x[7], absent on all four articles). An <img> reads the
  // intrinsic ratio, but its src cannot carry a {{ hole }} — the browser fetches
  // the literal braces during the stream, which is the 404 I shipped an hour
  // ago and which this file's own note at the news media slot already forbade.
  //
  // So: MEASURE IT. One Image() per distinct src, resolved once, cached by src
  // in this._arCache and mirrored into state so the render picks it up. The box
  // then matches the photograph exactly and there is no slack in any direction
  // — no gap under the photo, no letterbox, nothing to position around.
  //
  // Until the measurement lands the box falls back to 1/1, which is one frame
  // at most and never a visible reflow on a cached image. x[7] still wins when
  // the data supplies it: stated beats measured, and measured beats guessed.
  imgAR(src) {
    if (!src) return null;
    if (!this._arCache) this._arCache = {};
    if (this._arCache[src] !== undefined) return this._arCache[src];
    if (typeof window === 'undefined' || typeof Image === 'undefined') return null;
    this._arCache[src] = null;
    const im = new Image();
    im.onload = () => {
      if (!im.naturalWidth || !im.naturalHeight) return;
      this._arCache[src] = im.naturalWidth / im.naturalHeight;
      // A counter rather than the value: the cache is the store, this only says
      // "something was measured, render again".
      this.setState({ arTick: (this.state.arTick || 0) + 1 });
    };
    im.onerror = () => { this._arCache[src] = null; };
    im.src = src;
    return null;
  }
  // ══ §0-2 · THE INBOUND PLAYBACK CHANNEL (2026-09-08, engineering) ═════════
  // They caught a real hole in my own handoff note. I wrote "send started:false
  // through the transport path" — and THERE IS NO SUCH PATH. __DC_ON_TRANSPORT
  // is outbound only (design → host) and __DC_GO accepts navigation fields only;
  // `started` is not among them. So the only thing actually available to them was
  // window.__DC_ATLAS.setState(...), which is exactly what r49 told them to stop
  // doing when it added conAnchor to the tuple to get them off state-peeking.
  //
  // Asking them to reach into the instance would undo that, and worse: setState
  // is a WRITE into internals, where reading state was only a read. So the
  // answer is no, and here is the channel instead.
  //
  //   window.__DC_PLAYBACK({ queueExhausted: true })   the queue ran out
  //   window.__DC_PLAYBACK({ playing: false })         same thing, plainly said
  //   window.__DC_PLAYBACK({ playing: true })          host-initiated start
  //
  // Also reachable as a 'dc:playback' CustomEvent and as element.__dcPlayback,
  // the same three entry points __DC_GO has, so whichever one their host already
  // uses is the one that works.
  //
  // ⚠ queueExhausted CLEARS extPlay TOO. With the queue done there is nothing
  // sounding, so a lingering episode name would have the bar describing audio
  // that stopped — and a later bare play() would resume a name with no source.
  //
  // AND IT MUST NOT ECHO. Setting playing false makes _playTuple differ, which
  // would notify them of the change they just asked for. _inboundPlay suppresses
  // exactly one outbound update, and it is READ AND CLEARED AT THE TOP of
  // _notifyTransport on every path — the r49 lesson, where a latch cleared on
  // only some returns armed permanently and swallowed the next genuine update.
  playbackSet(p) {
    if (!p || typeof p !== 'object') return false;
    const done = p.queueExhausted === true || p.playing === false;
    const start = p.playing === true;
    if (!done && !start) return false;
    this._inboundPlay = true;
    if (done) this.setState({ started: false, paused: false, extPlay: null });
    else this.setState({ started: true, paused: false });
    return true;
  }
  // ── VOLS → ATH_DROPS, ONE JOIN (2026-09-04, ledger 15 + 18) ──────────────
  // VOLS carries the drop's SHAPE — curator, guests, one track title. ATH_DROPS
  // carries the fuller record: every track's own credit, title, label and
  // cover. The volumes list and the landing's Latest Drop were built from VOLS
  // alone, so two of every three tracks read "Title pending" and every cover
  // tile drew the hatch — while the rail's own cards, one function away, read
  // ATH_DROPS correctly. The join is by title: 'Vol. N Drop N' matches
  // ATH_DROPS[].title.
  //   tracks[ci] = [credit, title, label, portrait, cover]
  // ── "RELEASED <date>", AND THE ONE CASE IT MUST NOT SAY (2026-09-08, Tom) ──
  // The date alone read as a bare stamp; Tom asked for the word in front of it.
  // One reader for both surfaces, so the card and the release/track page cannot
  // label the same drop differently.
  //
  // ⚠ IT DOES NOT SAY "RELEASED" ON A PRE-ORDER. Engineering append their own
  // suffix, so meta arrives as '3 Dec 2026 · Pre-order' — and prefixing
  // 'Released' onto that would state the opposite of what the rest of the line
  // says, about a record that is not out. When the string already declares its
  // own status the string wins and is printed verbatim; only a plain date takes
  // the label. Same rule as printing meta verbatim in the first place: their
  // field describes itself, so the file must not talk over it.
  //
  // NO DATE MEANS NO LABEL. A bare 'Released' with nothing after it is exactly
  // the '—:—' defect — a field holding its space and saying nothing — so an
  // absent date drops the word with it.
  relDateLabel(relDate) {
    const s = String(relDate || '').trim();
    if (!s) return '';
    return /pre-?order|coming|announced/i.test(s) ? s : ('Released: ' + s);
  }
  // ══ WHAT IS PLAYING THAT THE CATALOGUE DOES NOT CONTAIN (2026-09-08, Tom) ══
  // "This is a track play, not a mouseover, so when that is clicked, the
  // transport needs to come up… anytime I hit a play button, it should change to
  // pause."
  //
  // THE TRANSPORT'S NOW-PLAYING WAS PAIRS-ONLY. npPair is PAIRS[npPlay], so any
  // surface whose audio is a catalogue record could raise the bar and name
  // itself — and a broadcast episode, which has no PAIRS row at all because it
  // is a row of the BROADCAST feed, had nothing it could be. THAT is why the two
  // broadcast controls only emitted to the host: there was no design state for
  // them to set. A press announced itself outward and changed nothing visible.
  //
  // extPlay is the missing thing: { id, artist, title } for audio the catalogue
  // does not carry. One key, one predicate, and the transport reads it FIRST —
  // it is the most recent press, so it outranks whatever record played before it.
  //
  // extOn(id) means "this is the current one AND it is sounding", so a paused
  // episode reads ▶︎ again and pressing it resumes — the same contract
  // dropRowOn gives a drop row. Every non-catalogue play control gates on this
  // one reader, so two of them can never disagree about what is playing.
  extOn(id) {
    const st = this.state;
    if (!id || !st.extPlay || st.extPlay.id !== id) return false;
    return !!st.started && !st.paused;
  }
  // The press itself, so the three broadcast controls share one path rather than
  // three copies of the same four lines. Toggle to paused when it is already
  // sounding; otherwise name it and start — this.play() with no record sets
  // started/paused and leaves extPlay standing, which is what raises the
  // transport and runs the meter.
  // `under` is the transport's second line — the SHOW for a broadcast episode,
  // an artist only when the caller happens to be a record. Named for the slot it
  // fills, not for one kind of thing that can fill it.
  // ⚠ IT NO LONGER DELEGATES TO play() (2026-09-08, found under the
  // playback-channel driving, and it was two bugs in one line).
  //
  // playExt used to set extPlay and then call this.play() with no arguments to
  // get started/paused set. But the NO-ARGUMENT form of play() does not mean
  // "start this" — it means "toggle or resume whatever is current". So with a
  // record already playing, rec fell back to that record, playingThis came out
  // TRUE, and pressing an episode PAUSED THE RECORD and returned early:
  //
  //   · the episode never started
  //   · the early return skipped `this._extPress = false`, so the flag stayed
  //     set and SWALLOWED THE NEXT genuine press's queue.played row
  //
  // Driven: record 0 playing → press an episode → paused, flag stuck → press
  // record 2 → no history row. Two presses, one row, and the wrong audio.
  //
  // Borrowing a method whose meaning depends on its arguments is the mistake.
  // An episode press is unambiguous — start this, now — so it writes that
  // directly. _extPress is gone with the delegation: there is no inner emit left
  // to suppress, so there is no flag to leak.
  playExt(id, under, title) {
    // Pressing the sounding episode again pauses it, same contract as a record.
    if (this.extOn(id)) { this.setState({ paused: true }); return; }
    // The mirror of play()'s handover — see its note. An episode takes the
    // speaker, so the record and the curator-map seat that were holding it let
    // go: without this a circle kept its ❙❙ while a podcast played, and the
    // reverse left the episode row lit while a record played.
    this.setState({ extPlay: { id: id, artist: under || '', title: title || '' }, started: true, paused: false, npPlay: null, mapSeat: null, trackIdx: null });
    // The episode's ONE history row, carrying the identity that makes it
    // readable. 'ext' also says WHY rec is null rather than leaving you to guess.
    this.emit('queue.played', { rec: null, track: null, source: 'ext', ext: { id: id, under: under || '', title: title || '' } });
    // ⚠ THE LESSON CALL IS GONE WITH THE TOOLTIP (2026-09-09). V1 ended this
    // with queueCoachOpen('press') — the explain-once notice that a press had
    // taken the floor from hover audio. V2 has no such collision to explain: see
    // the port banner above. Removed rather than stubbed, so nothing reads as a
    // lesson that was meant to fire and did not.
  }
  // ── REAL TEXT WIDTHS, CACHED (2026-09-09) ─────────────────────────────────
  // ⚠ THE PER-CHARACTER ESTIMATE WAS UNSAFE and it under-reported every string:
  // "Egghunt" by 14%, and the real rate ranges 11.9–13.4 px/char depending on
  // which glyphs a name happens to contain. No single constant can be right for
  // both "NNA TAPES" and "Whited Sepulchre", so there is no constant to tune —
  // the model was wrong, not its number.
  //
  // canvas measureText instead: the actual advance widths, from the actual font,
  // synchronously and without touching the DOM — so it can be read during
  // renderVals with no measure-then-render pass and none of the loop risk that
  // rules fitPanels out for this job.
  //
  // CACHED because the set of strings is fixed and tiny — five label names and
  // four short forms — so this measures nine strings once and never again.
  //
  // ⚠ LETTER-SPACING IS ADDED BY HAND. measureText does not apply it, and the
  // wordmark carries 0.02em — 0.36px per character at 18px. Left out, every
  // name reads a little narrow, which is the same class of error as the estimate
  // this replaces.
  _textW(str, font, tracking) {
    if (!str) return 0;
    this._twCache = this._twCache || {};
    const key = font + '|' + str;
    if (this._twCache[key] != null) return this._twCache[key];
    if (typeof document === 'undefined') return str.length * 13.5;
    const c = this._twCanvas || (this._twCanvas = document.createElement('canvas'));
    const ctx = c.getContext('2d');
    if (!ctx) return str.length * 13.5;
    ctx.font = font;
    const w = ctx.measureText(str).width + (tracking || 0) * str.length;
    this._twCache[key] = w;
    return w;
  }
  // ⚠ ink ARRIVES AS AN ARGUMENT TOO (2026-09-09, Tom: on the landing "the new
  // view and audio switchers need to be in light mode, like the rest of the
  // buttons"). The landing header sits ON A PHOTOGRAPH and every other item in
  // it — nav, Explore, cart, Browse — already reads the light ladder (cInk /
  // cInk70 / cHair). These two toggles were the only controls still taking the
