  // THEME's ink, so their unheld sides went dark against the picture. Passed in
  // for the same reason snug is: a second `route === 'landing'` inside this
  // method would be a second authority for one decision.
  modeSide(on, snug, lad) {
    const L = lad || {};
    return {
      display: 'flex', alignItems: 'center', justifyContent: 'center',
      height: snug ? '22px' : '26px', padding: snug ? '0 10px' : '0 12px', boxSizing: 'border-box',
      borderRadius: '4px', cursor: 'pointer', whiteSpace: 'nowrap',
      fontFamily: "'JetBrains Mono', monospace", fontSize: '9px',
      letterSpacing: '0.12em', textTransform: 'uppercase',
      background: on ? (L.tint || 'var(--goldtint)') : 'transparent',
      // The UNSELECTED side was ink55 — 3.93:1, the same fault one step milder.
      // These two words are how a visitor reads which mode they are NOT in, so
      // they are content, not decoration. Raised here rather than by changing
      // --ink55, which is used across the whole file and is a far broader
      // decision than this control; the held side stays gold and 600, so the
      // pair still reads as one chosen and one not.
      // ⚠ THE HELD SIDE TAKES THE LADDER'S GOLD TOO. On the landing the theme is
      // light, so --gold is #8a6a12 — a brown that all but disappears against a
      // photograph. The header's own gold there is the bright one, the same the
      // dark theme uses; everywhere else this is --gold exactly as before.
      color: on ? (L.gold || 'var(--gold)') : (L.ink || 'var(--ink70)'),
      fontWeight: on ? 600 : 500
    };
  }
  playMode(st) { return !this.hoverArmed(st); }
  // ── ONE GATE FOR EVERY PLAY CONTROL ───────────────────────────────
  // Wrap a play control's style in this and it disappears in hover mode. Every
  // ▶︎, ❙❙, Play button, queue control and the transport itself goes through it,
  // so "no play buttons anywhere" is one predicate rather than twenty — the same
  // reason hoverArmed() exists. A control that appears in one mode and not the
  // other must never be decided locally.
  playCtl(style) { return this.playMode(this.state) ? style : { display: 'none' }; }
  _playTuple(st) {
    // ⚠ IN HOVER MODE NOTHING IS "PLAYING" (2026-09-09). The mode has no player,
    // so the tuple must not carry one: playing/rec/track are the PLAYER's fields
    // and a host reading playing:true with a rec while the visitor is hovering
    // will keep sounding a record nothing on the page can stop. The live fields
    // there are hoverAudio / hoverRec / hoverTrack, and they clear on leave.
    const isStar0 = st.route === 'star' || (typeof st.route === 'string' && st.route.indexOf('ath-map') === 0);
    const vp = this.VPS[st.vp] || this.PROBE_VPS[(st.vp || 0) - this.VPS.length] || [0, 760];
    const stars = this['f' + vp[1]] || null;
    const hoverAudioNow0 = this.hoverArmed(st);
    // ── PAST THE 130-DOT CACHE (2026-09-04, interim 3) ──────────────────────
    // `stars` is the PAINTED cache, so a star id outside it found nothing and
    // this branch resolved null: records past the cache would neither lock nor
    // sound, while the hoverRow sibling below — which has a fallback — worked.
    // The id encodes its record ('s' + rec), so it is always recoverable
    // without the cache. Same shape as renderVals' hoverPair.
    const recOfId = (id) => { const n = parseInt(String(id).slice(1), 10); return isNaN(n) ? null : n; };
    // ⚠⚠ THE FALLBACK WAS INSIDE THE GUARD IT EXISTS TO BYPASS (2026-09-09).
    // This read `st.hoverStar != null && stars`, so with no cache the whole
    // branch was skipped and hoverRec came out null — including the recOfId
    // fallback added in r45 for exactly that case, whose own note says "the id
    // encodes its record, so it is always recoverable without the cache". The
    // comment described the intent; the guard defeated it.
    //
    // AND THE CACHE IS NEVER THERE. field(h) keys on the FIELD's height —
    // this['f' + 592] at 1180 — while this line looks up this['f' + vp[1]], the
    // VIEWPORT height, 760. The two have never matched, so `stars` was null in
    // normal operation and every star hover published hoverRec: null. Measured:
    // hoverStar 's5' set, cache keys present ['f592'], key looked up 'f760',
    // hoverRec null.
    //
    // That is engineering's §1 fear already true, and not because of the mode:
    // "hoverRec is the ONLY thing that tells us to sound a sample". Covers
    // (catHover) and rows (hoverRow) were unaffected, which is why hover audio
    // looked like it worked.
    //
    // ⚠ PRESENT IN V1 TOO, same line. Reported rather than silently fixed here.
    //
    // The id is now resolved from the id, with the cache as an optimisation
    // rather than a precondition — which is what r45 meant.
    const hov = (st.hoverStar != null)
      ? ((stars && stars.find(x => x.id === st.hoverStar)) || (recOfId(st.hoverStar) == null ? null : { rec: recOfId(st.hoverStar) }))
      : (st.hoverRow != null ? { rec: st.hoverRow }
      : (st.catHover != null && hoverAudioNow0 && st.route !== 'star') ? { rec: st.catHover } : null);
    const rec = hov ? hov.rec : (st.npPlay != null ? st.npPlay : (st.lock != null ? st.lock : null));
    // HOVER AUDIO, RESOLVED (§64). The site has its own resolver
    // (lib/atlas/hoverAudioView.ts) transcribed from this file's rule; both
    // agree, but transcription is the failure mode this project exists to end.
    // So the file publishes the ANSWER, not the inputs: hoverAudio is what the
    // ☰ switch shows right now — ruling 7's per-view default until the visitor
    // touches it, their choice after. hoverRec is the record under the cursor
    // that should be previewing, or null. playing ignores hover; hoverRec is
    // the hover layer on top of it.
    const isStar = isStar0;
    const hoverAudio = hoverAudioNow0;
    return {
      // The player's three fields, silent in hover mode (see the note at the
      // top of this method).
      playing: !hoverAudio && !!st.started && !st.paused,
      rec: hoverAudio ? null : (rec == null ? null : rec),
      track: hoverAudio ? null : (st.trackIdx == null ? null : st.trackIdx),
      queueIndex: (st.queue && st.qplaying != null) ? st.qplaying : null,
      shuffle: !!st.qshuffle,
      hoverAudio: hoverAudio,
      hoverRec: (hoverAudio && hov) ? hov.rec : (hoverAudio && st.catHover != null ? st.catHover : null),
      // ── hoverTrack · WHICH TRACK THE CURSOR IS ON (2026-09-09) ─────────────
      // hoverRec names a RECORD, so a tracklist could only ever preview track 1.
      // This is the row index within hoverRec, or null when the hover is on a
      // cover, a star or a face — which all mean "the record", and the host
      // should preview whatever it previews for a record.
      hoverTrack: (hoverAudio && st.catHover != null && st.hoverTrack != null) ? st.hoverTrack : null,
      // ── hoverSeat · THE CURATOR FACE UNDER THE CURSOR (2026-09-10) ─────────
      // ⚠ ENGINEERING WERE RIGHT AND THE DIAGNOSIS IS EXACT. The curator map's
      // face hover writes st.mnhover, which was in NEITHER this tuple nor the
      // change memo in _notifyTransport — so arriving on a face from empty
      // space notified the host of nothing at all, and a face whose record
      // cannot be resolved stayed silent from a cold approach. It DID sound
      // when you slid straight from a working face onto it, because hoverRec
      // changed on that path and the memo fired for its own reasons — which is
      // exactly why the fault read as intermittent rather than as a missing
      // field.
      //
      // THE SEAT, NOT THE RECORD. A face IS the subject on this surface, so the
      // host has to know the cursor moved between seats even when neither seat
      // resolves to a PAIRS row; hoverRec still says what to play when it can.
      // Gated on hoverAudio like its three siblings, so it is null in Player
      // mode rather than reporting a hover the mode has no use for.
      hoverSeat: (hoverAudio && st.mnhover != null) ? st.mnhover : null
    };
  }
  _notifyTransport() {
    if (typeof window === 'undefined') return;
    // ── THE ECHO, ACTUALLY SUPPRESSED (2026-09-09, code audit part 7) ────────
    // _inboundPlay was WRITTEN by playbackSet and never read or cleared — the
    // mechanism the comment above it describes as shipped did not exist, so a
    // host that set playback state got its own update handed straight back. Read
    // and cleared here at the top, on every path, which is the r49 lesson the
    // note already states.
    if (this._inboundPlay) { this._inboundPlay = false; this._lastPlay = this._playTuple(this.state); return; }
    const b = this._playTuple(this.state);
    const a = this._lastPlay;
    this._lastPlay = b;
    if (!a) return;
    if (a.playing === b.playing && a.rec === b.rec && a.track === b.track && a.queueIndex === b.queueIndex && a.shuffle === b.shuffle && a.hoverTrack === b.hoverTrack && a.hoverAudio === b.hoverAudio && a.hoverRec === b.hoverRec && a.hoverSeat === b.hoverSeat) return;
    if (typeof window.__DC_ON_TRANSPORT !== 'function') return;
    try { window.__DC_ON_TRANSPORT(b); } catch (e) { /* the host's problem, not the design's */ }
  }
  // ── §3 of r47 · WHICH ARTICLE, WHICH DROP, WHICH TRACK ─────────────────
  // You reproduced the tuple for an open journal piece and it named no article,
  // so every piece was indistinguishable to you and a Back landed on the wrong
  // one — undetectably. You were right that arrival() already names all three,
  // so these emit in EXACTLY the spelling arrival() accepts and round-trip
  // through goToNav() without translation on either side.
  //
  // The article's identity is its SLUG, not st.article, which is an index into
  // two different tables (JOURNAL_LIST carries the slug at [6], JOURNAL at [0]).
  // An index is the same mistake as the label index: correct until a list is
  // reordered. The slug is the stored string.
  articleSlug(st) {
    const i = Math.max(0, (st && st.article) || 0);
    const list = this.data('JOURNAL_LIST') || [];
    const x = list.length ? list[Math.min(list.length - 1, i)] : null;
    if (x && x[6]) return x[6];
    const j = (this['DEFAULT_JOURNAL'] || [])[i];
    return (j && j[0]) || null;
  }
  // 'v<vol>d<drop>t<idx>' — the form __DC_TRACK already takes. Volume and drop
  // are 1-based in the key and 0-based in state, which is arrival()'s own
  // convention; the conversion lives here so no caller has to know it.
  athTrackKey(st) {
    const d = this.athDrop(st).r;
    if (!d) return null;
    const i = (st && st.trackIdx == null) ? 0 : st.trackIdx;
    return 'v' + (d.vi + 1) + 'd' + (d.di + 1) + 't' + i;
  }
  _navTuple(st) {
    // §1 · THE CARRIED KEY, NOT THE ROUTE NAME. See labelKeyOf().
    const key = this.labelKeyOf(st);
    const ath = key === 'across-the-horizon';
    // The route-prefix fact the old `ath` used to mean, kept and reported
    // separately so the host does not lose it: `ath` now answers "whose site",
    // `athRoute` answers "is this one of ATH's own pages".
    const athRoute = typeof st.route === 'string' && st.route.indexOf('ath-') === 0;
    return {
      label: key,
      route: st.route, view: st.view,
      // ── THE SAME RESOLUTION THE PAGE USES (2026-09-05) ────────────────────
      // recIdx, then lock, and on the RECORD route then 0 — because that last
      // step is what the page itself does (`st.recIdx ?? st.lock ?? 0`), so
      // arriving on the route with neither set still shows record 0. Reporting
      // null there said "no record" about a page displaying one. Off the record
      // route, no lock genuinely means no record and stays null.
      record: st.recIdx != null ? st.recIdx : (st.lock != null ? st.lock : (st.route === 'record' ? 0 : null)),
      // ── THE ARTIST ON EVERY SURFACE THAT HAS ONE (2026-09-05, item 10) ────
      // This reported the artist on the artist route ALONE, so every ath-*
      // route said artist: null and the host had to read the person from the
      // state bridge instead. Each of these surfaces knows exactly whose page
      // it is; the tuple just was not asked.
      artist: (() => {
        if (st.route === 'artist') return this.curArtist() || null;
        // ⚠ WAS `st.athArtist`, a state key that does not exist — the ATH
        // artist is resolved, not stored, so the branch always yielded null
        // while the page showed the name (2026-09-05, second pass).
        if (st.route === 'ath-artist') return this.athArtistOnPage() || null;
        if (st.route === 'ath-track' || st.route === 'ath-release') {
          const d = this.athDrop(st).r;
          if (!d) return null;
          const i = st.trackIdx == null ? 0 : st.trackIdx;
          return ([d.curator].concat(d.guests || []))[i] || d.curator || null;
        }
        if (st.route === 'record' || st.lock != null) {
          // The page's own resolution, not a guess at it — see the `record`
          // field's note: recIdx, then lock, then 0 on the record route.
          const rec = st.recIdx != null ? st.recIdx : (st.lock != null ? st.lock : (st.route === 'record' ? 0 : null));
          const p = rec != null ? this.data('PAIRS')[rec] : null;
          return p ? p[0] : null;
        }
        return null;
      })(),
      ath: ath,
      athRoute: athRoute,
      // ── §5 of r47 · THE HELD STAR, SO A PHONE TAP IS REPORTABLE ───────────
      // You measured five phone taps: one locked a star and opened its card, so
      // the tap unquestionably changed what the visitor is looking at — and 0
      // tuples were emitted. You had the mechanism exactly right. The narrow
      // branch of starClick deliberately sets starLock / hoverStar / started /
      // prev and NOT lock or view, because view === 'album' would swap the
      // compact card for the full album panel. That design is right and I am not
      // changing it. The fault is that none of those four was in the tuple, so
      // `record`, `lock` and `view` did not move and the sameness test upstream
      // saw nothing.
      //
      // So the held star is IN the tuple, which is the less invasive of the two
      // routes you offered — no behaviour changes on either breakpoint.
      //   star        the design's own handle for what is held, or null
      //   starRecord  its PAIRS index when the star carries a record
      // Most of the field carries no catalogue row, so starRecord is null far
      // more often than star is — a held star is not the same claim as a held
      // record, and conflating them is what would have made `record` lie.
      // goToNav accepts `star` back, so the round trip closes.
      star: st.starLock == null ? null : st.starLock,
      starRecord: (() => {
        if (st.starLock == null) return null;
        const n = parseInt(String(st.starLock).slice(1), 10);
        return Number.isFinite(n) ? n : null;
      })(),
      // §3 of r47 · the three item keys, each null off its own surface so a
      // reader can never mistake a stale value for the current one.
      article: st.route === 'ath-article' ? this.articleSlug(st) : null,
      drop: (st.route === 'ath-release' || st.route === 'ath-track') ? (st.dropKey || null) : null,
      track: (st.route === 'ath-release' || st.route === 'ath-track') ? this.athTrackKey(st) : null,
      // ── THE MAP HAS A URL, AND IT CARRIES ITS GATEWAY (2026-09-05, Tom) ───
      // "Entering MAP view from any label keeps that label's header but the URL
      // becomes a StellarAtlas.music address." So the tuple says both: the
      // gateway brand (already `label` above, which map view does not change)
      // and the address the host should write. Every other route reports its
      // own site's url, so a host route can be written from the tuple alone
      // without re-deriving which brand the visitor is inside.
      url: (() => {
        // A card page names itself. This returned the MAP address for every
        // star route, so a card's canonical url pointed at a different page
        // than the one on screen — and this field is documented as canonical,
        // so a host is entitled to write it verbatim.
        if (st.route === 'star' && st.view === 'ath-curator') {
          // ⚠ addr() can now return null for a card the host does not serve,
          // and '…' + null is the string "null" — a worse address than none.
          // No address means fall through to the map's, which is a real page.
          const cp = this.addr('ath-card', st.athCard || 0);
          if (cp) return this.siteUrl('ath').replace(/\/$/, '') + cp;
        }
        if (st.route === 'star') return this.mapUrl(this.labelKeyOf(st));
        if (ath) return this.siteUrl('ath');
        if (this.CLS_PAGES && this.CLS_PAGES[st.route]) return this.siteUrl('cls');
        if (st.route === 'group') return this.siteUrl('group');
        return this.siteUrl(st.label == null ? 0 : st.label);
      })(),
      // THE BROWSE FACET (§63). "Any category can be represented by a Starmap —
      // it's literally governed by the Browse menu." So the host hears WHICH
      // facet, by NAME (indices shift when real values arrive):
      //   facet — results mode: one pick, { group, value } | null
      //   picks — filter mode:  ['Genres:Ambient', 'Formats:LP'] ; Clear → []
      // Both are what the design already keys on internally (browseSel / picks
      // are 'Group:Value' strings), exposed rather than re-derived.
      facet: (st.view === 'results' && st.browseSel) ? { group: String(st.browseSel).split(':')[0], value: String(st.browseSel).split(':').slice(1).join(':') } : null,
      // ── THE CONSTELLATION ANCHOR (2026-09-07, r49, page kind 1) ──────────
      // The one page kind you could not work around host-side, and you should
      // not have had to: you were reading window.__DC_ATLAS.state.conAnchor
      // directly, which is exactly the state-peeking the channel exists to
      // replace. The constellation set has a real canonical address (catCrumbs,
      // shareCat), so a Back to it was a promise the site could not keep.
      //
      // It is the anchor NAME, not an index — the same value catChipLabel and
      // the canonical path already print, and the same value goToNav accepts
      // back. null off the surface, so a stale anchor cannot be mistaken for a
      // live one. IT IS ALSO IN THE DIFF, because a field the tuple carries and
      // the diff ignores only works once, and the constellation anchor is the
      // original instance of that (r22).
      conAnchor: (st.view === 'results' && st.conAnchor) ? String(st.conAnchor) : null,
      // ── THE ATH DECK CARD (2026-09-11, Tom) ─────────────────────────────
      // The four items he named — Idea, Curator Map, Podcast, Subscribe — are
      // cards on the curator deck, and the tuple did not carry which card was
      // open. So the host had nothing to write an address from: copy returned
      // the page you arrived on, refresh lost the card, Back skipped all four.
      //
      // The SLUG, not the index, for the reason ATH_CARD_SLUG gives. Null off
      // the deck, so a stale card can never be mistaken for a live one — the
      // same discipline as article, drop and track above.
      card: (st.route === 'star' && st.view === 'ath-curator') ? this.athCardSlug(st.athCard || 0) : null,
      picks: (st.picks || []).slice()
    };
  }
  // Keeps its own last tuple: this runtime's componentDidUpdate is called
  // with no arguments, so prevState is not available to diff against.
  _notifyNav() {
    if (typeof window === 'undefined') return;
    const b = this._navTuple(this.state);
    const a = this._lastNav;
    this._lastNav = b;
    // ── THE LATCH IS CONSUMED ONCE, ON EVERY PATH (2026-09-07, r49) ────────
    // You found this and you are right: it was one line and it was half done.
    // The latch was cleared at the two LATE returns and not at the two EARLY
    // ones (first paint, and the sameness test). goToNav always produces a
    // non-empty patch and always arms the latch — so a __DC_GO whose
    // navigational fields already matched armed it PERMANENTLY, and the next
    // genuine click was swallowed in silence. A latch that is armed on one
    // path and disarmed on another is not a latch.
    //
    // So it is read and cleared HERE, before any return can skip it. Its
    // contract is exactly one update: whichever update follows the arming is
    // the one suppressed, whether or not that update changes a compared field.
    // Your host-side dedupe is no longer load-bearing — keep it if you like,
    // but it is belt-and-braces now rather than the thing holding this up.
    const inbound = this._inboundNav;
    this._inboundNav = false;
    if (!a) return;                                   // first paint — the host set it
    const sameFacet = (a.facet == null && b.facet == null) || (a.facet && b.facet && a.facet.group === b.facet.group && a.facet.value === b.facet.value);
    const samePicks = (a.picks || []).join('|') === (b.picks || []).join('|');
    if (a.label === b.label && a.route === b.route && a.view === b.view && a.record === b.record && a.artist === b.artist
      // §3 of r47 · the three item keys are IN THE DIFF. Without them, opening a
      // second journal piece changed no compared field, so NAV never fired and
      // you would hear about the first piece forever — which is the same defect
      // r22 left on the constellation anchor. A field the tuple carries and the
      // diff ignores is a field that only works once.
      && a.article === b.article && a.drop === b.drop && a.track === b.track
      // ── RULE 7, FOUR FIELDS SHORT (2026-09-09 audit) ─────────────────────
      // "A FIELD THE TUPLE CARRIES AND THE DIFF IGNORES ONLY WORKS ONCE. Add a
      // field to the tuple, add it to the diff in _notifyNav. This has bitten
      // three times." It had bitten a fourth: ath, athRoute, starRecord and url
      // were emitted and never compared.
      //
      // All four are DERIVED from fields the diff already had, so nothing has
      // gone wrong yet — which is exactly why it survived. url is the canonical
      // address, composed from the label, route and record; starRecord is the
      // held star's record; ath and athRoute restate identity and the ATH route.
      // A derived field is still a field: the moment one of their inputs changes
      // shape, or a new derivation reads something the diff does not, the tuple
      // goes quiet and the host stops being told. The rule does not exempt them
      // and neither does this.
      && a.ath === b.ath && a.athRoute === b.athRoute
      && a.starRecord === b.starRecord && a.url === b.url
      // Rule 7, fifth instance: a field the tuple carries and the diff ignores
      // only works once. Without this, paging the deck changes no compared
      // field, NAV never fires, and the host keeps writing the first card's
      // address for every card after it.
      && a.card === b.card
      // §5 of r47 · the held star, or a phone tap still reports nothing. Same
      // reason the three item keys are here: a field the tuple carries and the
      // diff ignores only works once.
      && a.star === b.star
      // The constellation anchor, for the same reason — r22 left it out of the
      // diff and that is the canonical example this rule is named after.
      && a.conAnchor === b.conAnchor
      && sameFacet && samePicks) return;
    if (typeof window.__DC_ON_NAV !== 'function') return;
    // §1e · DO NOT ECHO A MOVE THE HOST ASKED FOR. When the host calls
    // __DC_GO (popstate, a back button, a restored session) the design changes
    // view, componentDidUpdate fires, and without this guard we would announce
    // that move straight back to the host — which would write the address again
    // for an address the host is mid-way through applying. One-way in each
    // direction per gesture: a click announces outward, a host call does not.
    if (inbound) return;
    try { window.__DC_ON_NAV(b); } catch (e) { /* the host's problem, not the design's */ }
  }
  // ════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
  // §1e · THE INBOUND COUNTERPART TO __DC_ON_NAV (2026-09-07)
  // ════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
  // YOUR QUESTION, ANSWERED PLAINLY: **yes, at any moment, cleanly.** The design
  // does not assume it is the one initiating. Every navigator in this file —
  // go(), goRoute(), goAth(), goCard(), openRecord(), openAlbum() — is a plain
  // setState and nothing else; there is no mount-time-only work behind a view,
  // no imperative teardown, no animation that has to be driven from the click.
  // A view is a function of state, so putting the design on a view from outside
  // is the same operation as clicking, minus the click.
  //
  // So this takes the SAME TUPLE __DC_ON_NAV hands you, and puts the design on
  // that view. No re-mount, no reload, no flash: one setState.
  //
  // It is deliberately tolerant — send the whole tuple or just the fields you
  // know. Anything absent is left alone, and an unknown route is IGNORED rather
  // than rendered blank (same rule as __DC_ROUTE at arrival). It returns true if
  // it acted and false if it refused, so a caller can tell the difference
  // between "done" and "I did not understand that".
  //
  // Reached three ways, so it fits whatever you already have:
  //   window.__DC_GO(tuple)
  //   window.dispatchEvent(new CustomEvent('dc:go', { detail: tuple }))
  //   the same function on the element, as el.__dcGo, for a scoped host
  goToNav(t) {
    if (!t || typeof t !== 'object') return false;
    const st = this.state;
    const patch = {};
    // The label, by its carried key — the §1 mechanism, driven from outside.
    // This is what makes a back button across an Explore switch land on the
    // right brand rather than on index 0.
    if (typeof t.label === 'string' && t.label) {
      patch.labelKey = t.label;
      const hit = this.arrivalTable()[t.label];
      if (hit) { patch.label = hit[0]; if (t.theme == null) patch.theme = hit[1]; }
      else if (t.label === 'across-the-horizon' && t.theme == null) patch.theme = 'light';
    }
    if (typeof t.route === 'string' && t.route) {
      if (this.ROUTES.indexOf(t.route) === -1) return false;
      // ⚠ DOOR 7 (2026-09-14, engineering §4: "the six-door census is not
      // closed"). They are right — this method wrote route unconditionally
      // with no phoneNoMap() anywhere in it, and it is the door THEY drive, so
      // a host navigation to the map on a phone landed on a map that is not
      // supposed to exist there. They are gating their half; this gates ours,
      // so the rule holds however the navigation arrives.
      //
      // phoneMapNote: 1, because this IS arrival-class: goToNav is how a
      // shared address becomes a view, so the visitor has a link to explain.
      if (t.route === 'star' && this.phoneNoMap()) {
        patch.route = 'catalogue'; patch.view = 'releases'; patch.phoneMapNote = 1;
      } else patch.route = t.route;
    }
    // ⚠ CLOSED-CHECKED, like route (2026-09-09, code audit part 5). The column is
    // a chain of mutually exclusive tests with no default branch, so an unknown
    // view drew an EMPTY drawer beside a live star field. VIEWS is the list;
    // anything else is ignored, exactly as an unknown route is.
    if (typeof t.view === 'string' && t.view && this.VIEWS.indexOf(t.view) > -1) patch.view = t.view;
    // ── · PAGE KIND 3 · A RECORD BY HANDLE, AND A RECORD THAT CLEARS ───────
    // (2026-09-07, r49.) Two faults, both yours to have found.
    //
    // (a) AN INDEX IS NOT AN IDENTITY, and this is the third instance of that
    //     rule in this file. You rewrite PAIRS on every Browse pick, so a
    //     `record: 4` that crosses the channel means a different album after a
    //     filter. So a HANDLE is accepted alongside the index, matched against
    //     row[7] — the address you are filling now — and never against row[6],
    //     for the reason in addr('album'). A handle we cannot find is REFUSED
    //     rather than landing on record 0, same rule as an unmatched article
    //     slug: a Back to a record we cannot resolve should say so.
    //
    // (b) THE RULING YOU ASKED FOR, on `record: null` being ignored. The
    //     contract comment above stands and stays uniform: ABSENT means leave
    //     it alone. But absent and explicitly-null are different statements,
    //     and the tuple can tell them apart — so `'record' in t` with a null
    //     value now CLEARS recIdx, lock and open. You do not need to invent a
    //     separate clearing signal, and a host that omits the key still gets
    //     the old behaviour. You were right that the identity survived a Back
    //     off an album while the surface went away, and right to say so after
    //     withdrawing it — the withdrawal is what would have cost the next
    //     session a day.
    if ('record' in t) {
      if (t.record == null) {
        Object.assign(patch, { recIdx: null, lock: null, open: false });
      } else if (/^\d+$/.test(String(t.record))) {
        patch.recIdx = +t.record; patch.lock = +t.record; patch.open = true;
      } else {
        const want = String(t.record).replace(/^.*\//, '');
        const P = this.data('PAIRS') || [];
        let idx = null;
        for (let q = 0; q < P.length; q++) {
          const addr = P[q] && typeof P[q][7] === 'string' ? P[q][7] : '';
          if (addr && addr.replace(/^.*\//, '') === want) { idx = q; break; }
        }
        if (idx == null) return false;
        patch.recIdx = idx; patch.lock = idx; patch.open = true;
      }
    }
    // ── AND INBOUND, OR THE ADDRESS IS WRITE-ONLY (2026-09-11) ───────────
    // An address you can copy but not open is not an address. This takes the
    // slug the tuple emits, and sets the route and view with it: a cold load
    // of /curator-map has no deck to page, so naming the card alone would land
    // on the ATH map's default card and look like the link had failed.
    //
    // An unrecognised slug is REFUSED, not rounded to card 0 — the same ruling
    // as an unmatched article slug and an unmatched record handle. A Back to a
    // card we cannot resolve should say so rather than show a different page.
    if (t.card != null && String(t.card)) {
      const ci = this.athCardIndex(t.card);
      if (ci == null) return false;
      patch.athCard = ci;
      // Door 7b, same method: ?card= names a curator card ON the map, so a
      // phone gets the volumes list those cards summarise — the same answer
      // arrival() gives for the same address.
      if (this.phoneNoMap()) {
        patch.route = 'ath-all-releases'; patch.theme = 'light';
        patch.labelKey = 'across-the-horizon'; patch.phoneMapNote = 1;
      } else {
        patch.route = 'star';
        patch.view = 'ath-curator';
      }
      // ⚠ UNCONDITIONALLY, AND THE GUARD THAT WAS HERE WAS DEAD CODE.
      // It read `patch.labelKey == null && st.labelKey == null`, but §1 of
      // arrival() resolves labelKey on first paint and says outright that
      // "nothing re-derives it" — so the second test is never true and the
      // branch never ran. From the Northern Spy landing, goToNav({card}) gave
      // route star + view ath-curator + athCard 2 under labelKey
      // 'northern-spy', and the tuple then reported ath:false with
      // url '/map/northern-spy' — so a host writing the address from the tuple
      // overwrote /curator-map the instant it accepted it. The feature undid
      // itself one tick after working.
      //
      // A CARD IS AN ATH SURFACE. There is no such thing as this deck under
      // another label, so identity is not conditional on what the visitor was
      // looking at before. arrival()'s cold-load branch has always set both
      // fields flatly; these two halves of one feature now agree.
      patch.labelKey = 'across-the-horizon';
      if (t.theme == null) patch.theme = 'light';
    }
    if (typeof t.artist === 'string' && t.artist) patch.artist = t.artist;
    // ── §3 of r47 · the same three keys, inbound ─────────────────────────
    // Accepts a slug (what the tuple emits) or an index (what __DC_ARTICLE has
    // always also taken). An unmatched slug is REFUSED rather than silently
    // landing on article 0 — a Back to a piece we cannot find should tell you
    // so, not show a different piece.
    if (t.article != null && String(t.article)) {
      const want = String(t.article);
      const list = this.data('JOURNAL_LIST') || [];
      let idx = list.map((x, k) => ({ s: x[6], k: k })).filter(x => x.s === want).map(x => x.k)[0];
      if (idx == null) {
        const j = this['DEFAULT_JOURNAL'] || [];
        idx = j.map((x, k) => ({ s: x[0], k: k })).filter(x => x.s === want).map(x => x.k)[0];
      }
      if (idx == null && /^\d+$/.test(want)) idx = +want;
      if (idx == null) return false;
      patch.article = idx;
    }
    // The drop key as state stores it ('d<vi>-<di>'), and the track key in
    // arrival()'s 'v<vol>d<drop>t<idx>' form — or a bare index on the drop the
    // deck already holds, exactly as __DC_TRACK allows.
    if (typeof t.drop === 'string' && t.drop) patch.dropKey = t.drop;
    // §5 of r47 · the held star, inbound. `star: null` RELEASES it, which is what
    // a Back off a tapped star has to mean — and it releases the way this file's
    // own release does, breakpoint by breakpoint, rather than leaving the album
    // panel standing over an empty field (the seam starClear was fixed for).
    if ('star' in t) {
      if (t.star == null) {
        Object.assign(patch, { starLock: null, hoverStar: null, constellation: false });
        const narrowNow = (st.winW || (this.VPS[st.vp] && this.VPS[st.vp][0]) || 1180) < 700;
        if (!narrowNow) { patch.lock = null; patch.view = st.prev || 'releases'; }
      } else if (typeof t.star === 'string' && t.star) {
        patch.starLock = t.star;
        patch.hoverStar = t.star;
        // ── · PAGE KIND 4 · prev, open AND started (2026-09-07, r49) ───────
        // You found that this branch never wrote `prev`, while the RELEASE
        // branch six lines up reads st.prev to decide where a released star
        // returns to. So a host-driven lock-then-release landed on whatever
        // view the last CLICK happened to record — the same tuple giving a
        // different page by click than by __DC_GO, which is the one thing this
        // channel must never do. starClick's own narrow branch sets prev,
        // open and started; this now sets the same three, from the same view,
        // so the two paths converge.
        if (t.view == null && st.view !== 'album') patch.prev = st.view;
        patch.open = true;
        patch.started = true;
      }
    }
    if (t.track != null && String(t.track)) {
      const m = String(t.track).match(/^v(\d+)d(\d+)t(\d+)$/i);
      if (m) { patch.dropKey = 'd' + (+m[1] - 1) + '-' + (+m[2] - 1); patch.trackIdx = +m[3]; }
      else if (/^\d+$/.test(String(t.track))) patch.trackIdx = +t.track;
      else return false;
    }
    if (typeof t.theme === 'string' && (t.theme === 'light' || t.theme === 'dark')) patch.theme = t.theme;
    // The browse facet, in the same 'Group:Value' form the design keys on.
    // ── · PAGE KIND 2 · THE HEADING, NOT JUST THE FILTER (2026-09-07, r49) ──
    // This restored browseSel and picks and stopped there, so the set came back
    // filtered but unnamed — and resultLabel/resultCat are what the heading
    // prints, what the crumb reads and what the canonical is built from. You
    // were right that both are derivable from what was already here, and right
    // about the shape: the Group:Value split for a single pick, and
    // commitFilter's own 'Filter' + joined picks for a multi-facet view. Built
    // the same way in both places rather than a second time, so the heading and
    // the set cannot disagree. It clears `lock` too, as you asked — a results
    // view with a record still held is the seam commitFilter clears for.
    if (t.facet && t.facet.group) {
      const g = String(t.facet.group);
      const v = t.facet.value == null ? '' : String(t.facet.value);
      patch.browseSel = g + ':' + v;
      patch.resultCat = g;
      patch.resultLabel = v;
      patch.lock = null;
      patch.open = true;
    } else if (t.facet === null) {
      patch.browseSel = null;
      if (!Array.isArray(t.picks) || !t.picks.length) { patch.resultCat = null; patch.resultLabel = null; }
    }
    if (Array.isArray(t.picks)) {
      patch.picks = t.picks.slice();
      // commitFilter's wording, verbatim, so a restored multi-facet view reads
      // exactly as the one the click built. A single named facet above wins.
      if (!(t.facet && t.facet.group)) {
        patch.resultCat = 'Filter';
        patch.resultLabel = t.picks.length ? t.picks.map(p => String(p).split(':')[1]).join(' + ') : 'All records';
        patch.lock = null;
      }
    }
    // ── · PAGE KIND 1 · THE CONSTELLATION SET, INBOUND ──────────────────
    // Last, deliberately: a constellation is a more specific claim than a
    // facet, so if a host sends both it wins. Sets the same five fields
    // rv_openCon does — anchor, constellation, the two result strings and the
    // view — because that is the surface the address names. `conAnchor: null`
    // clears the set the way clearCatFilter does, so Back OUT of a
    // constellation is expressible too, not just Back INTO one.
    if ('conAnchor' in t) {
      if (t.conAnchor == null) {
        Object.assign(patch, { conAnchor: null, constellation: false, resultCat: null, resultLabel: null });
        if (t.view == null) patch.view = 'releases';
      } else if (String(t.conAnchor)) {
        const who = String(t.conAnchor);
        Object.assign(patch, {
          conAnchor: who, constellation: true,
          resultCat: 'Constellation', resultLabel: who,
          open: true, lock: null
        });
        if (t.view == null) patch.view = 'results';
      }
    }
    // Arriving from outside is an arrival, so the transient furniture closes —
    // a back button should not restore a page with someone's cart hanging open.
    // ⚠ hoverStar is NOT in this list. It was, and it silently clobbered the
    // §5 star block above — which runs first, so a blanket reset here won the
    // very field it had just set. The star's own branch owns hoverStar; when no
    // star is named, it is cleared here as before.
    // getMusic JOINED THIS LIST (2026-09-07, r49): every real navigator resets
    // the ath-release / ath-track "Get the music" disclosure and this reset did
    // not, so a host-driven move onto a drop could arrive with another drop's
    // panel already hanging open. Folded in here rather than given its own turn,
    // as you suggested — it is the same class as the rest of this line.
    Object.assign(patch, { menuOpen: false, browseOpen: false, explore: false, cart: false, queue: false, getMusic: null });
    if (!('star' in t)) patch.hoverStar = null;
    if (Object.keys(patch).length === 0) return false;
    // Suppress the echo (see _notifyNav) and apply. pinLabel is not used: a
    // host-driven move states its label or leaves the carried one alone.
    this._inboundNav = true;
    if (patch.route === 'ath-map' || (patch.route || st.route) === 'ath-map') this._mapPass = 0;
    this.setState(patch);
    return true;
  }
  // ══ THE PHONE-HAS-NO-MAP RULE IS AN INVARIANT, NOT A SET OF GATES ════════
  // (2026-09-15, Tom: "I don't understand what's happening when I look at the
  // ATH landing page in a mobile view, it still has the hover settings. There
  // are no play buttons… maybe the views in this app in the .dc.html file are
  // not accurate and it's really set up the right way.")
  //
  // His second guess was right: the file IS set up correctly. Eight doors
  // redirect a phone away from a map — six of them send ath-map to
  // ath-all-releases. But EVERY ONE OF THEM FIRES ON NAVIGATION, and becoming
  // narrow is not a navigation. So:
  //
  //   · in the design tool, switching the viewport picker is a setState, not a
  //     resize and not a nav — so the ATH landing stayed on the DESKTOP curator
  //     map squeezed into 390px. That is exactly what he was looking at: a
  //     hover surface with no hover, and therefore no play buttons.
  //   · on a real phone, rotating landscape → portrait crosses 700 with no
  //     navigation at all.
  //   · on a desktop, narrowing the window does the same.
  //
  // AND IT IS THE ANSWER TO THE DOOR-COUNTING PROBLEM. My comment claimed four
  // doors, my audit found six, engineering found eight. Counting was always the
  // wrong instinct: a rule enforced at N call sites is a rule that breaks at
  // site N+1. Enforced HERE it holds for every door that exists, every door
  // added later, and for the three transitions above that are not doors at all.
  //
  // Fires once: the patch leaves a non-map route, so the condition is false on
  // the next pass. No note — nothing was clicked, and a visitor who rotated
  // their phone does not need a shared-link explanation.
  // ══ MAP IS A MODE, NOT A DESTINATION — ONE PLACE (2026-09-15, Tom) ═══════
  // "In map mode, all pages show up in the sidebar. They don't pop back to
  // grid mode… If I'm on an album page and I click an artist profile, that
  // profile shows up in the sidebar if I'm in map mode."
  //
  // The principle was already stated in this file, at goStar: "MAP IS A MODE,
  // NOT A DESTINATION… the surface you are on is the surface you stay on" —
  // and the artists list honours it (L15651: on 'star' it fills the column
  // instead of leaving). But the rule was written at each call site, so the
  // sites nobody revisited kept navigating away: goAlbumArtist was the one Tom
  // names, and it wrote route:'artist' unconditionally.
  //
  // THIS IS THE DOOR-COUNTING LESSON AGAIN. A rule enforced at N call sites
  // breaks at site N+1 — my no-phone-map comment claimed four doors, the audit
  // found six, engineering found eight, and the fix was to make it an
  // invariant. Same shape here: artistPatch decides once, and every artist
  // navigation asks it rather than restating it.
  //
  // WHAT IT RETURNS. Off the map: the artist's page, as before. On the map:
  // the same view the artists list already produces for the same click, so
  // one artist reached two ways lands in one place. The map stays open, the
  // lock is released, and the column carries the artist.
  // ⚠⚠ browseSel CARRIES THE FACET, AND null WAS THE BUG (2026-09-15, review).
  // I copied `browseSel: null` from the artists-list click and spread it to
  // six paths, which produced a panel headed "Jeremy Cunningham · Artist · 9
  // records" above nine records by eight different artists — a header
  // asserting a fact its own rows contradict.
  //
  // THE DESIGN MUST NOT FILTER THIS ITSELF, and that is settled: "THE DESIGN
  // DOES NOT FILTER BY LABEL, AND MUST NOT... PAIRS carries no label field, so
  // there is nothing here to filter ON... Scoping a label's catalogue is a HOST
  // re-scope. One authority: the table." (2026-09-09, Tom.) Filtering rows here
  // would put the count line and the grid in disagreement with whatever the
  // host thinks it published — the exact failure that rule exists to prevent.
  //
  // The facet channel is how a set gets scoped: browseSel is a 'Group:Value'
  // string, published to the host as `facet: { group, value }` in the nav
  // tuple, and the host answers by re-publishing PAIRS for that facet. With
  // browseSel null NO FACET WAS PUBLISHED AT ALL, so the host was never asked
  // to scope and the panel drew the whole table. One string fixes it, through
  // the contract that already exists rather than around it.
  //
  // ⚠ AND THE FACET TURNED OUT TO BE THE WRONG INSTRUMENT (2026-09-15, second
  // pass). I reached for browseSel because the no-filter ruling seemed to
  // forbid the design resolving this itself. Re-reading it, the ruling is
  // narrower than I took it to be: "PAIRS carries no LABEL field, so there is
  // nothing here to filter ON." Artist is not in that position — it is
  // PAIRS[q][0], a field the design already reads on every row it draws.
  //
  // So the panel filters its own records by name, honestly, from the table it
  // has; there is no facet to publish, nothing new for the host to resolve,
  // and no wire item. The ruling stands untouched — it forbids inventing a
  // scope the data cannot support, not reading a field that exists.
  artistPatch(name, extra) {
    const st = this.state;
    const onMap = st.route === 'star' || st.route === 'ath-map';
    const base = { browseOpen: false, explore: false, cart: false, queue: false, menuOpen: false, ctx: null, ctxHover: null };
    return Object.assign(base,
      onMap
        ? { view: 'artist', artist: name, browseSel: null, open: true, lock: null, hoverStar: null }
        : { route: 'artist', artist: name },
      extra || {});
  }
  // ⚠⚠ 'ath-map' IS NOT IN THIS RULE, AND PUTTING IT HERE WAS A REGRESSION
  // (2026-09-15, Tom: "the ATH landing page won't load in the design doc on
  // mobile… it was working 10 minutes ago. You did something to it.")
  //
  // He was right and it was this. I wrote 'star' and 'ath-map' into one
  // condition because both are called maps. They are not the same thing:
  //
  //   · 'star' IS the star field — a VIEW MODE, and the one the phone does not
  //     get. Redirecting it to the catalogue is the whole simplification.
  //   · 'ath-map' is ACROSS THE HORIZON'S LANDING PAGE. Its phone branch,
  //     isPhoneMap (markup ~L1795), is `narrow && route === 'ath-map'` — a
  //     surface that EXISTS ONLY AT NARROW, purpose-built as ATH's phone
  //     landing: a deck of drop cards with curator discs and names, in a
  //     drawer. There is no star field in it.
  //
  // So this rule was redirecting away from the very surface built for the case
  // it was trying to serve, and ATH lost its landing page on the phone
  // entirely. The lesson is narrow and I should have caught it: a route's NAME
  // is not its kind. isPhoneMap's own gate said what it was for.
  //
  // ATH's phone landing is its own design question — it is a hover surface,
  // which is what Tom reported before this, and that is worth answering. But
  // the answer is not to delete the page.
  _enforceNoPhoneMap() {
    if (!this.phoneNoMap()) return;
    if (this.state.route !== 'star') return;
    this.setState({ route: 'catalogue', view: 'releases', lock: null, hoverStar: null, hoverRow: null, prev: null, open: true });
  }
  componentDidUpdate() {
    this._enforceNoPhoneMap();
    this._notifyNav();
    this._notifyTransport();
    this.fitPanels();
    // Once more after the browser has laid the new cards out: on a route change
    // the first pass can run before the incoming panels have their heights.
    if (this._fitAgain) cancelAnimationFrame(this._fitAgain);
    this._fitAgain = requestAnimationFrame(() => this.fitPanels());
    // Selection out to the star field. Here rather than in the bridge's own
    // hook precisely so there is one hook.
    this._pushStarState();
  }

  // ══ V2 · WHEN A COACH MARK APPEARS ═══════════════════════════════
  // Two lessons, each fired once, from the one place — called by
  // componentDidMount and componentDidUpdate so a lesson can be triggered by
  // arriving OR by moving, and cannot be triggered twice by both.
  //
  //   1. ARRIVAL · point at the Audio menu and name the choice. Tom's words:
  //      "navigate the site as a traditional audio player with play buttons and
  //      a queue OR use hover audio mode."
  //   2. THE STAR MAP IN PLAYER MODE · "Star map browsing is better in hover
  //      mode — try now." Only in player mode, because in hover mode there is
  //      nothing to suggest.
  //
  // EVERY GUARD IS A FLAG, NOT A TIMER, so this is safe to call from
  // componentDidUpdate: it sets state only when a flag flips, and setting the
  // flag is what stops it. A coach mark that could re-fire on its own render is
  // an infinite loop, and this file has been bitten by a setState in an update
  // hook before.
  //
  // The arrival lesson waits 900ms. Not for drama: a callout that is already
  // there when the page paints reads as part of the page, and the visitor has
  // to be looking at the site before being told something about it. The star
  // lesson has no delay — it answers a move the visitor just made.
  // ── THE COACH MARKS ARE GONE, SUPERSEDED BY GUIDE (2026-09-11) ─────────
  // They were PAUSED on 2026-09-09 (Tom: "let's remove the tooltips for now")
  // behind a single COACH_ENABLED flag, on the reasoning that "for now" meant
  // restoring them should not be a rebuild. That was right on the day and it
  // stopped being true the next one: GUIDE, written 2026-09-10, absorbed both
  // lessons deliberately — "one teaching surface, one state model" — and it
  // says where each went. The arrival lesson became the orientation card
  // (GUIDE_INTRO_ID). The star lesson became 'guide-map', fired by the
  // visitor's own move to the map rather than shown on arrival.
  //
  // So this was not a paused feature waiting to come back, it was a REPLACED
  // one waiting to be noticed — a flag nobody would ever set true, holding
  // ~250 lines of markup, styles, state and a scheduler that could not run.
  // Removed whole: COACH_ENABLED, _coachCheck and its two call sites, the
  // callout markup and its style keys, and the coach/coachSeen/coachStarSeen
  // state. Both lessons still exist as the record of what was tried — in
  // GUIDE, doing their job.
  //
  // ⚠ NOT REMOVED, deliberately: the QUEUE NOTICE (the "hover audio is paused
  // while tracks are in the queue" cursor tag). The original flag note called
  // that out as a separate thing and it was right — it is a RULING about two
  // audio systems colliding, fired by an action the visitor just took, not a
  // first-visit lesson. Removing it would leave hover mode silently doing
  // nothing with no explanation.

  // ══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
  // GUIDE · the site teaching itself (2026-09-10, Tom)
  //
  // THE VOCABULARY IS LOCKED AND THESE FOUR WORDS DO NOT OVERLAP:
  //
  //   EXPLORE   where else can I go — the Stellar Atlas universe. Reserved.
  //             Nothing in this system may use that word.
  //   VIEW      Grid | Map · how I see this catalogue
  //   AUDIO     Hover | Player · how I listen
  //   GUIDE     how does this work
  //
  // GUIDE absorbed the old coach marks rather than sitting beside them: one
  // teaching surface, one state model. They were paused behind a flag on
  // 2026-09-09 and removed on 2026-09-11, once it was clear the flag would
  // never be set true again — the note where COACH_ENABLED used to live has
  // the reasoning. The arrival lesson is now the orientation card; the star
  // lesson is now 'guide-map', fired by the visitor's own move to the map
  // rather than shown on arrival.
  //
  // WHAT THIS SYSTEM IS NOT: a tour. Nothing is modal, nothing has a Next, and
  // every surface yields the moment the visitor does the thing it is describing
  // (see guideYield). Teaching is a consequence of an action, not a gate before
  // one — the single exception is the arrival orientation, which is also the
  // only thing here that is not triggered by the visitor.
  // ══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════

  XP_KEY = 'stellar-atlas:experience:v1';
  XP_VERSION = 1;

  // ── WHAT THE BROWSER REMEMBERS ─────────────────────────────────────────────
  // ⚠ THIS IS NOT IDENTITY AND MUST NEVER BECOME IT. No cookie, no fingerprint,
  // no attempt to know who anyone is. One localStorage key holding what THIS
  // BROWSER has already been taught — the same discipline the hero cache
  // follows: this file only ever reads and writes keys it owns.
  //
  // ⚠ VERSIONED, NOT A BOOLEAN. `hasSeenOnboarding = true` is the trap: a year
  // from now one ancient flag would block every new lesson. Each tip carries
  // its own version in its id ('guide-map:v1'), so a single feature's teaching
  // can be reissued without replaying the introduction.
  xpDefault() {
    return {
      version: this.XP_VERSION,
      firstSeenAt: null, lastSeenAt: null, sessionCount: 0,
      viewMode: null, audioMode: null,
      marks: {},      // 'map.opened' → ISO timestamp of the first time
      completed: {},  // tip id → ISO timestamp
      dismissed: {}   // tip id → { count, at }
    };
  }
  xpLoad() {
    if (this._xp) return this._xp;
    let v = null;
    try {
      const raw = (typeof localStorage !== 'undefined') && localStorage.getItem(this.XP_KEY);
      v = raw ? JSON.parse(raw) : null;
    } catch (e) { v = null; }
    // A version we do not recognise is not an error and is not a reset: keep the
    // marks (what the visitor has DONE is still true) and drop the teaching
    // state (what they were TOLD may no longer be what we say).
    if (v && v.version !== this.XP_VERSION) {
      v = Object.assign(this.xpDefault(), { marks: v.marks || {}, firstSeenAt: v.firstSeenAt || null, sessionCount: v.sessionCount || 0 });
    }
    this._xp = Object.assign(this.xpDefault(), v || {});
    return this._xp;
  }
  xpWrite(patch) {
    const next = Object.assign(this.xpLoad(), patch || {});
    next.lastSeenAt = new Date().toISOString();
    this._xp = next;
    try { if (typeof localStorage !== 'undefined') localStorage.setItem(this.XP_KEY, JSON.stringify(next)); } catch (e) {}
    return next;
  }
  // Feature code calls THIS and nothing else. It reports what happened; whether
  // that is worth teaching is the coordinator's question, not the feature's.
  // (§21: components report, the guide decides.)
  xpMark(id) {
    const xp = this.xpLoad();
    if (xp.marks[id]) return;                       // first time only, by definition
    const marks = Object.assign({}, xp.marks); marks[id] = new Date().toISOString();
    this.xpWrite({ marks: marks });
    this.emit('guide.mark', { action: 'guide.mark', mark: id });
    // ⚠ A MARK HAS TO REACH THE RENDER. Marks live on the instance, not in
    // state, so writing one changes nothing on screen by itself — and the
    // "Click to lock" line reads a mark directly. It survived its own lock
    // until the next unrelated setState. xpTick is the one state field whose
    // only job is to say "the marks changed"; nothing reads its value.
    this.setState({ xpTick: (this.state.xpTick || 0) + 1 });
    if (this._mounted) this.guideCheck(id);
  }
  xpHas(id) { return !!this.xpLoad().marks[id]; }
  xpDone(tipId) { return !!this.xpLoad().completed[tipId]; }

  // ── CAPABILITY, EACH FROM ITS OWN TEST ─────────────────────────────────────
  // ⚠ WIDTH IS NOT HOVER, and this file has been conflating them: _narrowNow()
  // (w < 700) currently decides the layout tier AND whether audio resolves to
  // player. They are different questions. A 1280px touch display has no hover;
  // a 690px window on a laptop has one. The layout tier keeps the width test —
  // that IS a width question — and audio now asks about hover.
  caps() {
    if (this._caps) return this._caps;
    const mq = (q) => (typeof window !== 'undefined' && window.matchMedia) ? window.matchMedia(q).matches : false;
    this._caps = {
      hover: mq('(hover: hover)'),
      finePointer: mq('(pointer: fine)'),
      reducedMotion: mq('(prefers-reduced-motion: reduce)')
    };
    return this._caps;
  }
  // Every animation in the guide reads this. Reduced motion does not mean "less
  // teaching" — it means the same information arrives at once instead of being
  // choreographed.
  rm() { return this.caps().reducedMotion; }

  // ── THE REGISTRY · tips are DATA ───────────────────────────────────────────
  // Copy lives here so it can change without touching behaviour, and every tip
  // states the event that RETIRES it. A timer is the floor, not the rule: a tip
  // that outlives its own answer is noise.
  //
  // `anchor` is 'header' (hangs under the mode toggles, where the old coach
  // mark hangs) or 'stage' (a card at the field's bottom-left). Two anchors
  // rather than per-element measurement, because a callout that measures a
  // moving control is how the old one ended up pointing at empty space twice.
  GUIDE_TIPS = [
    // ⚠ THIS ONE SPEAKS AFTER THE FACT, AND NOW READS THAT WAY (2026-09-11,
    // Tom: "the hover tip is something I already discovered"). Its mark is
    // audio.hoverHeard — it cannot fire until a cover has ALREADY sounded
    // under the cursor — so the old copy introduced a feature the visitor had
    // just used by accident. "Move across releases to sample them instantly"
    // is a sentence for someone who has not done it yet, and this tip is
    // structurally incapable of reaching that person.
    //
    // So it names what just happened, then does the one useful thing a
    // confirmation can: says how to stop, and offers the other mode. The
    // other three tips DO precede their feature — map.opened, player.opened
    // and queue.first all fire as the surface arrives — which is why they
    // stay introductions and this one does not.
    { id: 'guide-hover:v1', anchor: 'audio', mark: 'audio.hoverHeard',
      title: 'That was hover audio',
      body: 'Every cover plays as you pass it. Switch to Player for full tracks and a queue.',
      ctaLabel: 'Switch to Player →', cta: 'player', ttl: 7000 },
    { id: 'guide-map:v1', anchor: 'field', mark: 'map.opened',
      title: 'Map View',
      body: 'Navigate through the stars to discover releases and artists. Click a star to lock it in place.',
      ctaLabel: 'Got it', cta: 'close', ttl: 12000 },
    { id: 'guide-player:v1', anchor: 'audio', mark: 'player.opened',
      title: 'Full-track Player',
      body: 'Listen to complete tracks, build a queue, and create playlists.',
      ctaLabel: 'Got it', cta: 'close', ttl: 7000 },
    { id: 'guide-queue:v1', anchor: 'queue', mark: 'queue.first',
      title: 'Make it yours',
      body: 'Keep adding tracks to build your listening queue. Turn it into a playlist you can save or share.',
      ctaLabel: 'Got it', cta: 'close', ttl: 8000 }
  ];
  guideTip(id) { return this.GUIDE_TIPS.filter(t => t.id === id)[0] || null; }

  // ── THE COORDINATOR ────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
  // ONE surface at a time, and the arrival orientation outranks everything.
  // Nothing shows over an open panel, during a drag, or on a viewport where the
  // control it names is absent.
  guideBlocked() {
    const st = this.state;
    if (this._narrowNow()) return true;                       // the toggles are not there to point at
    if (st.guideIntro) return true;                           // the orientation owns the screen
    if (st.menuOpen || st.browseOpen || st.exploreOpen || st.qover) return true;
    return false;
  }
  guideCheck(mark) {
    if (!this._mounted || typeof window === 'undefined') return;
    if (this.guideBlocked()) return;
    if (this.state.guideTip) return;                          // one at a time
    const hit = this.GUIDE_TIPS.filter(t => t.mark === mark && !this.xpDone(t.id))[0];
    if (!hit) return;
    this.guideOpen(hit.id);
  }
  // ── A CALLOUT THAT NAMES A CONTROL IS ANCHORED TO THAT CONTROL ───────────
  // (2026-09-10, Tom: "the pop-up came up way to the left… it should come up
  // right over the button, or right under the button.")
  //
  // The tips were pinned to the stage's right gutter, so the card floated ~86px
  // off the toggle it was describing. This is the SAME fault the coach mark
  // above already records — "a fixed offset from the right gutter put the arrow
  // in empty space the moment the chrome stepped at 1728" — and the same fix:
  // measure the control.
  //
  // Measured at OPEN, not per render: the control does not move while a tip is
  // up, and reading layout on every render is how the fit pass used to thrash.
  // Stage-local px, the same frame anchorLeft() uses for the browse panel.
  guideAnchorRect(name) {
    if (typeof document === 'undefined') return null;
    // ⚠ THE VISIBLE ONE. Several headers carry the same anchor name — the
    // label chrome and ATH's own both have a cart — and only one of them is
    // rendered at a time. querySelector takes the first in document order,
    // which is as likely to be the hidden one, and a zero-size rect would
    // park the card in the corner.
    const el = [].slice.call(document.querySelectorAll('[data-guide-anchor="' + name + '"]'))
      .filter(function (n) { const r = n.getBoundingClientRect(); return r.width > 0 && r.height > 0; })[0];
    const stage = document.querySelector('[data-guide-stage]');
    if (!el || !stage) return null;
    const a = el.getBoundingClientRect(), s = stage.getBoundingClientRect();
    // The stage is scaled to fit, so divide by its own scale or every offset is
    // wrong by that factor — the lesson the fit pass paid for twice.
    const k = s.width ? (stage.offsetWidth / s.width) : 1;
    return {
      left: (a.left - s.left) * k,
      right: (s.right - a.right) * k,
      bottom: (a.bottom - s.top) * k,
      width: a.width * k
    };
  }
  // Anchors that, on a narrow viewport, exist ONLY inside the ☰ sheet.
  _sheetAnchors() { return ['audio', 'view', 'queue']; }
  guideOpen(id) {
    const t = this.guideTip(id);
    if (!t) return;
    // ⚠ WAIT FOR THE SHEET RATHER THAN PLACE BLIND (2026-09-10, Tom). On a
    // phone these controls are in the ☰ sheet, so with it closed there is no
    // visible anchor and the card would either not place or park in a corner.
    // Held, not dropped: guideArrive re-runs it the moment the sheet opens, so
    // the teaching still happens — one gesture later, pointing at a row the
    // visitor can actually see and use.
    // ⚠ AND SOME ANCHORS NO LONGER EXIST AT ALL ON A PHONE (2026-09-14). The
    // modes block is hidden, so 'audio' and 'view' can never be measured —
    // without this the tip would sit in _guidePending for the whole session,
    // waiting for a sheet row that is not coming. 'field' goes with them: the
    // map is unreachable. Dropped rather than deferred, which is the honest
    // difference between "not yet" and "not here".
    if (this.phoneNoMap() && ['audio', 'view', 'field'].indexOf(t.anchor) > -1) {
      this._guidePending = null;
      return;
    }
    if (this._narrowNow() && !this.state.menuOpen && this._sheetAnchors().indexOf(t.anchor) > -1) {
      this._guidePending = id;
      return;
    }
    this._guidePending = null;
    this.setState({ guideTip: id, guideAt: this.guideAnchorRect(t.anchor) });
    this.emit('guide.shown', { action: 'guide.shown', tip: id });
    if (this._guideT) clearTimeout(this._guideT);
    this._guideT = setTimeout(() => { this._guideT = null; this.guideClose(id, false); }, t.ttl || 7000);
  }
  guideClose(id, byUser) {
    if (this._guideT) { clearTimeout(this._guideT); this._guideT = null; }
    if (this.state.guideTip !== id) return;
    const xp = this.xpLoad();
    const completed = Object.assign({}, xp.completed); completed[id] = new Date().toISOString();
    this.xpWrite({ completed: completed });
    this.setState({ guideTip: null });
    this.emit(byUser ? 'guide.dismissed' : 'guide.expired', { action: byUser ? 'guide.dismissed' : 'guide.expired', tip: id });
  }
  // ⚠ INTENT OUTRANKS TEACHING (§17). If the visitor does the thing a tip is
  // describing, the tip has done its job and must get out of the way in the
  // same gesture — not after its timer. Called from the mode toggles, the star
  // lock and the queue.
  // Locking a star is the map tip's own answer, so it retires the tip in the
  // same gesture rather than on a timer, and permanently removes the "Click to
  // lock" line from every star plate.
  starLocked() { this.xpMark('map.starLocked'); this.guideYield(); }
  guideYield() {
    if (this.state.guideTip) this.guideClose(this.state.guideTip, false);
    if (this.state.guideIntro) this.guideIntroEnd(false);
  }

  // ── THE ARRIVAL ORIENTATION ────────────────────────────────────────────────
  // "Experience <label> your way. Choose how you see the catalogue and how you
  // hear it." Two sentences that describe the whole architecture, and then the
  // visitor is on their own.
  //
  // It is NOT a modal. The page stays visible and stays clickable; the scrim is
  // a 38% wash with pointer events only on its own two buttons. The controls
  // are lit by being left OUT of the scrim rather than ringed — they are pill
  // toggles with their own hairlines already, and a ring around them would read
  // as a third border.
  //
  // ⚠ NOT ON A PHONE. VIEW and AUDIO are absent below 700 (14c), so there is
  // nothing to point at — the same reason the old coach mark refused there.
  GUIDE_INTRO_ID = 'guide-intro:v1';
  guideIntroStart(manual) {
    if (this._narrowNow() && !manual) return;
    if (this._guideIntroT) { clearTimeout(this._guideIntroT); this._guideIntroT = null; }
    // Under reduced motion the whole sequence lands at once: same words, no
    // choreography. Otherwise the steps are 0 → view → audio → actions.
    // Measured like every other Guide surface: the card describes VIEW and
    // AUDIO, so it hangs off them.
    this.setState({ guideIntro: true, guideStep: this.rm() ? 3 : 0, guideAt: this.guideAnchorRect('audio') });
    this.emit('guide.intro.shown', { action: 'guide.intro.shown', manual: !!manual });
    if (this.rm()) return;
    const step = (n, ms) => setTimeout(() => { if (this.state.guideIntro) this.setState({ guideStep: n }); }, ms);
    this._guideSteps = [step(1, 200), step(2, 1000), step(3, 1500)];
  }
  guideIntroEnd(started) {
    (this._guideSteps || []).forEach(clearTimeout);
    this._guideSteps = null;
    if (!this.state.guideIntro) return;
    const xp = this.xpLoad();
    const completed = Object.assign({}, xp.completed);
    completed[this.GUIDE_INTRO_ID] = new Date().toISOString();
    this.xpWrite({ completed: completed });
    this.setState({ guideIntro: false, guideStep: 0 });
    this.emit(started ? 'guide.intro.completed' : 'guide.intro.skipped',
              { action: started ? 'guide.intro.completed' : 'guide.intro.skipped' });
  }

  // ── ARRIVAL · SESSION BOOKKEEPING AND THE RESTORE ─────────────────────────
  // ⚠ TWO RULINGS MET HERE AND THEY ONLY LOOK LIKE A CONTRADICTION. 09-09:
  // "all the sites default to grid view and hover audio." 09-10 §7: a returning
  // visitor comes back to the modes they left. A DEFAULT is what you do with no
  // information; a RESTORE is what you do with some. So: no stored preference —
  // and that includes every first visit — resolves to Grid + Hover exactly as
  // ruled. A stored one wins, because the visitor chose it.
  //
  // ⚠ AUDIO TRAVELS BETWEEN LABELS, VIEW DOES NOT. How someone listens is about
  // them and is the same question on every site. Whether a catalogue is worth
  // seeing as a constellation is about that catalogue — Egghunt's field is not
  // NNA's. So audioMode is stored globally and viewMode per label, and the key
  // namespace makes either flippable without a migration.
  //
  // ⚠ AND A TOUCH-ONLY DEVICE NEVER RESTORES HOVER. Hover audio on a device
  // with no hover is a mode with no way to start a sound — the capability test
  // overrides the stored preference, every time, silently.
  // ── THE SESSION BOUNDARY IS OURS, NOT THE HOST'S (2026-09-10) ────────────
  // ⚠ THIS CORRECTS DROP-NOTE §4e, WHICH WAS WRONG. I told engineering to keep
  // a sessionStorage marker AND to increment sessionCount in localStorage — and
  // separately claimed the design is the only writer of
  // stellar-atlas:experience:v1. Both cannot be true. Two writers on one key is
  // exactly the collision they raised, and I had specified it.
  //
  // The design owns it, because there is nothing here a host is better placed
  // to answer: sessionStorage is in the same browser, and a session boundary is
  // UI state, not identity. Engineering now writes NOTHING to this key.
  //
  // ⚠ AND IT FIXES A REAL BUG ON OUR SIDE. guideArrive() incremented on every
  // call, and it is called from componentDidMount — so an SPA that remounts the
  // shell counted a new session per navigation, which is the over-count §4e
  // warned engineering about while the design did it too. The marker is the
  // fix: no marker or one older than 30 minutes is a new session; anything
  // fresher is the same visit and counts nothing.
  XP_SESSION_KEY = 'stellar-atlas:session:v1';
  XP_SESSION_IDLE = 30 * 60 * 1000;
  xpNewSession() {
    let prev = null;
    try { prev = (typeof sessionStorage !== 'undefined') ? sessionStorage.getItem(this.XP_SESSION_KEY) : null; } catch (e) { prev = null; }
    const now = Date.now();
    const fresh = prev && (now - Number(prev)) < this.XP_SESSION_IDLE;
    try { if (typeof sessionStorage !== 'undefined') sessionStorage.setItem(this.XP_SESSION_KEY, String(now)); } catch (e) {}
    return !fresh;
  }
  guideArrive() {
    const xp = this.xpLoad();
    const label = this.labelKeyOf(this.state) || 'default';
    const first = !xp.firstSeenAt;
    const newSession = this.xpNewSession();
    this.xpWrite(newSession
      ? { firstSeenAt: xp.firstSeenAt || new Date().toISOString(), sessionCount: (xp.sessionCount || 0) + 1 }
      : { firstSeenAt: xp.firstSeenAt || new Date().toISOString() });
    const caps = this.caps();
    const canHover = caps.hover && caps.finePointer;
    const patch = {};
    const storedView = (xp.viewMode && xp.viewMode[label]) || null;
    // ⚠ DOOR 8, AND THE CENSUS IS ONLY CLOSED NOW. A returning visitor whose
    // stored viewMode is 'map' had that preference restored on a phone — a door
    // no link and no tap goes through, since it opens by itself on load.
    //
    // The preference is KEPT rather than rewritten: the phone declines to act
    // on it, and the same visitor on a desktop still gets their map. No note —
    // nothing was clicked, so there is no link to explain.
    if (storedView === 'map' || storedView === 'grid') {
      patch.route = (storedView === 'map' && !this.phoneNoMap()) ? 'star' : this.state.route;
    }
    if (!canHover) patch.hoverAudio = false;
    else if (xp.audioMode === 'hover' || xp.audioMode === 'player') patch.hoverAudio = xp.audioMode === 'hover';
    if (Object.keys(patch).length) this.setState(patch);
    // The orientation runs once per browser, and only where there is something
    // to point at. ~900ms: a callout already there when the page paints reads
    // as part of the page — the visitor has to be looking at the site before
    // being told anything about it.
    // ⚠ AND newSession GATES THE ORIENTATION TOO. Without it a remount mid-visit
    // could re-arm the 900ms timer on a browser whose first visit this still is.
    if (first && newSession && !this.xpDone(this.GUIDE_INTRO_ID) && !this._narrowNow()) {
      this._guideIntroT = setTimeout(() => { this._guideIntroT = null; this.guideIntroStart(false); }, 900);
    }
  }
  // Called by the two toggles. Records the choice so the next visit can honour
  // it, and yields any teaching that was describing the control just used.
  guideModeChosen(kind, value) {
    const xp = this.xpLoad();
    if (kind === 'audio') this.xpWrite({ audioMode: value });
    else {
      const label = this.labelKeyOf(this.state) || 'default';
      const vm = Object.assign({}, xp.viewMode || {}); vm[label] = value;
      this.xpWrite({ viewMode: vm });
    }
    this.emit(kind === 'audio' ? 'audio_mode_changed' : 'view_mode_changed',
              { action: kind === 'audio' ? 'audio_mode_changed' : 'view_mode_changed', value: value });
    this.guideYield();
    if (kind === 'audio' && value === 'player') this.xpMark('player.opened');
    if (kind === 'view' && value === 'map') this.xpMark('map.opened');
  }
  // The same width test the chrome uses, read from the same place, so a lesson
  // can never appear pointing at a control the breakpoint has removed.
  _narrowNow() {
    const st = this.state;
    // ⚠ PROBE_VPS TOO, AND renderVals ALREADY DID (2026-09-14 audit).
    // renderVals resolves its viewport as
    //   this.VPS[st.vp] || this.PROBE_VPS[st.vp - this.VPS.length]
    // and this read only VPS — so at any PROBE index it fell through to the
    // 1180 default and answered "not narrow". The probe set is 699×699,
    // 699×700, 700×699, 700×700 and up: it exists SPECIFICALLY to test the
    // 700px boundary this method defines. So every _narrowNow()-gated
    // behaviour — hoverArmed, phoneNoMap, playMode and playCtl through it, the
    // guide's sheet deferral, all six map doors — was untestable at exactly
    // the widths built to test it, and answered for a desktop at 699px.
    //
    // 13 callers read this. One resolver, shared with the render, so the
    // methods and the styles can never disagree about what a phone is.
    const vp = this.VPS[st.vp] || this.PROBE_VPS[st.vp - this.VPS.length];
    const w = st.winW || (vp && vp[0]) || 1180;
    return w < 700;
  }
  // ══ TOUCH IS THE PHONE'S HOVER (2026-09-10, Tom) ═════════════════════════
  // ⚠⚠ INERT SINCE 2026-09-14, AND KEPT DELIBERATELY. hoverArmed() returns
  // false on any narrow viewport now the phone is grid-and-player only, and
  // every handler below self-gates on it — so this bridge dispatches nothing
  // on a phone today. It is not dead code by accident: it is the whole of what
  // brings finger-hover back if the map returns to the phone. Read the rest of
  // this note as the design it is, not as behaviour currently shipping.
  // "We want hover to work on mobile. For album covers, putting your finger on
  // the album cover plays the track. It's simple: it's a hover. For stars, you
  // can do a short press to listen quickly and a longer press to lock it."
  //
  // ⚠ THIS METHOD WAS CITED BEFORE IT WAS WRITTEN. hoverArmed()'s narrow block
  // and the menu's dead Hover side were both removed on the strength of it and
  // it did not exist, so for one drop a phone visitor arrived in hover mode
  // with no gesture that could make a sound — the exact fault the deleted block
  // had documented — while a note underneath taught the missing gesture. The
  // lesson is narrow and worth stating: a comment naming a method is not the
  // method.
  //
  // ONE DOM BRIDGE, NOT NINE BUILDERS. React delegates onMouseEnter/onMouseLeave
  // from mouseover/mouseout, so dispatching those at the touched element drives
  // every hover surface the file already has — covers, tracklist rows, curator
  // faces, stars — through their own handlers. Nothing per-surface to add, and
  // no second definition of what a hover means.
  //
  //   FINGER DOWN   mouseover  → the preview starts, exactly as a cursor would
  //   LIFT, SHORT   mouseout   → it stops. A star also stays unlocked.
  //   LIFT, LONG    click      → the star locks; nothing navigates
  //
  // The threshold is the tooltip's own 450ms, so "settles after you stop" and
  // "a hold" are one number in this file rather than two.
  _touchLongMs() { return 450; }
  touchHoverBridge() {
    // ⚠ THE SHELL, NOT THE DOCUMENT (2026-09-10, engineering). this._root is
    // never assigned in this file — stageRef writes this._stage — so the
    // `|| document` fallback was the only path that ever ran, and four
    // listeners landed on document alongside six host touch modules.
    const root = this._stage || document;
    let node = null, t0 = 0, moved = false;
    // The star test is gone with the synthetic click it gated — see _touchEnd.
    // Nothing here needs to know whether the finger is on a star any more: a
    // hover is a hover on every surface.
    const fire = (el, type) => {
      if (!el || !el.dispatchEvent) return;
      el.dispatchEvent(new MouseEvent(type, { bubbles: true, cancelable: true, view: window }));
    };
    this._touchStart = (e) => {
      // Inert in Player mode: there the play controls exist and a tap is a tap.
      if (!this.hoverArmed(this.state)) return;
      const t = e.touches && e.touches[0];
      if (!t) return;
      node = document.elementFromPoint(t.clientX, t.clientY);
      if (!node) return;
      t0 = Date.now(); moved = false;
      fire(node, 'mouseover');
    };
    // A scroll is not a press. Any real travel cancels the preview and the
    // gesture, so swiping the deck or the catalogue does not leave audio behind.
    this._touchMove = () => {
      if (!node || moved) return;
      moved = true; fire(node, 'mouseout'); node = null;
    };
    this._touchEnd = (e) => {
      if (!node) return;
      const held = Date.now() - t0, el = node;
      node = null;
      // ⚠ THE STAR LOCK IS THE HOST'S, AND THIS NO LONGER DISPATCHES A CLICK
      // (2026-09-10, engineering — right on all three counts).
      //
      //   · The "a star, not the sky" guard could not hold: a
      //     <div data-star-field-mount> inside the field means elementFromPoint
      //     never returns the field itself, so EVERY press over the threshold —
      //     empty sky included — dispatched a synthetic click.
      //   · A synthetic MouseEvent carries no coordinates, and their picker
      //     reads clientX/clientY, so it resolved a star at the canvas origin
      //     rather than under the finger.
      //   · Their own long-press-to-lock already exists at 500ms, so two
      //     gestures competed for one press.
      //
      // And star-field behaviour is the host's by Tom's 2026-08-27 ruling. So
      // the bridge does ONE thing on every surface: it turns a finger into a
      // hover. Locking a star, and the coordinates that needs, belong to
      // whoever owns the picker; the gesture spec is in the handover.
      //
      // A long press still swallows the click so hold-to-hear never navigates.
      // That is true of a cover and a star alike, and is not star behaviour.
      if (held >= this._touchLongMs() && e && e.cancelable) e.preventDefault();
      fire(el, 'mouseout');
    };
    root.addEventListener('touchstart', this._touchStart, { passive: true });
    root.addEventListener('touchmove', this._touchMove, { passive: true });
    root.addEventListener('touchend', this._touchEnd, { passive: false });
    root.addEventListener('touchcancel', this._touchMove, { passive: true });
    this._touchRoot = root;
  }
  componentDidMount() {
    // Where the cursor is, so muteHoverOpen can tell "the layout moved under a
    // still pointer" from "the visitor moved". Passive and write-only — it sets
    // an instance field and never triggers a render, so it costs nothing per
    // event.
    if (typeof window !== 'undefined') {
      this._ptrTrack = (e) => { this._ptr = { x: e.clientX, y: e.clientY }; };
      window.addEventListener('mousemove', this._ptrTrack, { passive: true });
    // Touch is the phone's hover — registered beside the other channels, and
    // INERT since 2026-09-14: every handler inside self-gates on hoverArmed(),
    // which is false on a phone. Registered rather than skipped so the bridge
    // has exactly one lifecycle, whatever the mode does later.
    this.touchHoverBridge();
    }
    // ⚠ THE FIRST MEASUREMENT CAN BE A FALLBACK FONT. Archivo arrives by link,
