    // so measureText before it loads returns Helvetica's advances and the ladder
    // picks a tier from the wrong widths. One cache clear and one re-render when
    // the font is ready fixes it; without this the header could settle a step
    // too generous or too mean and stay there for the session.
    if (typeof document !== 'undefined' && document.fonts && document.fonts.ready) {
      document.fonts.ready.then(() => {
        if (this._gone) return;
        this._twCache = {};
        this.forceUpdate();
      }).catch(() => {});
    }
    // ── §1e · OPEN THE INBOUND CHANNEL (2026-09-07) ────────────────────
    // Registered FIRST, before the hand-over signal below, so the moment you
    // are told the design is up the channel is already there — a popstate that
    // lands in the same tick as the ready flag has somewhere to go.
    // See goToNav() for the contract and for the answer to your question.
    if (typeof window !== 'undefined') {
      this._dcGo = (t) => this.goToNav(t);
      window.__DC_GO = this._dcGo;
      this._dcGoEvent = (e) => this.goToNav(e && e.detail);
      window.addEventListener('dc:go', this._dcGoEvent);
      // ── §0-3 · THE STARS CHANNEL (2026-09-11, engineering Q1 option 1) ────
      // "We publish __DC_STARS when the field settles and dispatch something
      // like dc:stars; field() clears its 'f' + h cache and rebuilds."
      // Adopted verbatim, and registered here so it has the same three entry
      // points as the other two: a global function, an event, and the plain
      // presence of the data.
      //
      // Fire it AFTER assigning window.__DC_STARS. Detail is ignored — the
      // event says "ask again", the global says what the answer is, which
      // keeps one source of truth for the seats.
      //
      // Drops every f<height> cache rather than one: the same component can
      // have built a field at more than one height across a resize, and a
      // stale sibling would resurface on the next rotation.
      this._starsChanged = () => {
        Object.keys(this).forEach((k) => { if (/^f\d+$/.test(k)) delete this[k]; });
        this.forceUpdate();
      };
      window.__DC_STARS_READY = this._starsChanged;
      window.addEventListener('dc:stars', this._starsChanged);
      // §0-2 · the playback channel, registered beside the nav one and with the
      // same three entry points, so whichever shape their host already uses
      // works. See playbackSet for why this exists rather than them calling
      // setState on the instance. Ported from V1 with playbackSet itself.
      this._dcPlay = (p) => this.playbackSet(p);
      window.__DC_PLAYBACK = this._dcPlay;
      this._dcPlayEvent = (e) => this.playbackSet(e && e.detail);
      window.addEventListener('dc:playback', this._dcPlayEvent);
      // The THIRD entry point, promised in two comments and never assigned until
      // 2026-09-09 (code audit part 7). __DC_GO has had its element-scoped form
      // all along; this is the same shape for playback, so a host that avoids
      // window has the same three doors for both.
      try { const nEl = this._root && this._root.current; if (nEl) nEl.__dcPlayback = this._dcPlay; } catch (e) { /* optional */ }
      // Up/down flip the column's card deck (2026-09-09, Tom: "you should be
      // able to flip through the cards with the scroll wheel or the up and down
      // arrow"). Guarded inside athColKey by view and route, so the keys mean
      // nothing anywhere else in the site.
      window.addEventListener('keydown', this.athColKey);
      // The article slug arrival() could not resolve, resolved now that data()
      // can read JOURNAL. Unmatched → the journal index, the same honest
      // fallback arrival() applies to a dead link.
      if (this.state.artSlug) {
        const list = this.data('JOURNAL') || [];
        const hit = list.map((j, ji) => ({ slug: j[0], ji: ji })).filter(x => x.slug === this.state.artSlug)[0];
        this.setState(hit ? { article: hit.ji, artSlug: null } : { route: 'ath-journal', artSlug: null });
      }
      // The scoped form, for a host that would rather not read window.
      try { const n = this._root && this._root.current; if (n) n.__dcGo = this._dcGo; } catch (e) { /* optional */ }
    }
    // ── THE HAND-OVER SIGNAL (2026-09-06, Tom: "it shows you the code on
    // screen and then blinks into the design") ────────────────────────────────
    // ⚠ THE BLINK IS NOT THIS FILE RENDERING. What flashes is the host's
    // server-rendered crawl layer — the plain-text document that gives crawlers
    // and JS-off visitors real links (their own §7). It is deliberately painted
    // FIRST and is supposed to fade once the design mounts. "Unformatted text,
    // sometimes more than just this page" is that document exactly: it carries
    // the whole route's copy, which is why it can be longer than the page.
    //
    // Their layer used to wait two animation frames and now waits a deadline —
    // both are guesses about when the design is up. This is the fact instead:
    // the runtime sets data-dc-ready on <html> and fires 'dc:ready' the moment
    // the first real render has committed. Hand over on that and there is no
    // window in which both documents are visible.
    //
    // Written on the element rather than a global so their layer can style
    // against it — `html[data-dc-ready] .crawl-layer { display: none }` needs no
    // JS at all and cannot race.
    if (typeof document !== 'undefined' && document.documentElement) {
      const el = document.documentElement;
      if (!el.hasAttribute('data-dc-ready')) {
        el.setAttribute('data-dc-ready', '1');
        try { window.dispatchEvent(new CustomEvent('dc:ready', { detail: { route: this.state.route } })); } catch (e) {}
      }
    }
    this.loadDataPackage();
    // THE FORM'S ACK CHANNEL (2026-09-04, request A1). The host calls
    // window.__DC_FORM_ACK(true) on a delivered enquiry, (false) on a failure,
    // and the Send control reports whichever it is told. Nothing claims
    // success on the strength of having emitted.
    if (typeof window !== 'undefined') {
      window.__DC_FORM_ACK = (ok) => {
        this.setState({ clsSending: false, clsSent: !!ok, clsFail: !ok });
        // ── A SENT FORM CLOSES AND RESETS (2026-09-06, Tom) ─────────────────
        // "It sort of makes you feel like you need to cook it more." Right: a
        // form still sitting there with your words in it reads as unfinished,
        // whatever the label above it says. So on a DELIVERED send the panel
        // closes and the fields empty after a beat.
        //
        // 1.4s, because the confirmation has to be read before the thing it is
        // confirming disappears — closing instantly would leave a visitor
        // unsure anything happened, which is the same fault in the other
        // direction.
        //
        // The fields are cleared as it closes, so re-opening gives you a clean
        // form and a second enquiry is possible — which is what makes "you
        // could send it twice if you wanted to change your thing" true.
        // clsSent stays set, so the control still reads "Sent — we will be in
        // touch" until the visitor opens the form again.
        //
        // ⚠ ONLY ON SUCCESS. A failed send keeps the panel open and every field
        // exactly as typed (r25's rule) — clearing on failure would throw away
        // the visitor's words at the worst possible moment.
        if (ok) {
          setTimeout(() => {
            this.setState({ clsFormOpen: false, clsFields: {}, clsSvc: {}, clsPick: {} });
          }, 1400);
        }
      };
    }
    setTimeout(() => this.fitPanels(), 60);
    // The guide reads storage and counts the session here — after mount, never
    // in a field initialiser. guideArrive also applies the restored modes.
    this._mounted = true;
    this.guideArrive();
    this._fit = () => {
      const site = !!(this.props.site || window.__DC_SITE);
      const cw = document.documentElement.clientWidth || 1180;
      const ch = document.documentElement.clientHeight || 760;
      if (site) {
        // The real window, whole. The 48 below is the tool canvas's 24px of air
        // on each side of the artboard — a presentation inset, not a margin the
        // site has.
        if (cw !== this.state.winW || ch !== this.state.winH) this.setState({ winW: cw, winH: ch });
        return;
      }
      const w = Math.max(320, cw - 48);
      if (w !== this.state.avail) this.setState({ avail: w });
    };
    this._fit();
    window.addEventListener('resize', this._fit);
    // The host reaches the bridge here. Set on mount so a field mounting later
    // can find it, and pushed once immediately so it starts in step.
    window.__DC_ATLAS = this;
    this._pushStarState();
    this.setState({ links: this.loadLinks() });
  }

  componentWillUnmount() {
    window.removeEventListener('resize', this._fit);
    window.removeEventListener('dc:stars', this._starsChanged);
    // The touch bridge, torn down with the rest.
    if (this._touchRoot) {
      this._touchRoot.removeEventListener('touchstart', this._touchStart);
      this._touchRoot.removeEventListener('touchmove', this._touchMove);
      this._touchRoot.removeEventListener('touchend', this._touchEnd);
      this._touchRoot.removeEventListener('touchcancel', this._touchMove);
      this._touchRoot = null;
    }
    // The column deck's arrow keys, torn down with the rest (2026-09-09).
    window.removeEventListener('keydown', this.athColKey);
    this._mounted = false;
    if (this._guideT) clearTimeout(this._guideT);
    if (this._guideIntroT) clearTimeout(this._guideIntroT);
    (this._guideSteps || []).forEach(clearTimeout);
    if (this._closeT) clearTimeout(this._closeT);
    // The Audio button's hover-intent timer, torn down with the rest — an
    // outstanding setTimeout that calls setState after unmount is the one thing
    // this file's own teardown rule exists to prevent.
    if (this._audioT) clearTimeout(this._audioT);
    // V2 · the hover mute's listener AND its backstop timer — _unmute drops both,
    // which is why it is one call rather than two here.
    this._unmute();
    if (this._holdT) { clearTimeout(this._holdT); this._holdT = null; }
    if (this._ptrTrack) { window.removeEventListener('mousemove', this._ptrTrack); this._ptrTrack = null; }
    // document.fonts.ready is a promise and cannot be cancelled, so the flag is
    // how its callback knows not to forceUpdate a component that has gone.
    this._gone = true;
    // §1e · hand the inbound channel back rather than leaving a dead function
    // on window pointing at an unmounted component.
    if (typeof window !== 'undefined') {
      if (window.__DC_GO === this._dcGo) { try { delete window.__DC_GO; } catch (e) { window.__DC_GO = undefined; } }
      if (window.__DC_PLAYBACK === this._dcPlay) { try { delete window.__DC_PLAYBACK; } catch (e) { window.__DC_PLAYBACK = undefined; } }
      if (this._dcPlayEvent) window.removeEventListener('dc:playback', this._dcPlayEvent);
      if (this._dcGoEvent) window.removeEventListener('dc:go', this._dcGoEvent);
    }
  }

  // Hover-owned panels: mouse on opens, mouse off closes. The short grace
  // period covers the gap between the header button and the panel below it —
  // without it, crossing that 18px of dead air would close the menu.
  // Closing NEVER touches browseCat / browseMode / picks / services, so the
  // panel reopens exactly where you left it.
  // ONE PANEL AT A TIME, and one list that says which panels exist. Every
  // opener closes every other panel by reading PANELS — so adding a panel means
  // adding its key here and nowhere else.
  //
  // This used to be a hand-written sibling list repeated in panelEnter and in
  // each toggle handler, and every one of those copies omitted 'follow' when the
  // Follow Us panel was added — so Follow could sit open alongside Explore,
  // Browse or the cart. That is the class of bug a duplicated list always
  // produces eventually; the list is now declared once.
  // 'view' joins the exclusive set (2026-09-08): the View dropdown must close
  // Browse and Audio the way they close each other, or two panels overlap in the
  // same corner of the header.
  // ⚠ 'view2', NOT 'view' — AND I MADE THIS MISTAKE ON THE FIRST PASS. st.view
  // is this file's RECORD view ('releases' | 'album' | 'results' …), read in
  // dozens of places including _navTuple and goToNav. closeAllPanels writes
  // EVERY key in this list to a boolean, so listing 'view' meant that opening any
  // panel — Browse, Cart, anything — set st.view = false and took the star
  // field's own view with it. Driven: the View button would not open, because the
  // handler read st.view2 while the list wrote st.view.
  PANELS = ['menuOpen', 'browseOpen', 'explore', 'cart', 'queue', 'follow', 'audio', 'view2'];

  closeAllPanels(except) {
    const patch = {};
    this.PANELS.forEach(k => { patch[k] = (k === except); });
    return patch;
  }

  panelEnter(key) {
    // ── A PANEL DOES NOT OPEN BECAUSE THE LAYOUT MOVED (2026-09-09, Tom) ────
    // "When I switch between grid and map view, the header buttons move over to
    // the left and back to the right, and my mouse floats over a bunch of
    // mouseover menus… while it's switching, the mouseovers are disabled."
    //
    // Exactly right, and the cause is the drawer: `gutter` is drawerW + 16 on an
    // open star map and 24 everywhere else, so switching view slides the whole
    // right-hand run ~372px sideways. Under a stationary cursor that sweeps
    // Browse, the cart and Explore THROUGH the pointer, and each one's
    // mouseenter fires — a panel opening because the page moved, not because
    // anyone pointed at it.
    //
    // GUARDED HERE because this is the one funnel: hoverBrowse, hoverCart and
    // hoverExplore all call it, so one check covers all three and any future
    // one. A CLICK is untouched — every toggle sets state directly and never
    // routes through panelEnter, which is the distinction that matters: a click
    // is intent already stated, a hover during a slide is not intent at all.
    if (this.hoverBlocked()) return;
    if (this._closeT) { clearTimeout(this._closeT); this._closeT = null; }
    if (!this.state[key]) this.setState(this.closeAllPanels(key));
  }
  // ── THE MUTE · UNTIL THE CURSOR ITSELF MOVES ──────────────────────────────
  // Not a fixed delay. Tom's own condition is "when I move my mouse again back
  // over the menus, they work", and that is the honest test: the fault is the
  // LAYOUT moving under a still pointer, so the cure is to wait for the pointer
  // to move. Distance, not a timer — 4px, enough to ignore a tremor and less
  // than any deliberate movement.
  //
  // The timeout is only a backstop, at the transition's own 280ms plus a frame
  // or two. Without it a visitor who never moves the mouse again would leave
  // hover-open dead for the rest of the session; with it, the mute can never
  // outlive the slide that caused it.
  //
  // ⚠ THE LISTENER IS ON window, NOT THE STAGE, and it must be: the pointer can
  // be over the drawer or the chrome when the switch happens, and the events
  // that prove it moved would never reach a stage-scoped handler.
  muteHoverOpen() {
    if (typeof window === 'undefined') return;
    // ⚠ CLEAR THE OLD MUTE BEFORE SETTING THE NEW FLAG, not after. _unmute()
    // drops the flag as well as the listener and the timer, so calling it second
    // switched the mute straight back off — every panel still opened mid-slide
    // and the flag read false the moment it was set. Two rapid view switches are
    // exactly why the prior teardown is needed at all, so the call stays; only
    // its order was wrong.
    const from = this._ptr || null;
    this._unmute();
    this._hoverMute = true;
    this._muteMove = (e) => {
      if (from && Math.abs(e.clientX - from.x) < 4 && Math.abs(e.clientY - from.y) < 4) return;
      this._unmute();
    };
    window.addEventListener('mousemove', this._muteMove, { passive: true });
    this._muteT = setTimeout(() => { this._muteT = null; this._unmute(); }, 340);
  }
  // ── AND A HOLD THAT SURVIVES MOVEMENT (2026-09-09, Tom) ───────────────────
  // "When using the hamburger menu in map mode, we need to allow people to get
  // to the sidebar to scroll. Pause all mouseover states for 2 seconds right
  // after somebody clicks the hamburger menu and clicks something."
  //
  // ⚠ muteHoverOpen IS THE WRONG TOOL HERE and the difference is the whole
  // point. It unmutes on the first real pointer movement, because it exists for
  // a layout that slid under a STILL cursor. This case is the opposite: the
  // visitor is about to move, deliberately, all the way across the field to the
  // column — and every star they cross would arm a preview and every panel they
  // clip would open. Cancelling on movement would cancel it immediately.
  //
  // So this one is time only. Two seconds, Tom's number, and nothing shortens
  // it: a hold that ends early is the same as no hold on the trip it exists to
  // protect.
  //
  // IT BLOCKS HOVER STATES TOO, not just panel opens — "pause all mouseover
  // states". The path from the hamburger to the column crosses the star field,
  // so the previews are the bigger half of what was firing.
  holdHover(ms) {
    if (typeof window === 'undefined') return;
    if (this._holdT) clearTimeout(this._holdT);
    this._hoverHold = true;
    this._holdT = setTimeout(() => { this._holdT = null; this._hoverHold = false; this.forceUpdate(); }, ms || 2000);
  }
  // The one predicate both mutes answer to, so a new hover surface has one thing
  // to ask rather than two flags to remember.
  // ── THE STAR FIELD'S BOTTOM INSET · ONE AUTHORITY (2026-09-09, Tom) ───────
  // "The representative star map is not filling the total map view. You moved
  // the brackets down, which is great, but the star field should be bigger too,
  // vertically."
  //
  // Correct, and the box grew while the CONTENT did not. Two numbers described
  // the same edge and only one of them moved:
  //
  //   fieldStyle.bottom   hasTransportNow ? 90 : 24   ← changed with the mode
  //   fieldH              H - 168                     ← 78 top + 90 tail, frozen
  //
  // So the dots were laid out in 592px of an available 658 and stopped 66px
  // short of the brackets. field(h) multiplies each dot's normalised y by h, so
  // the array itself was right for a shorter field — the geometry was stale, not
  // the data.
  //
  // ⚠ WHY A METHOD AND NOT A LOCAL. fieldH is computed at the top of
  // renderVals, before `route` and `hasTransportNow` exist, so it could not read
  // either — which is exactly how the two drifted. This takes only `st`, so both
  // readers can call it wherever they sit, and there is one place to change.
  //
  // The rule is hasTransportNow's, narrowed to the surfaces that draw a field:
  // hover mode never shows a bar, and the ATH deck never does in either mode
  // (Tom, 2026-09-03), so both give the field its space back.
  //
  // ⚠ FOR THE BUILD: nothing about __DC_STARS changes. Star y values are
  // normalised 0..1 and multiplied by this height, so a taller field re-spreads
  // the same rows — the host supplies no pixels and needs no new field. The
  // cache key is 'f' + height, so a mode switch builds a second array rather
  // than reusing the short one.
  // ── IS THIS CURATOR-MAP CIRCLE THE ONE SOUNDING? (2026-09-09) ─────────────
  // Both seat builders ask it, so it is one method rather than two copies of the
  // same expression — the drift path and the structured path have drifted before
  // (see the tapPad note, "the second call site"), and this is the exact shape
  // that lets them.
  //
  // A circle is sounding when the transport's record is this circle's record AND
  // playback is not paused. It reads recFor(name), the same resolver the press
  // uses, so the button that started a track is the button that can stop it.
  // ⚠ THE SEAT IS THE IDENTITY, NOT THE RECORD (2026-09-09, second pass).
  // This compared npPlay to recFor(name), and recFor's ATH fallback matches
  // loosely — `p[1] === d[2] || p[0] === d[1] || String(p[0]).indexOf(name) > -1`
  // — so distinct curators on the same drop collapse onto one PAIRS index.
  // Three collisions among the nine playable seats: rec 1 is Clarice Jensen AND
  // Grey Mcmurray, rec 3 Balmorhea and Takeshi Nishimoto, rec 5 marine eyes and
  // Lauren Helene Green. Pressing one lit two faces, and Tom's spec is singular:
  // "the play button that appeared turns to pause".
  //
  // So the pressed SEAT is recorded and compared. Both builders already compute
  // a unique key — name|kind|vi|i and name|mode|vi|gi2|di — which is the only
  // value on this surface that identifies one circle.
  //
  // ⚠ THE KEY IS PASSED IN, not closed over: this is a class method and `key` is
  // a local in each builder. Same shape as modeSide(on, snug) and starTailPx(st),
  // for the same reason — a method reaching for a builder's local is the fault
  // that blanked this page once already.
  //
  // A SEAT THAT IS PLAYING BUT IS NOT WHAT THE TRANSPORT HOLDS IS NOT PLAYING:
  // npPlay is checked too, so a press anywhere else — a cover, a track row, the
  // drawer — clears this seat's pause without needing its own teardown. That is
  // the one condition that cannot go stale, because it is the same value every
  // other play surface writes.
  mapSeatOn(st, seatKey, name) {
    const s = st || this.state;
    if (!s.started || s.paused) return false;
    if (!seatKey || s.mapSeat !== seatKey) return false;
    const rec = this.recFor(name);
    return rec != null && s.npPlay === rec;
  }
  // ── THE BAR NEEDS SOMETHING TO BE ABOUT (2026-09-09) ─────────────────────
  // hasTransportNow decided the bar renders from the ROUTE alone — radio,
  // record, tour and news are not in its started-gated list, so they got `true`
  // unconditionally. While npRec fell back to record 0 that was survivable
  // (wrongly): the bar always had a subject because one was manufactured.
  // With npRec resolving null the empty path became reachable and the bar
  // painted a blank title over "artist · title from catalogue" — a designer's
  // placeholder, visitor-facing on four routes with zero interaction. Same class
  // as the —:— timestamp r54 removed: a field occupying its place and saying
  // nothing.
  //
  // So the bar is gated on having a subject, which is also what the file already
  // says about a resting state — "arriving at an album grid shows NO player…
  // the bar appears on exactly two acts". This extends that from the grid to
  // every route rather than inventing a rule.
  //
  // ⚠ FROM `st` ALONE, deliberately: npPair is computed far below
  // hasTransportNow and reading it there was impossible. These are the four
  // inputs npPair and extPlay derive from, so the answer cannot drift from what
  // the bar would have displayed.
  // ── CHOOSING PLAYER IS ITSELF A SUBJECT (2026-09-10, Tom) ────────────────
  // "Switching between hover and player mode, the transport isn't coming up
  // when I hit player, which is confusing. It should be really obvious: oh,
  // now there's a player."
  //
  // The bar was earned by AUDIO — something had to be sounding — so choosing
  // Player changed nothing visible and the mode read as a setting that had not
  // taken. But the transport IS the mode: it is the only thing on the page that
  // says which of the two you are in, and hover mode's own answer is that it
  // has no bar. A mode you cannot see is a mode you have to test.
  //
  // ⚠ AN EXPLICIT CHOICE, NOT THE DEFAULT. st.hoverAudio is null until the
  // visitor picks, and null resolves to hover — so a strict false is only ever
  // "this person pressed Player". A first arrival still gets no bar, because it
  // has not been asked for.
  //
  // ⚠ AND THE TERM GOES HERE, IN hasSubject, BECAUSE ITS THREE READERS ARE THE
  // BAR-PRESENCE TRIO — hasTransportNow, barShown and starTailPx — and this
  // file's own note on them says they must share every term or the layout
  // reserves for a bar that is not there. Adding it to one would have brought
  // the bar up over a pane that had not made room for it. There are no other
  // callers; I checked before widening it.
  playerChosen(st) { return (st || this.state).hoverAudio === false; }
  hasSubject(st) {
    const s = st || this.state;
    if (this.playerChosen(s)) return true;
    return s.npPlay != null || s.lock != null || !!s.extPlay || s.hoverStar != null;
  }
  starTailPx(st) {
    const s = st || this.state;
    const r = s.route;
    if (!this.playMode(s)) return 24;
    // ⚠ AND NO SUBJECT MEANS NO RESERVE (2026-09-09, third term). Player mode at
    // rest kept a 90px tail for a bar hasTransportNow had already removed, so
    // the field stopped 90px short of the stage with its brackets stranded 6px
    // outside a tail with nothing in it — the exact fault fixed for hover mode
    // two rounds ago ("the box grew and the content didn't"), reintroduced at
    // the other end by giving the bar a subject test this did not have.
    //
    // The three answers to "is a bar showing" — hasTransportNow, barShown and
    // this — must share every term or the layout reserves for a bar that is not
    // there. hasSubject is the term they were missing.
    if (!this.hasSubject(s)) return 24;
    if (r === 'ath-map' || r === 'ath-deck') return 24;
    return 90;
  }
  hoverBlocked() { return !!(this._hoverMute || this._hoverHold); }
  // Every hover-IN goes through here, so the hold has one door to close rather
  // than nine handlers to remember. Hover-OUT is deliberately NOT guarded: a
  // leave must always land, or a row lit before the hold began would stay lit
  // for the whole two seconds.
  // A hover that RESOLVED A RECORD is the only one that counts: the tip says
  // "move across releases to sample them instantly", so it must not fire on a
  // hover that made no sound. hoverEnter is already the intent gate (it is what
  // separates a crossing from a look), so the mark rides it rather than adding
  // a second threshold that could disagree with the first.
  hoverEnter(patch, then) {
    if (this.hoverArmed(this.state) && patch && (patch.catHover != null || patch.hoverStar != null)) this.xpMark('audio.hoverHeard');
    if (this.hoverBlocked()) return;
    this.setState(patch, then);
  }
  _unmute() {
    if (this._muteMove && typeof window !== 'undefined') {
      window.removeEventListener('mousemove', this._muteMove);
      this._muteMove = null;
    }
    if (this._muteT) { clearTimeout(this._muteT); this._muteT = null; }
    this._hoverMute = false;
  }

  panelLeave(key) {
    if (this._closeT) clearTimeout(this._closeT);
    this._closeT = setTimeout(() => {
      this._closeT = null;
      const patch = {};
      patch[key] = false;
      this.setState(patch);
    }, 220);
  }

  panelHold() { if (this._closeT) { clearTimeout(this._closeT); this._closeT = null; } }

  // PHONE REFERENCE — corrected 2026-08-31. THIS FILE IS THE PHONE DESIGN.
  // The narrow (W < 700) and tight (narrow && H < 700) branches throughout this
  // logic class are the build instructions, and the eight presets below are the
  // sizes they are drawn against.
  //
  // Frames 14a–14g and 9a were the phone reference BEFORE this file carried full
  // phone pages. They are cited in prose here and in SPEC-mobile.md, but they are
  // not in any handback bundle and should not be treated as outstanding: where a
  // 14-series citation and this file disagree, THIS FILE WINS. Earlier wording
  // said the reverse ("the presets are a RESPONSIVE SANITY CHECK, not a drawing
  // of the phone") — that is superseded and was the source of a build blocker.
  //
  // Two things the older comment got right and that still hold: desktop chrome
  // crushed into 390 is a lie, and the phone star view is a multi-detent bottom
  // sheet (Mobile2026Root, flag MOBILE_2026_ROOT_ENABLED) rather than a 340px
  // side drawer — so nothing in these presets is the phone star view.
  // ══ FRAME NUMBERS IN THIS FILE ARE HISTORY, NEVER AUTHORITY ══════════════
  // Comments below still mention 8a and the 15-series (15b, 15c, 15d, 15e,
  // 15f, 15g, 15h, 15n). Every one of those is a frame of a RETIRED suite:
  // they are in no manifest, most are not on disk, and NOTHING should be built
  // from them. They survive only as notes on where a value originally came
  // from, the way a commit message survives.
  //
  // This mattered materially: the build's ATH components cite 15b/15f/15g/15h
  // as their stated source, because this file cited them first. That is how
  // /catalogue/across-the-horizon came to be built to 15e2 and the artists
  // index to 15f — both wrong, both reported to me as defects while my own
  // captions told the build it was right.
  //
  // THE RULE: this file's renderVals() is the only authority in it. A frame
  // number in a comment is provenance. If a comment and the code disagree, the
  // CODE is the design — and tell me, because the comment is a bug.
  // ══ THE DESKTOP PRESETS WERE NOT REAL MACHINES (2026-09-09, Tom) ═════════
  // "The 13-inch view is so much tighter, especially in the header, than the
  // 16-inch view… on my screen there's literally a 4-inch difference in the
  // header, not 3. Designing the top menu is almost impossible."
  //
  // TWO FAULTS, AND NEITHER IS A SCALE PROBLEM.
  //
  // 1 · 1180 IS NOT A 13-INCH LAPTOP. Real logical widths: 13" Air 1470, 14"
  //     Pro 1512, 16" Pro 1728. The 16" preset was exact — whoever chose 1728
  //     used real points — and the 13" one was 290px short of the nearest real
  //     machine. So the header has been designed against a width no laptop
  //     has, which is why it felt impossible: it was.
  //
  // 2 · THE TWO PRESETS SAT ON OPPOSITE SIDES OF FIVE THRESHOLDS. This file
  //     branches at 1000, 1100, 1300, 1560, 1660, 1700, 1900, 1980 and 2000 —
  //     and NOT at 1180 or 1728. So 1180 landed in the 1100–1300 band, taking
  //     the narrowest desktop arm of nearly every ternary, and 1728 landed
  //     above 1700, taking the widest. Stepping between them flipped four or
  //     five branches at once with NOTHING previewable in between. That is the
  //     "4 inches, not 3" — not a 46% width difference read as scale, but four
  //     simultaneous layout decisions, none of which could be seen happening.
  //
  // SO: real machines, and one preset in every band the file actually branches
  // on. Heights subtract browser chrome at the same rate the 16" preset always
  // did (1117 → 1000, so −117).
  //
  // 1180 STAYS, relabelled honestly. It is a real case — a window nobody
  // maximised — and a dozen measurements in this file cite it by number. It is
  // simply not what a 13-inch laptop gives you.
  //
  // ⚠ NOTHING BREAKS AND SOME THINGS GAIN SLACK. Cards tuned to fit 1180 (the
  // curator map's bands, the ATH deck's 1132×582) still fit a wider window with
  // room to spare; they were over-constrained, not wrong. Check them at 1470
  // before spending the slack.
  //
  // A preview ZOOM would not have helped: zoom changes apparent size, not the
  // CSS width the layout has to work in. Room comes from pixels.
  VPS = [
    [1180, 760, '1180 · narrow window'],
    [1470, 840, '1470 · 13" Air'],
    [1512, 865, '1512 · 14" Pro'],
    [1600, 900, '1600 · desktop'],
    [1728, 1000, '1728 · 16" Pro'],
    [2048, 1180, '2048 · 20"'],
    // Phones, at the height Safari actually leaves.
    [390, 844, '390 · phone'],
    [375, 553, 'SE · 375×553'],
    [375, 632, '13 mini · 375×632'],
    [390, 664, '14/15 · 390×664'],
    [393, 672, '15 Pro · 393×672'],
    [430, 752, 'Pro Max · 430×752'],
    [412, 735, 'Pixel 7 · 412×735'],
    [360, 620, 'S21 · 360×620']
  ];

  // ── PROBE WIDTHS · machine sample points, not a picker ──────────────────
  // WHY THIS IS SEPARATE FROM VPS. VPS is what a HUMAN previews at — three real
  // desktop sizes and eight real phones, each with a label someone chose. A
  // token extractor needs something different: one sample on each side of every
  // width threshold this file declares, so that no branch of a viewport ternary
  // can go unobserved. Those are not viewports anyone wants in a picker (1699,
  // 1899…), so putting them in VPS would corrupt the harness to serve the gate.
  //
  // THE FAILURE THIS EXISTS TO END. drawerStyle.width shipped frozen at the 1180
  // value because extraction sampled ONE viewport and the gap detector reports
  // keys whose observed values VARY — an axis never sampled produces no
  // variation, so it cannot be reported as missing. A clean gap report was
  // evidence about the axes sampled, not about coverage.
  //
  // ⚠ THIS LIST IS DERIVED, NOT CHOSEN — ON BOTH AXES. It is the CROSS PRODUCT
  // of every width threshold and every height threshold this file compares
  // against, each taken at n and n-1:
  //
  //   grep: /\bW\s*(>=|>|<=|<)\s*(\d{3,4})/  →  700 1100 1560 1660 1700 1900 1980 2000
  //   grep: /\bH\s*(>=|>|<=|<)\s*(\d{3,4})/  →  700
  //
  //   …plus the DERIVED crossings declared just above (1349, 1353).
  //   20 widths × 2 heights = 40 entries.
  //
  // ⚠ THE HEIGHT AXIS IS NOT DECORATION, AND v1 OF THIS LIST GOT IT WRONG.
  // The first version sampled only widths and paired each with a height chosen
  // by feel — 760, 800, 900… — every one of them ABOVE 700, i.e. entirely on
  // one side of the only height threshold in the file. `tight` is
  // `narrow && H < 700` (see renderVals) and `VP[0] < 700 && VP[1] < 700` in
  // the phone builder, and there are ~79 `tight ? … : …` ternaries here. Under
  // that list every one of them would have extracted frozen at its non-tight
  // branch, and — by the same argument this list exists to answer — an axis
  // never sampled produces no variation, so the collapse step would have
  // stamped all 79 "viewport-invariant" and emitted one token each. The frozen
  // 380, reproduced on the other axis, inside the change written to end it.
  //
  // So: no value in this array is chosen. If a number here is not a threshold
  // or a threshold minus one, it is a bug.
  //
  // Addressed the same way VPS is: renderVals resolves st.vp across both, so
  // the extractor sets an index and stays a pure observer of this file. It
  // never appends to, patches, or synthesises the drop's own data.
  // ── DERIVED VIEWPORT THRESHOLDS · declared, because they cannot be scanned ──
  // A grep for `W <op> N` finds every LITERAL viewport comparison. It cannot
  // find a threshold expressed on a value DERIVED from W — and one exists:
  //
  //   buildMosaic: colW = o.W - (narrow ? 28 : (o.pad || 48))
  //                n    = narrow ? 2 : (colW > 1300 ? 4 : 3)
  //
  // That is a viewport branch — three columns or four — crossing at a width the
  // literal scan never sees. And it crosses at TWO widths, not one, because the
  // pad differs by caller: 48 on the artist wall, 52 on the ATH artist page.
  // So there is no single number for an extractor to compute; resolving it
  // would mean knowing each call site's pad, which is interpretation rather
  // than observation. The extractor stays an observer and the DROP declares the
  // crossings instead.
  //
  // ⚠ A COMMENT WOULD NOT HAVE BEEN ENOUGH. Prose next to an expression drifts
  // from it — that is DQ-36 and five dead branches. This is an array so the
  // test can read it, and the test asserts BOTH directions:
  //   1. PROBE_VPS brackets every crossing declared here (n and n-1); and
  //   2. every comparison in this file against a local assigned from W or H
  //      HAS an entry here — found by scanning for `const x = …W…` followed by
  //      `x <op> N`. A new derived threshold that nobody declares goes RED.
  // The machine finds that a derived comparison exists; I supply the number.
  // ⚠ THE CONVENTION, AND IT IS SEMANTIC, NOT ARITHMETIC. A declared threshold
  // is ALWAYS THE FIRST VALUE ON THE TRUE SIDE of its comparison, so n-1 is the
  // last value on the false side and the pair straddles by construction. That
  // one sentence covers literals and derived alike:
  //
  //   W >= 1700       first true W = 1700              → probe 1699 / 1700
  //   colW > 1300     first true W = 1300 + pad + 1    → probe 1348 / 1349
  //
  // ⚠ v1 DECLARED 1348 AND 1352 AND BRACKETED NOTHING. Those are 1300 + pad —
  // the LAST width on the 3-column side — so 1347/1348 and 1351/1352 both sat
  // wholly on the false side and the four-column branch was never entered by
  // any declared probe. It was still exercised, but only because 1559 and above
  // happen to clear it: coverage by accident, which is the exact objection this
  // round was written to answer. The arithmetic slipped because `>=` and `>`
  // were treated as the same shape. Stating the rule semantically is the fix —
  // "first true value" is unambiguous where "the threshold" is not.
  DERIVED_VP_THRESHOLDS = [
    [1349, 'buildMosaic colW > 1300, pad 48 (artist wall) — first W giving 4 columns'],
    [1353, 'buildMosaic colW > 1300, pad 52 (ATH artist page) — first W giving 4 columns']
  ];

  // Declared and DELIBERATELY NOT PROBED, with the reason, so its absence from
  // PROBE_VPS is a decision on the record rather than an oversight:
  //   fitPanels: floorK = panel.clientHeight < 560 ? 0.8 : 0.86
  // clientHeight is a measured LAYOUT height, not a viewport dimension — it
  // depends on what the card contains, so no choice of viewport reliably puts
  // it either side of 560. Probing viewports cannot cover it; if it needs a
  // gate it needs a different one.
  DERIVED_VP_UNPROBED = [
    [560, 'fitPanels floorK — panel.clientHeight, a layout measure, not a viewport']
  ];

  // ── AXES HELD CONSTANT ON PURPOSE ────────────────────────────────────────
  // DQ-50's rule applied to itself: an axis that is not sampled must be NAMED,
  // because a detector reporting only what varied cannot report what never
  // moved. `vp` was invisible for exactly this reason. Anything held constant
  // during extraction belongs here, with the reason, so that silence is scoped.
  UNSAMPLED_AXES = [
    ['props.drawer16 / drawer20 / cover16 / cover20',
     'AUTHORING RANGE, NOT A SHIPPED STATE. The defaults ARE the design — the ' +
     'drawer is 380 / 440 / 500 and the cover 60 / 72. The sliders exist so I ' +
     'can explore; nothing a visitor can do reaches 560 or 680. Extraction ' +
     'must hold these at their defaults, and a captured 380/440/500 is ' +
     'CORRECT rather than frozen. If I change a default that is a new drop ' +
     'with a new value, not a range the build should sweep.']
  ];

  PROBE_VPS = [
    [699, 699, 'probe · 699×699'],
    [699, 700, 'probe · 699×700'],
    [700, 699, 'probe · 700×699'],
    [700, 700, 'probe · 700×700'],
    [1099, 699, 'probe · 1099×699'],
    [1099, 700, 'probe · 1099×700'],
    [1100, 699, 'probe · 1100×699'],
    [1100, 700, 'probe · 1100×700'],
    [1348, 699, 'probe · 1348×699'],
    [1348, 700, 'probe · 1348×700'],
    [1349, 699, 'probe · 1349×699'],
    [1349, 700, 'probe · 1349×700'],
    [1352, 699, 'probe · 1352×699'],
    [1352, 700, 'probe · 1352×700'],
    [1353, 699, 'probe · 1353×699'],
    [1353, 700, 'probe · 1353×700'],

    [1559, 700, 'probe · 1559×700'],
    [1560, 699, 'probe · 1560×699'],
    [1560, 700, 'probe · 1560×700'],
    [1659, 699, 'probe · 1659×699'],
    [1659, 700, 'probe · 1659×700'],
    [1660, 699, 'probe · 1660×699'],
    [1660, 700, 'probe · 1660×700'],
    [1699, 699, 'probe · 1699×699'],
    [1699, 700, 'probe · 1699×700'],
    [1700, 699, 'probe · 1700×699'],
    [1700, 700, 'probe · 1700×700'],
    [1899, 699, 'probe · 1899×699'],
    [1899, 700, 'probe · 1899×700'],
    [1900, 699, 'probe · 1900×699'],
    [1900, 700, 'probe · 1900×700'],
    [1979, 699, 'probe · 1979×699'],
    [1979, 700, 'probe · 1979×700'],
    [1980, 699, 'probe · 1980×699'],
    [1980, 700, 'probe · 1980×700'],
    [1999, 699, 'probe · 1999×699'],
    [1999, 700, 'probe · 1999×700'],
    [2000, 699, 'probe · 2000×699'],
    [2000, 700, 'probe · 2000×700']
  ];

  // [name, mark, fit, default theme, STABLE KEY]
  // The key is the arrival contract's handle (see arrival() below). It is the
  // label's URL handle and never changes — an index would move if this list
  // were ever reordered, and the build must not have to know the order.
  // ── THE URL MAP (2026-09-05, Tom's ruling) ───────────────────────────────
  // "Every brand switch is a real navigation to a real URL with a real file
  // path — never an in-place view swap." So Explore rows are ANCHORS, not
  // spans with handlers: a real href is what makes a new tab, a copied link
  // and a refresh all work, and it is what a screen reader announces as a
  // link. The design still emits the pick so the host can intercept it for a
  // client-side transition, but the href is the truth underneath.
  //
  // Keyed by the label index Explore already uses; ATH, Clandestine and the
  // group are named.
  SITE_URLS = {
    0: 'https://northernspy.music',
    1: 'https://nnatapes.music',
    2: 'https://egghunt.music',
    3: 'https://whitedsepulchre.music',
    ath: 'https://acrossthehorizon.music',
    cls: 'https://clandestinelabelservices.com',
    // Corrected 2026-09-05: was StellarAtlasLabelGroup.com. Because siteUrl()
    // is the one home, this single string moves the group cards, all three
    // Explore panels, the nav tuple and the contact mailto together — which is
    // the return on having consolidated it.
    group: 'https://StellarAtlasMusicGroup.com',
    // ── AND THIS DEFINES StellarAtlas.music ────────────────────────────────
    // Map view from any label keeps THAT label's header — the gateway holds
    // the header — while the URL becomes a StellarAtlas.music address. The
    // map is the shared universe and the URL says so. The gateway travels on
    // the nav tuple so the host can write the route (see the star nav emit).
    // ── THE MAP'S URL CARRIES ITS GATEWAY (2026-09-05, settled) ────────────
    // stellaratlas.music/map/<gateway-handle>: the label entered through keeps
    // its header AND rides in the path, so refresh, back and sharing land on
    // the same map with the same chrome. See mapUrl().
    map: 'https://stellaratlas.music/map'
  };
  siteUrl(key) { return this.SITE_URLS[key] || ''; }
  // ── EXPLORE IS NAVIGATION, NOT A VIEW SWITCH (2026-09-06, Tom) ────────────
  // ⚠ FOR CLAUDE CODE, AND FOR ANY FUTURE PASS OVER THIS PANEL. Read this
  // before adding a handler to an Explore row.
  //
  // Every Explore row is an <a href> to the REAL address and NOTHING ELSE. No
  // onClick, no setState, no route swap, no intercept. The browser navigates.
  //
  //   northernspy.music · nnatapes.music · egghunt.music ·
  //   whitedsepulchre.music · acrossthehorizon.music ·
  //   clandestinelabelservices.com · StellarAtlasMusicGroup.com
  //
  // WHY, because the reason is the whole point of the panel: Explore is where
  // someone who arrived at one label moves to another. Those are different
  // SITES on different domains — not views of this one — so a state swap is
  // the wrong verb even when it happens to render something plausible.
  //
  // WHAT THE BLIP WAS: the rows were anchors AND carried the internal switch.
  // The click ran first and repainted this app as the other label, then the
  // href navigated — so a visitor saw the wrong site assembled locally for a
  // moment before the real one loaded. Two navigations racing for one click.
  // Removing the handler removes the race; the href was always correct.
  //
  // This also restores what an anchor is for: cmd-click, middle-click, "copy
  // link address" and a crawler all work, and none of them can be given back
  // by a handler.
  //
  // The one exception on the panel is Haulix, which is a <div> on purpose — it
  // scrolls to a section of the group page rather than going to a site.
  siteUrlNote() { return 'Explore rows are anchors only — see the note above siteUrl.'; }
  // ── DQ-61 · THE BREADCRUMB (2026-09-05) ──────────────────────────────────
  // Drawable at last, because the middle crumb now exists: category pages have
  // real addresses on the label's own domain (/genres → /genre/free-jazz), so
  // every crumb is a real URL and none of them is decorative.
  //
  // Two rules from my own DQ-61 answer, both load-bearing:
  //   · EVERY crumb is a real link. A decorative crumb is worse than none,
  //     because it teaches the reader the trail is fake.
  //   · The LAST crumb is the page you are on and is NOT a link, for the same
  //     reason the album page's label token goes inert on its own domain.
  //
  // And the short form is canonical, per the build: a crumb points at
  // /genre/free-jazz, never /browse?genres=free-jazz — that is the address a
  // visitor copies and the one search engines are told to keep.
  //
  // slug() is the file's own, so a crumb and the route it names cannot
  // disagree.
  crumbTrail(kind, opts) {
    const o = opts || {};
    const root = this.siteUrl(this.state.label == null ? 0 : this.state.label);
    const labelName = (this.LABELS[this.state.label == null ? 0 : this.state.label] || [])[0] || 'Stellar Atlas';
    // The label's own home. Relative, per §1c: within a label a relative path
    // cannot name the wrong host, and each label's own domain is canonical.
    const out = [{ name: labelName, href: '/' }];
    // ── §1b · THE CATEGORY TREE HAS NO ADDRESSES ANY MORE (2026-09-07) ──────
    // These two crumbs used to link to `/genres` and `/genre/<slug>` — the
    // browsable portal your URL lane planned on 2026-09-04. Tom superseded it
    // on 09-05, verbatim: "There should be no route directly to the Starmap
    // because it doesn't have an overall page." No root, no category tree; what
    // survives is `/map/<gateway>` alone.
    //
    // So the crumbs KEEP THEIR NAMES and LOSE THEIR HREFS. They still say where
    // the visitor is — which is the crumb's job — and they no longer offer a
    // navigation to a page that does not exist. A crumb that 404s is worse than
    // a crumb that is only a label, and §1c's rule is explicit: do not link.
    if (o.catGroup) {
      out.push({ name: o.catGroup, href: null });
      if (o.catValue) out.push({ name: o.catValue, href: null });
    }
    if (kind === 'artist' && o.artist) out.push({ name: o.artist, href: this.addr('artist', o.artist) });
    if (kind === 'record') {
      // The artist is a real intermediate page, so it is a crumb rather than a
      // label on the leaf: label / artist / record.
      if (o.artist) out.push({ name: o.artist, href: this.addr('artist', o.artist) });
      // §2 of r47 · the crumb reads the row's supplied address too, so the crumb
      // and the card still cannot disagree. o.row is the PAIRS row when the
      // caller has one; without it there is no address to state.
      if (o.title) out.push({ name: o.title, href: o.row ? this.addr('album', o.row) : null });
    }
    return out.map((c, i) => {
      const last = i === out.length - 1;
      return {
        key: 'cr' + i,
        name: c.name,
        // The leaf keeps its href in the DOM — it IS this page's address, which
        // is worth having for a crawler — but it is inert and unstyled as a
        // link, because a link to where you already are is the decorative
        // crumb DQ-61 rules out.
        href: c.href,
        style: last
          ? { color: 'var(--ink)', textDecoration: 'none', pointerEvents: 'none', cursor: 'default' }
          : { color: 'var(--ink55)', textDecoration: 'none', cursor: 'pointer' },
        sepStyle: last ? { display: 'none' } : { color: 'var(--ink40)', padding: '0 0.5em' }
      };
    });
  }
  // The portal's header, per DQ-57: mark · where you are · Browse · Explore ·
  // cart, and NOT a label's nav (Releases / Artists / Tour), because those are
  // a label's pages and the portal has none. "Where you are" is the same
  // breadcrumb component the label pages use — its first crumb is Stellar
  // Atlas instead of the label, which is the only difference.
  portalCrumbs(group, value) {
    const root = this.siteUrl('group');
    const out = [{ name: 'Stellar Atlas', href: root }];
    // §1b · same retirement as crumbTrail: the category tree these two crumbs
    // linked into does not exist. Names kept, hrefs dropped.
    if (group) out.push({ name: group, href: null });
    if (value) out.push({ name: value, href: null });
    return out.map((c, i) => {
      const last = i === out.length - 1;
      return {
        key: 'pc' + i, name: c.name, href: c.href,
        style: last
          ? { color: 'var(--ink)', textDecoration: 'none', pointerEvents: 'none', cursor: 'default' }
          : { color: 'var(--ink55)', textDecoration: 'none', cursor: 'pointer' },
        sepStyle: last ? { display: 'none' } : { color: 'var(--ink40)', padding: '0 0.5em' }
      };
    });
  }
  crumbRowStyle() {
    return {
      display: 'flex', alignItems: 'center', flexWrap: 'wrap',
      fontFamily: "'JetBrains Mono', monospace", fontSize: '9px',
      letterSpacing: '0.14em', textTransform: 'uppercase'
    };
  }
  // ════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
  // §1a / §1c · THE ADDRESS TABLE · ONE AUTHORITY (2026-09-07)
  // ════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
  // You measured that the rendered page contains NO LINKS AT ALL — 0 anchors and
  // 1,310 cursor:pointer divs on a releases page — and asked for the thing
  // underneath the click: a destination that exists in the document. This is it.
  //
  // ⚠ EVERY href IN THIS FILE COMES FROM HERE. Not from a template literal, not
  // from a second copy of the scheme. The reason is your own two 404s: the route
  // registry declares `/releases/<handle>` and `/artists/<handle>` while the
  // live site serves `/album/` and `/artist/`, so a plausible-looking plural
  // 404s for every visitor. A scheme written in eleven places drifts to the
  // wrong one of those; written once, it moves in one edit when you tell me a
  // row moved.
  //
  // THE TABLE, as you measured it live on 2026-09-07:
  //   /album/<handle>                    200
  //   /artist/<handle>                   200
  //   /releases /artists /tour /news /about   200 · the label's own views
  //   /radio                             200 · NORTHERN SPY ONLY
  //   /map/<gateway>                     200 · stellaratlas.music only
  //   /releases/<handle>                 404 · DO NOT LINK
  //   /artists/<handle>                  404 · DO NOT LINK
  //
  // Within a label: RELATIVE, because the label's own domain is the canonical
  // host and a relative path cannot name the wrong one. Across labels: absolute
  // to that brand's own home, which is a real cross-domain navigation (the
  // Explore rows already work this way). The map is absolute because it lives
  // only on stellaratlas.music.
  //
  // Returns null for anything with no address, NOT ''. The difference is not
  // cosmetic: a hole resolving to '' renders href="" and a bare href="" reloads
  // the page the visitor is already on, which is worse than no link. null makes
  // React omit the attribute, so the element is an <a> with no href — not a
  // link, behaves exactly as the span it replaced. Measured this the wrong way
  // round once: 13 of 31 routes carried empty hrefs before this was fixed.
  // Site mode: the host mounted this file as a real site rather than the design
  // tool opening it on its own. Declared as one reader because r47 §2 turns on
  // it — a DERIVED address may render in the tool and must never render on the
  // site, where a wrong address is a 404 for a visitor.
  siteMode() {
    const w = (typeof window !== 'undefined') ? window : null;
    return !!((this.props && this.props.site) || (w && w.__DC_SITE));
  }
  addr(kind, a, b) {
    const S = (v) => this.slug(String(v || ''));
    switch (kind) {
      case 'releases': case 'artists': case 'tour': case 'news': case 'about':
        return '/' + kind;
      // An ATH deck card. 'a' is the card index; the path is its slug, so the
      // four Tom named read /the-idea, /curator-map, /broadcast and /subscribe.
      // Cards 5 and 7 double as their own routes (ath-journal,
      // ath-all-releases) and those keep their existing addresses — this is the
      // address of the CARD, which is the map with that card open.
      // ── ONLY THE CARDS THE HOST SERVES (2026-09-11, engineering: "should
      // addr('ath-card') return null for a card the host cannot serve yet, so
      // the design fails visibly instead of emitting a dead address?" — yes,
      // and that is this file's standing rule: a derived address may render in
      // the design tool and must NEVER render on the site. Same ruling as
      // addr('album') and addr('artist').)
      //
      // ATH_CARD_SLUG still lists all eight — it is the name↔index map, and a
      // partial one is the trap its own note warns about. THIS decides which
      // of them is a real URL, and today that is the four the nav links.
      // /the-core-idea and /the-drops were emittable through tuple.url and
      // both 404.
      //
      // The host widens it without a design change: publish
      // window.__DC_CARD_ROUTES = ['the-idea', …] and this defers to it.
      case 'ath-card': {
        const s = this.athCardSlug(a);
        if (!s) return null;
        // ⚠ A CARD ADDRESS EXISTS ONLY INSIDE ACROSS THE HORIZON (2026-09-11,
        // engineering r70-A Q6). Their athJournalRoutes.ts holds a standing
        // rule that journal must NEVER become a label view, because "adding
        // journal there would mint /journal on all five domains and four of
        // them would resolve to a route that does not exist" — and they are
        // right that the two mechanisms agree today and would diverge the
        // moment someone reconciled them.
        //
        // ⚠ THIS TABLE IS NOT THE AUTHORITY FOR /journal, NOR FOR ANY OF THESE
        // PATHS ON ANOTHER LABEL. It describes the eight cards of ATH's own
        // deck and nothing else. The nav only calls it under isAth, so the
        // rule held by convention; it is a GATE now so a future caller cannot
        // break it by accident. Same shape as addr('radio'), which is Northern
        // Spy's alone, and addr('ath-subscribe') above.
        if (!this.athArrival(this.state)) return null;
        const w = (typeof window !== 'undefined') ? window : null;
        // ⚠ WHAT THE HOST SERVES TODAY, MEASURED — not what the nav links.
        // /the-idea and /curator-map measure 404, so listing them would keep
        // the design emitting the known-dead addresses this gate exists to
        // stop.
        //
        // ⚠ /journal WAS HERE FOR ONE DROP AND IS OUT AGAIN. I added it on the
        // reasoning that it "has always carried an href and was never reported
        // dead" — true, and not sufficient. r70-A Q6 then told me it resolves
        // only through a redirect minted 2026-09-06 for an unrelated reason. A
        // redirect is not a route, and a default that quietly leans on one
        // works until somebody tidies it up. It returns the day they call
        // /journal a real card route — via __DC_CARD_ROUTES, no design change.
        const served = (this.props && this.props.cardRoutes)
          || (w && w.__DC_CARD_ROUTES)
          || ['broadcast', 'subscribe'];
        if (served.indexOf(s) < 0) return this.siteMode() ? null : ('/' + s);
        return '/' + s;
      }
      // The same two pages reached as routes rather than cards. Both returned
      // null, so Subscribe had NO address by either path — the card one was
      // never wired to the control and this one did not exist.
      // ⚠ LABEL-GATED, like radio above (2026-09-11, engineering: all four
      // non-ATH domains were serving a crawlable <a href="/subscribe"> to a
      // measured 404). These pages exist on Across the Horizon and nowhere
      // else, so off ATH they have no address — the same shape as radio,
      // which is Northern Spy's alone.
      case 'ath-subscribe': return this.athArrival(this.state) ? '/subscribe' : null;
      case 'ath-broadcast': return this.athArrival(this.state) ? '/broadcast' : null;
      // Northern Spy alone has a radio page; on any other label the row does
      // not exist, so neither does its address.
      case 'radio':
        return this.labelKeyOf(this.state) === 'northern-spy' ? '/radio' : null;
      // ── ⚠ THE HANDLE, NOT A RE-SLUGGED DISPLAY NAME (2026-09-10) ─────────
      // Engineering: 30 of 307 live artist links are hard 404s — 9.8%, and
      // 23.4% on ATH. /artist/yarn/wire, /artist/dama/libra,
      // /artist/thurston-moore-&-john-moloney. slug() folds accents and strips
      // ' . , ( ) but / & # pass straight into the path — and two stored
      // handles (v-v-lightbody, j-o-y-s) cannot be derived from the display
      // name by ANY rule, so no amount of stripping reaches them.
      //
      // ⚠ AND slug() IS NOT WIDENED, deliberately — engineering asked us not
      // to and they are right twice over. Their matching side shares that
      // character class and the curator-photo join depends on it, so widening
      // it here would break portraits on the curator map to fix links on the
      // artists wall. It is also the wrong SHAPE of fix: "V V Lightbody" →
      // "v-v-lightbody" is not a transformation, it is a FACT, and this file's
      // standing rule is that a derived address may render in the tool and must
      // never render on the site.
      //
      // So: INDEX[6] is the artist's stored handle — one index past the current
      // tuple, the same slot and the same argument as PAIRS[6] for the album
      // id. handleFor() reads it and falls back to the derived slug, so nothing
      // changes for the 277 names where the two already agree.
      case 'artist': {
        if (!a) return null;
        const h = this.handleFor(a);
        if (h) return '/artist/' + h;
        const d = S(a);
        // No handle, and the derived slug carries a character that cannot
        // survive a path segment: this address is KNOWN WRONG. Render nothing
        // on the site rather than a 404 — the tile keeps its name, its portrait
        // and its hover, it simply is not a link until the handle lands. In the
        // design tool it still renders, so the shape stays inspectable.
        if (/[\/&#?]/.test(d)) return this.siteMode() ? null : ('/artist/' + d);
        return '/artist/' + d;
      }
      // ⚠ THE ONE HANDLE I CANNOT KNOW FOR CERTAIN. Your example
      // (`/album/suss-counting-sunsets`) is artist-then-title, which is what
      // this builds — but four live records are titled 'S/T' and two 'Split',
      // so the derived handle COLLIDES for six of them, and PAIRS[6] (Album.id)
      // is the fix we have both already agreed to. Until it lands, every album
      // anchor also carries data-album-artist and data-album-title so you can
      // resolve the true address server-side without parsing my href — you said
      // you would rather give me a resolver than have me guess, and this is me
      // naming the one place I am guessing.
      // ── §2 of r47 · THE ADDRESS IS READ, NOT BUILT ─────────────────────
      // You measured 118 of 199 derived album addresses 404ing — 59.3% — and you
      // are right that a handle is a stored string and not a function of artist
      // and title. Four records share "S/T"; some handles carry debris like a
      // trailing -copy. No amount of care in a derivation fixes either.
      //
      // So this READS. `a` is the PAIRS row, and the order of preference is:
      //   row[7]  the site's own address for this record  ← the one to fill
      //   derived ONLY when this file is open on its own (siteMode() false), so
      //           the design tool keeps working links and THE SITE NEVER EMITS A
      //           GUESS. On the site an unsupplied address yields null, which
      //           means no anchor — a card that is not a link beats a card that
      //           links to a 404, and it is visible to you in a way a wrong
      //           href is not.
      case 'album': {
        const row = a;
        if (!row) return null;
        if (typeof row[7] === 'string' && row[7]) return row[7];
        // ── THE INDEX-6 FALLBACK IS GONE (2026-09-07, r49, engineering) ──────
        // This used to return '/album/' + row[6] (Album.id). You measured that
        // your toPairs() writes index 6 on EVERY row — it is the r4 §18 join
        // key we asked for — and that your album route matches the handle
        // verbatim (findAlbumByHandle), so an id is never a handle and every
        // one of those links 404s. A last-resort branch that fires on every
        // card is not a last resort: it would have made r47/r48 WORSE than
        // r45, 199 dead instead of 118. You were right to push back, and the
        // measurement is the reason this changed rather than the argument.
        //
        // So index 6 is no longer read here. It keeps its other job — the
        // record key for REC_TRACKS / REC_NOTES / REC_COVERS /
        // REC_CONTRIBUTORS (recKey) — which is what it was asked for.
        // With no row[7], the site emits NO ANCHOR, which is the behaviour
        // both sides already agreed was the safe failure.
        if (this.siteMode()) return null;
        return (row[0] && row[1]) ? '/album/' + S(row[0]) + '-' + S(row[1]) : null;
      }
      // The LONG path. r39 shipped these at a short `/journal/<slug>` that does
      // not resolve, and you asked for the long one.
      case 'journal':
        return a ? '/l/across-the-horizon/journal/' + a : null;
      // Absolute: the map lives on stellaratlas.music and nowhere else.
      case 'map':
        return this.mapUrl(a || this.labelKeyOf(this.state));
      // Another brand's home — a real cross-domain navigation.
      case 'site':
        return this.siteUrl(a);
      default:
        return null;
    }
  }
  // The click that belongs on an anchor this file draws: keep the design's own
  // in-page behaviour, and stop the browser doing a document load. Your
  // intercept does the same thing for same-origin clicks; doing it here too
  // means the file behaves identically with no host at all, which is what keeps
  // it openable in the design tool. Established pattern — the r45 answer for
  // clsServices said exactly this: "<a href> with the handler kept and
  // preventDefault()".
  navTo(run) {
    return (e) => { if (e && e.preventDefault) e.preventDefault(); return run(e); };
  }
  // The map's address for the gateway currently held. Bare /map is never
  // emitted — a gateway is always known, and bare stellaratlas.music reroutes
  // to Northern Spy host-side (2026-09-05).
  // ⚠ 2026-09-07, §1 AUDIT: this took an INDEX, and ATH has no index — so an
  // ATH visitor on the map got `/map/northern-spy`, and the file's own ruling
  // (see SITE_URLS.map) is that the gateway rides in the path precisely so
  // refresh, back and sharing land on the same map with the same chrome. The
  // §1 bug persisted into a URL: a shared ATH map link came back as Northern
  // Spy. It takes a KEY now, and an index still works so nothing else moved.
  mapUrl(labelIdxOrKey) {
    const keys = ['northern-spy', 'nna-tapes', 'egghunt', 'whited-sepulchre'];
    if (typeof labelIdxOrKey === 'string' && labelIdxOrKey) {
      return this.SITE_URLS.map + '/' + labelIdxOrKey;
    }
    return this.SITE_URLS.map + '/' + (keys[labelIdxOrKey == null ? 0 : labelIdxOrKey] || keys[0]);
  }
  LABELS = [
    ['Northern Spy', 'brand/marks/northern-spy.svg', 'contain', 'light', 'northern-spy'],
    ['NNA Tapes', 'brand/marks/nna-tapes.svg', 'contain', 'dark', 'nna-tapes'],
    ['Egghunt', 'brand/marks/egghunt.svg', 'contain', 'dark', 'egghunt'],
    ['Whited Sepulchre', 'brand/marks/whited-sepulchre.png', 'contain', 'dark', 'whited-sepulchre']
  ];

  // ── THE ARRIVAL LABEL ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────
  // Same shape as __DC_SITE and __DC_DATA: the host tells the file, before
  // first render, which label the visitor arrived on.
  //
  //     window.__DC_LABEL = 'nna-tapes'      // or props.label
  //
  // One of: northern-spy · nna-tapes · egghunt · whited-sepulchre ·
  // across-the-horizon. A key, not an index — it is the route handle you
  // already have, and it survives this list being reordered.
  //
  // WHAT IT SETS, AND NOTHING MORE:
  //   · st.label  — the chrome: lockup, nav, hero set, Explore's "you are
  //                 here". This is the value the map keeps after arrival (the
  //                 2026-09-01 ruling); setting it here IS that ruling's
  //                 wiring, no second mechanism.
  //   · st.theme  — the label's default: light for Northern Spy and ATH, dark
  //                 for the other three. Tom, 2026-08-25.
  //   · route     — 'ath-map' for across-the-horizon, since ATH is not one of
  //                 the four LABELS but its own route family.
  //
  // WHAT IT DOES NOT DO: filter data. INDEX and PAIRS arrive already scoped
  // from the host. st.label has never selected data and does not start now.
  //
  // Absent or unrecognised → Northern Spy, light, the star view: exactly what
  // the file did before this existed, so the standalone design is unchanged.
  //
  // ⚠ CLASS-FIELD ORDER. `state` is declared ~450 lines ABOVE `LABELS`, and
  // class fields initialise top to bottom — so when this runs, this.LABELS is
  // still undefined. The key table is therefore repeated here as a static
  // rather than read from LABELS. Keep the two in step; a mismatch here is a
  // label that silently arrives as Northern Spy.
  // (A method, not a static field or a class-name reference — the runtime that
  // evaluates this source does not need to support either.)
  // ── DATA IS A PROP (2026-09-03) ──────────────────────────────────────────
  // Every content table the host can feed from a real source is read through
  // data(name): props.<name> → window.__DC_DATA.<name> → this file's own
  // DEFAULT_<name> literal. The literal is the design's mock and MUST keep
  // working when nothing is supplied — that is what keeps this file openable
  // on its own. The host's pattern-matching of class-field literals is retired
  // by this: name the field, pass the value, done.
  //
  //   PAIRS      the catalogue — [artist, title, meta, cover, alt, …] per record
  //   INDEX      the artist roster — ALREADY SCOPED TO THE ARRIVAL LABEL by the host
  //   PHOTO_BASE / PHOTO_HAVE   ATH portrait path and the set on disk
  //   NEWS       the label feed · JOURNAL  ATH field notes · QUEUE  tracklists
  //   BUY        the buy-row table (DQ-33/37) · VOLS  ATH volumes / drops
  // Shapes are the DEFAULT_ literals' shapes, exactly. This file does not coerce.
  // Plain method calls, not accessors: `get` hangs this runtime like `static` did.
  // ── THE DATA PACKAGE ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────
  // Resolution order, first hit wins:
  //   1. props.<NAME>                 — the host passing a table directly
  //   2. window.__DC_DATA.<NAME>      — the host's runtime injection (site)
  //   3. data-package.json            — a SNAPSHOT of the real catalogue that
  //                                     ships beside this file, so the design
  //                                     tool shows real covers, names, counts
  //   4. DEFAULT_<NAME>              — the typed mock, last resort
  //
  // The package is the build's mirror of my drop: they generate it from the
  // live catalogue in THIS FILE's own positional formats and it travels in the
  // same folder, images beside it, path-referenced like every res() asset.
  // Loaded once in componentDidMount; until it lands (or if there is none) the
  // mock renders, which is what keeps the file openable on its own.
  //
  // The package NEVER overrides a host injection. On the site, __DC_DATA is
  // the live truth and the snapshot is just what the file happened to ship
  // with. In the tool there is no __DC_DATA, so the snapshot is what you see.
  // ── COUNT LINES ───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
  // "N of TOTAL": N is a WINDOW — how many rows are painted right now — and
  // only this file knows it at runtime. TOTAL is the catalogue's size and is
  // DATA. So TITLES.<view>[1] is the TOTAL (a plain number-string), and this
  // composes the line; the site never has to know how many rows are on screen.
  // The phone sub (TITLES.<view>[2]) is a finished string — no window there.
  // The painted window. The drawer and the grid page in CAP-sized chunks;
  // until a real list is wired the mock window is CAP. When PAIRS is real this
  // is min(CAP, rows) — never more than there are.
  shownCount(view) {
    const cap = this.paintCap();
    // Releases: the painted list is PAIRS capped — the same slice the drawer
    // and the grid map over, so the number is the number of tiles. Artists:
    // the wall filters on portrait presence, so it is what is on the wall.
    const n = (view === 'artists') ? this.data('INDEX').filter(a => !!a[1]).length : this.data('PAIRS').length;
    return Math.min(cap, n);
  }
  countLine(view, shown) {
    const row = this.titlesFor(view) || this.data('TITLES').releases;
    const raw = String(row[1]).replace(/^\d+ of /, '');   // tolerate the old "12 of 179" form
    // A bare number is a TOTAL and gets the window in front of it. Anything
    // else ("8 with records", "3 dates") is a finished line and renders as is.
    if (!raw) return String(shown);                  // no total supplied → just what is shown
    return /^\d+$/.test(raw) ? (shown + ' of ' + raw) : raw;
  }
  data(name) {
    const p = this.props || {};
    if (p[name] != null) return p[name];
    const w = (typeof window !== 'undefined') ? window : null;
    if (w && w.__DC_DATA && w.__DC_DATA[name] != null) return w.__DC_DATA[name];
    if (this._pkg && this._pkg.tables && this._pkg.tables[name] != null) return this._pkg.tables[name];
    // ⚠ IN SITE MODE A MOCK IS A DEFECT, NOT A FALLBACK (2026-09-03). Seven
    // routes on the live site mounted the drop with no data= and rendered the
    // mock — Jeremy Cunningham, "Passing Fires", the mock tour dates — as a
    // 200 with nothing in the console. From a browser that is indistinguishable
    // from "the data did not load", and it cost a day. The design file must
    // keep falling back so it opens on its own; the SITE must not. So: site
    // mode + no host table for a DATA-class name → throw, naming the table.
    // Design-class tables (see manifest.contentTables.design) are never
    // injected and never throw.
    if (w && (this.props.site || w.__DC_SITE)) {
      // ── THE STOP FRAMES ARE EXEMPT (2026-09-07, engineering) ──────────────
      // Their ask, and it is right on its own terms: "an error page is precisely
      // the surface that must still render when the data pipeline is what
      // failed — requiring PAIRS there means the error page dies of the same
      // cause it exists to report." A 404 was rendering this throw's text
      // instead of "No such record" (live: /artist/carlos-nino on r49).
      //
      // THE GUARD ITSELF IS NOT WEAKENED, which is the part that matters. It
      // still throws for every content route, and the stop frames still get
      // NOTHING rather than the mock: PAIRS and INDEX are SITE_CATALOGUE, so
      // execution falls into the branch below and they come back empty. "The
      // mock must not render on the site" holds; what changes is that an absent
      // table on a surface that displays no catalogue is no longer a defect
      // report, because there is no wiring on these two routes to have got
      // wrong. §4-4 and §4-5 described them as label-agnostic and data-free and
      // this is what it takes for that to be true.
      //
      // Read from the route rather than a flag so nothing has to be set: see
      // stopFrameRoute() for why it checks three places.
      if (this.SITE_REQUIRED.indexOf(name) > -1 && !this.stopFrameRoute()) {
        throw new Error('[drop] site mode but window.__DC_DATA.' + name + ' is absent — this route mounted the drop without data. The mock must not render on the site.');
      }
      // ⚠ THREE KINDS OF TABLE ON THE SITE (2026-09-03, §61 — corrects §58).
      //
      // §58 said "on the site the mock never renders, for any table" and made
      // every unsupplied table empty. The build emits five tables; the file has
      // fifty-one. Result: a site with records and no heroes, no news, no tour,
      // no radio, no journal, no about — Tom: "it's not showing any data."
      // That was over-correction. The distinction that matters:
      //
      //   CATALOGUE — records, artists, counts, covers. The site HAS this. An
      //     absent table is a wiring fault: PAIRS/INDEX throw, the rest go
      //     empty (SITE_CATALOGUE). Mock catalogue rows must never render.
      //   VISITOR — cart, queue. Absent means the visitor has none. Empty.
      //   EDITORIAL — everything the label WRITES: heroes, news, tour dates,
      //     radio shows, about copy, the journal, ATH's volumes, drops, quotes
      //     and tracklists, the group and Clandestine pages. This file is where
      //     that copy is authored until a CMS exists. It is not mock; it is the
      //     content. Absent from __DC_DATA → the file's own copy renders, on
      //     the site too. When the site grows a source, inject and it wins.
      //
      // The design tool always shows the file's own content in every class.
      if (this.SITE_CATALOGUE.indexOf(name) > -1) {
        const d = this['DEFAULT_' + name];
        const empty = this.SITE_EMPTY[name];
        if (typeof empty === 'function') return empty(d);
        if (Array.isArray(d)) return [];
        if (d && typeof d === 'object') return {};
        if (typeof d === 'string') return '';
        return d;
      }
      if (this.SITE_EMPTY_WHEN_ABSENT.indexOf(name) > -1) return [];
      // EDITORIAL: fall through to the authored content.
    }
    return this['DEFAULT_' + name];
  }
  // Is this one of the two stop frames? Read from three places in the order
  // they become true, because data() can be called BEFORE arrival() has settled
  // state — a first render that reads PAIRS would otherwise throw on the very
  // route this exemption exists for:
  //   this.state.route   once the design has arrived
  //   this.props.route   a host that passes the route as a prop
  //   window.__DC_ROUTE  the arrival key the host publishes before mount (§4-1)
  // Anything else — including no route at all — is NOT a stop frame, so the
  // guard stays on by default and only these two names turn it off.
  // ── THE IDEA'S TWO CARDS · ONE COPY (2026-09-09, Tom) ─────────────────────
  // The deck card's grid needs them as two sibling divs and the map's sidebar
  // wants them as a list, so both shapes are needed and neither may hold the
  // words. They live here once; renderVals exposes six flat keys for the grid
  // and the array for the sidebar, both derived from this.
  ATH_IDEA_CARDS = [
        { key: 'ic0', kicker: "Drop 2", title: "Luke Schneider invites Marisa Anderson", body: "Curating Volume One’s second drop, Schneider brought forward Kyle Hamlett Duo — and Marisa Anderson, who joins him on “first bounce fly fly fly.”" },
        { key: 'ic1', kicker: "Drop 4", title: "Marisa Anderson curates her own drop", body: "Two drops later, Anderson returns as a curator in her own right — introducing Charlie Parr and Emily Dix Thomas." }
  ];
  STOP_ROUTES = ['notfound', 'error'];
  // ATH_MAP_PANEL DELETED 2026-09-07 with the curator map's corner switch. It
  // existed so audioWrapStyle could stand the header button down on the one
  // panel that had its own control; there is no such panel now — the header
  // carries all seven. Removed rather than left defined, per this file's rule
  // that a constant with no caller is what a build reads as intent.
  //
  // ONE FACT FROM IT IS WORTH KEEPING, because it misled two passes: the ATH
  // landing's `panelNavLabel` names array is OFFSET BY ONE from the panel it
  // labels — it names where the control GOES, not where it is. The curator map
  // is panel 2 and the drops card is panel 3, both measured. Anything that
  // reasons about a specific ATH panel index should measure, not read that array.
  stopFrameRoute() {
    const s = this.state || {};
    const p = this.props || {};
    const w = (typeof window !== 'undefined') ? window : null;
    const r = s.route || p.route || (w ? w.__DC_ROUTE : null);
    return this.STOP_ROUTES.indexOf(r) > -1;
  }
  // The tables the SITE must always supply. Every other DEFAULT_ is either
  // content the site legitimately may not have yet (BUY, QUEUE, JOURNAL) or
  // design-class. Kept short on purpose: the point is to catch a route that
  // forgot data= entirely, not to police coverage.
  // ⚠ NOT ON THE STOP FRAMES — see the exemption in data(). They render no
  // catalogue, so there is no wiring on them to have got wrong.
  SITE_REQUIRED = ['PAIRS', 'INDEX'];
  // Catalogue-class tables: the site owns this data, so an unsupplied one is
  // empty, never the mock. Everything not here and not per-visitor is
  // EDITORIAL and renders the file's authored copy until the site supplies it.
  SITE_CATALOGUE = ['PAIRS', 'INDEX', 'PHOTOS', 'PLACE', 'SOCIALS', 'BUY', 'TITLES', 'CAT_VALUES', 'REC_TRACKS', 'REC_CONTRIBUTORS', 'REC_TOUR_EVENTS', 'REC_NOTES', 'REC_COVERS', 'TOUR', 'TRACKS', 'VIDEOS', 'PHOTO_HAVE'];
  // Tables that are the VISITOR'S state, not the catalogue's. In site mode an
  // absent one is empty, not mocked.
  // Subsumed by §58 (every data-class table is empty when absent in site mode);
  // kept because the manifest names the contract. Listed here are the ones
  // that are per-VISITOR rather than per-catalogue.
  // VIDEOS moved OUT of this list on 2026-09-04 (designer request 12): it is
  // catalogue-class — the site always has it — so an absent VIDEOS is a wiring
  // gap that SITE_CATALOGUE reports, not a visitor with none.
  // TOUR_APRIL / TOUR_MAY / TOUR_CITIES added 2026-09-04 (ledger 19, 22): they
  // are editorial-class in shape but their literals state catalogue facts —
  // named artists, real venues, a city count — so falling through published
  // invented dates. ABOUT_CONTACTS and the two radio tables likewise (23, 24):
  // a half-written address and a claimed residency are worse than an absence.
  SITE_EMPTY_WHEN_ABSENT = ['CART', 'QUEUE', 'TOUR', 'RADIO_NOW', 'NOW_PLAYING',
    'BROADCAST_HOMES', 'BROADCAST_RECENT',
    'TOUR_APRIL', 'TOUR_MAY', 'TOUR_CITIES', 'ABOUT_CONTACTS', 'RADIO_HOMES', 'RADIO_BEFORE', 'LEVEL'];
  // The EMPTY value per data table, where a plain [] / {} is not enough because
  // a reader indexes into a known shape. Anything not listed gets [] or {}.
  //   TITLES          — headings stay, totals blank → countLine shows the window alone
  //   NEWEST_RELEASED — a pointer; with no VOLS there is no newest drop → vol/drop 0
  //   PHOTO_BASE      — a path prefix, stays a string
  // ── §4-2 · THE DEFAULT_ BLOCK MARKERS (2026-09-07) ───────────────────────
