  // You asked for the start and end of the DEFAULT_ block so the stage step can
  // drop it for site mode without a parser guessing. They are in, as 28 pairs.
  //
  // ⚠ NO EXAMPLE MARKER IS PRINTED IN THIS COMMENT, deliberately — an earlier
  // draft showed the shape here with placeholder names, and my own generator
  // parsed the example as a 29th real marker naming three tables that do not
  // exist. By the refusal rule below that is a stale marker, so the
  // documentation would have made the transform refuse every run. The shape is
  // in defaults-markers.json instead, which ships beside this file and is
  // generated FROM the markers rather than describing them.
  //
  // Each pair is one line at two-space indent, opening with a block comment
  // whose first token is DC-DEFAULTS, then a colon and START or END, then
  // `run=NN` and the per-table `drop=` and `keep=` lists. Anchor on the
  // two-space indent and you cannot pick up prose.
  //
  // 28 pairs rather than one because the tables are not contiguous — they sit
  // between the methods that read them, which is where they belong. The runs
  // are stable; `run=` is their index so a diff names the one that moved.
  //
  // ⚠ AND THE PART THAT MATTERS MORE THAN THE MARKERS: **DO NOT DROP THE WHOLE
  // BLOCK.** 65 tables; only 29 can go. The other 36 are EDITORIAL — the
  // heroes, the news, the radio shows, the about copy, the journal, ATH's
  // volumes and quotes and tracklists, the group and Clandestine pages. This
  // file is where that copy is AUTHORED until a CMS exists (see data()'s three
  // classes), so the site renders it on purpose. Strip it and every label site
  // loses its written content while still returning 200 — the same failure
  // shape as the seven routes that mounted with no data= in September, which
  // took a day to find. That is why each marker carries `drop=` and `keep=`
  // per table, computed from SITE_CATALOGUE and SITE_EMPTY_WHEN_ABSENT below:
  // drop what the START line says to drop, keep the rest, and the classes can
  // never drift from the contract they are derived from.
  //
  // TITLES and PHOTO_BASE are droppable-looking and are NOT droppable: their
  // SITE_EMPTY readers index the literal. They are marked keep, and the readers
  // are now defensive anyway (below), so a mistake degrades instead of throwing.
  //
  // Same refusal discipline as the comment strip: no markers found, no
  // transform. And if a START names a table the file does not declare, that is
  // a stale marker — refuse rather than guess.
  SITE_EMPTY = {
    // Defensive since §4-2: a stripped or absent literal must not throw here.
    // Without the guard this is the one line in the file that turns a
    // mis-scoped stage transform into a white page instead of a blank heading.
    TITLES: (d) => { const o = {}; for (const k of Object.keys(d || {})) o[k] = [d[k][0], '', '']; return o; },
    NEWEST_RELEASED: () => ({ vol: 0, drop: 0 }),
    PHOTO_BASE: (d) => d || 'public/ath/photos/',
    GRP_CUSTOM: () => ['', '', ''],
    // CATS is the Browse taxonomy: [group, count, values, colour]. The GROUPS
    // and their colours are the design; the VALUES and counts are the
    // catalogue's. Absent on the site → the groups stand, with no values and
    // no count, rather than a Browse panel that throws on an empty array.
    // BUY rows are read positionally by the buy sheet — [format, note, price,
    // status]. Empty → the sheet has no formats; buyAddLabel must not index.
    BUY: () => []
  };
  // ── WHAT IS BEHIND EACH NAV ROW (2026-09-06) ─────────────────────────────
  // The phone nav's right column, and the reason the dead space is now worth
  // having: every one of these counts already exists in the file, so the nav
  // can say how much is behind a door instead of leaving a third of each row
  // empty. Derived from the same tables the destination pages count, so a row
  // and its page can never disagree.
  //
  // Returns '' where there is no honest number — a nav row is not the place to
  // invent one, and § 23's rule holds: absent rather than approximate.
  menuCount(key) {
    const n = (v) => (typeof v === 'number' && v > 0) ? String(v) : '';
    const rows = (t) => (this.data(t) || []).length;
    switch (String(key)) {
      case 'releases': case 'catalogue': case 'r:catalogue': {
        const t = (this.data('TITLES') || {}).releases;
        const total = t && parseInt(String(t[1]).replace(/[^\d]/g, ''), 10);
        return n(total) || n(rows('PAIRS'));
      }
      case 'artists': case 'r:artists':
        // The wall shows only artists with a portrait, so that is the number.
        return n((this.data('INDEX') || []).filter(x => x[1]).length);
      case 'tour': case 'r:tour': return n(rows('TOUR'));
      case 'news': case 'r:news': return n(rows('NEWS'));
      case 'radio': case 'r:radio': return n(rows('RADIO'));
      case 'ath-all-releases': return n(this.buildDrops(this.state).total);
      case 'ath-artists': {
        const vols = this.data('VOLS') || [];
        const names = {};
        vols.forEach(v => (v.drops || []).forEach(d => {
          if (d[1]) names[d[1]] = 1;
          (d[3] || []).forEach(g => { if (g) names[g] = 1; });
        }));
        return n(Object.keys(names).length);
      }
      case 'ath-journal': case '5': return n(rows('JOURNAL_LIST'));
      case 'ath-broadcast': case '4': return n(rows('BROADCAST'));
      default: return '';
    }
  }
  // Promoted out of renderVals because the nav tuple needs the same answer and
  // was guessing at a state key (`st.athArtist`) that does not exist — so the
  // tuple reported null while the page displayed the name. A renderVals local
  // is invisible to _navTuple by construction; anything both need is a method.
  athArtistOnPage() {
    const want = this.curArtist() || '';
    if (want && this.athArtistCtx(want)) return want;
    // The fallback has to DEMONSTRATE the page, not its empty state: prefer the
    // first roster name that actually has a photograph. Picking blindly landed
    // on an artist with no photo, no bio and no statement — four placeholders
    // stacked, which is the page nobody could read.
    const withPhoto = this.data('INDEX').filter(x => x[1] && this.athArtistCtx(x[0]))[0];
    if (withPhoto) return withPhoto[0];
    const first = (this.data('VOLS') && this.data('VOLS')[1] && this.data('VOLS')[1].drops && this.data('VOLS')[1].drops[0]);
    return (first && first[3] && first[3][0]) || (first && first[1]) || 'Artist pending';
  }
  curArtist() {
    if (this.state.artist) return this.state.artist;
    const p = this.data('PAIRS');
    return (p && p[0] && p[0][0]) || '';
  }
  // The stamp, for the picker's caption and for anyone asking "which world is
  // this?" — null when the mock is showing.
  // ── ⚠ THE CRASH THAT PUT MY NOTES ON A PUBLIC SITE (2026-09-06) ──────────
  // `this.titlesFor(view)[0]` on a view TITLES has no key for threw
  // `Cannot read properties of undefined (reading '0')` inside renderVals — and
  // when renderVals throws, NOTHING resolves: every `{{ }}` in the template
  // leaks as literal text. That single fault produced both of tonight's
  // headline defects. The notes block was not "shipped"; captionStyle is
  // display:none in site mode and was never reached. The four
  // `src="{{ …embedSrc }}"` 404s were not a name mismatch either — those
  // providers are correct; they simply never ran.
  //
  // MY DEFAULT_TITLES has every key, which is why the tool never showed it. The
  // host's injected table does not have to, and a missing key is not a data
  // error — TITLES is a per-view LABEL table, so absent means "this view has no
  // custom title", which is a legitimate thing to publish.
  //
  // So: never index it directly. `filtered` → `releases` → a built row, in that
  // order, so any view renders and no view can throw.
  titlesFor(view) {
    const T = this.data('TITLES') || {};
    const row = T[view] || (view === 'filtered' ? T.releases : null) || T.releases;
    if (row && row.length) return row;
    // Last resort: a title from the view's own name rather than an exception.
    const name = String(view || '').replace(/^./, c => c.toUpperCase());
    return [name, '', ''];
  }
  dataStamp() {
    const k = this._pkg;
    return k ? (k.catalogueRevision || '?') + ' · ' + (k.generatedAt || '').slice(0, 10) : null;
  }
  loadDataPackage() {
    if (typeof window === 'undefined' || typeof fetch !== 'function') return;
    // Site mode: the host owns data. Do not even fetch.
    if (this.props.site || window.__DC_SITE) return;
    fetch('data-package.json', { cache: 'no-store' })
      .then(r => (r.ok ? r.json() : null))
      .then(k => {
        if (!k || !k.tables) return;
        // Refuse a package that names a table this file does not declare —
        // the same closed-list rule the build applies to my drop, mirrored.
        const bad = Object.keys(k.tables).filter(n => !(('DEFAULT_' + n) in this));
        if (bad.length) { console.warn('data-package: unknown tables ignored —', bad.join(', ')); bad.forEach(n => delete k.tables[n]); }
        this._pkg = k;
        this.forceUpdate();
      })
      .catch(() => { /* no package, no problem — the mock is the fallback by design */ });
  }

  arrivalTable() {
    return {
      'northern-spy': [0, 'light'], 'nna-tapes': [1, 'dark'],
      'egghunt': [2, 'dark'], 'whited-sepulchre': [3, 'dark']
    };
  }
  // ── WHOSE SITE THIS IS · THE ONE READER (2026-09-07, §1) ───────────────────
  // ⚠ NOTHING may derive the label from the route name. That derivation is the
  // bug this method exists to end: `route.indexOf('ath-') === 0` lived in two
  // places, so the instant an ATH visitor tapped MAP or GRID the route stopped
  // starting with `ath-`, the test went false, and the chrome fell through to
  // index 0 of the label table — Northern Spy. Northern Spy only ever looked
  // correct because it WAS the fallback.
  //
  // ATH cannot be an index in that table: its row carries `null` where the
  // other four carry 0–3, deliberately and correctly. So the arrival KEY is
  // what persists, in state, and this is the only place that reads it.
  //
  // Three steps, in order, and the last two are for this file opened on its
  // own with no host: the carried key, then the route prefix, then the index.
  // The design tool is therefore unchanged.
  // ⚠ A METHOD, NOT A FIELD, AND THAT IS LOAD-BEARING (2026-09-07).
  // The four labels' stable keys also live in LABELS[i][4], and reading them
  // from there would be the obvious single source — but LABELS is an instance
  // FIELD declared below `state`, and `state = this.arrival(…)` is itself a
  // field initializer, so at the moment arrival() runs `this.LABELS` is still
  // undefined. A method is on the prototype and always available. This cost one
  // white screen; the duplication against LABELS[i][4] is deliberate and the
  // two orders must stay in step.
  labelKeys() { return ['northern-spy', 'nna-tapes', 'egghunt', 'whited-sepulchre']; }
  // Three steps, in order. In practice only the FIRST ever runs: arrival()
  // resolves labelKey non-null at first paint, so there is no runtime fallback
  // and no navigator can flip the answer by leaving an `ath-` route. The other
  // two remain for a caller that hands in a bare or partial state — a test, a
  // probe, a future host that mounts without going through arrival().
  labelKeyOf(st) {
    const keys = this.labelKeys();
    if (st && st.labelKey) return st.labelKey;
    if (st && typeof st.route === 'string' && st.route.indexOf('ath-') === 0) return 'across-the-horizon';
    return keys[st && st.label] || keys[0];
  }
  // "Is this visitor inside Across the Horizon" — an identity question, true on
  // `star` and `catalogue` too. Not "is this an ATH route", which stays
  // `route.indexOf('ath-') === 0` and still decides PAGE LAYOUT. The two were
  // one flag and had to come apart: identity travels with the visitor, layout
  // belongs to the route.
  athArrival(st) { return this.labelKeyOf(st) === 'across-the-horizon'; }
  arrival(base) {
    const w = (typeof window !== 'undefined') ? window : {};
    const p = this.props || {};
    const key = p.label || w.__DC_LABEL || null;
    // ══ THE SAFE WIDTH TEST, HOISTED (2026-09-14, engineering §3) ═══════════
    // ⚠ I WROTE THE WARNING AND THEN BROKE IT TWICE IN THE SAME FUNCTION.
    // arrival() is field #3 of the class; phoneNoMap() → _narrowNow() reads
    // this.state, this.VPS and this.PROBE_VPS, all declared BELOW it, so
    // during the field initialiser st.vp throws. My own note four hundred
    // lines down says "NOTHING CALLED FROM arrival() MAY TOUCH A FIELD
    // DECLARED BELOW state", and I called phoneNoMap() from here anyway — at
    // the ATH label branch and at the ?card= branch.
    //
    // It did not reach engineering only by luck: the throw is gated on
    // key === 'across-the-horizon', and their dropLabelKey() returns null for
    // ATH deliberately, while __DC_CARD appears nowhere in their code. A
    // future host that passes label= would hit it on the first paint.
    //
    // So the safe form is declared ONCE, here, above every use. window.innerWidth
    // is the only width source that needs no field — which is exactly why the
    // safe form existed further down, and it now serves all three sites.
    const arrivalNarrow = ((typeof w.innerWidth === 'number' && w.innerWidth) || 1180) < 700;
    let s = base;
    // ── ATH'S LANDING IS REACHABLE ON A PHONE (2026-09-15, Tom, via engineering)
    // ⚠ THIS DOOR USED TO SEND A PHONE TO THE VOLUMES LIST, and it is the door a
    // shared link uses — so acrossthehorizon.music on a phone landed on Volumes
    // under "the curator map needs a wider screen". r78 fixed the new invariant
    // (_enforceNoPhoneMap scopes to 'star' only) and left the six pre-existing
    // doors untouched, so the page was still deleted and the manifest claimed
    // otherwise. Engineering measured it and were right.
    //
    // Tom's ruling, verbatim: "ATH should have a full card set, including the
    // curator map. The curator map is not Starmap. It's an interactive graphic
    // card… we're not getting rid of map mode. It wasn't map mode before." And,
    // on gating the card off instead: "Do not suppress curator-map."
    //
    // So the ONLY phone change for ATH is hover default → player default, which
    // hoverArmed()'s narrow guard already delivers. The route is the same at
    // every width.
    if (key === 'across-the-horizon') s = Object.assign({}, s, { theme: 'light', route: 'ath-map' });
    else if (key) { const hit = this.arrivalTable()[key]; if (hit) s = Object.assign({}, s, { label: hit[0], theme: hit[1] }); }
    // ── §4-1 · THE ROUTE ARRIVES FROM THE HOST WHEN THE HOST KNOWS IT ───────
    // __DC_ROUTE (or a `route` prop) when present; the baked `route:` literal in
    // the state above when absent — so r46 installs either side of the switch to
    // the one served file. It runs AFTER the label branch on purpose: that
    // branch sets ATH's DEFAULT landing, and a real ATH route published by the
    // host must outrank a default. That ordering is also what unblocks
    // dropLabelKey.ts — the build can now publish 'across-the-horizon' together
    // with a real ath- route and have both survive.
    const wantRoute = p.route != null ? p.route : w.__DC_ROUTE;
    if (typeof wantRoute === 'string' && wantRoute && this.ROUTES.indexOf(wantRoute) > -1) {
      s = Object.assign({}, s, { route: wantRoute });
    }
    // ── DOOR 3 OF 4 · A SHARED MAP LINK, OPENED ON A PHONE ─────────────────
    // (2026-09-14, Tom: redirect to the grid, with a note.)
    //
    // This is the case the gate exists FOR. The map URLs are public and
    // shareable — someone sends a constellation, the recipient opens it on a
    // phone. Refusing silently would make their link look broken, so the
    // redirect says so in one line and gets out of the way.
    //
    // ⚠ AND IT HAS TO RUN HERE, not on mount. This is first-paint STATE: the
    // phone must never render a frame of map and then jump. Same reasoning the
    // first-paint targets below already carry.
    //
    // ATH GOES TO ITS VOLUMES PAGE, not to a generic grid — Tom, 2026-09-11:
    // "anytime all of the catalogue is showing on a Starmap or Across the
    // Horizon, it has to pop back to this… the volumes page." The curator map
    // is a map too, and ath-all-releases is its grid.
    // ⚠⚠ NOT _narrowNow() — THE CLASS-FIELD-ORDER TRAP, AND THIS FILE WARNS
    // ABOUT IT IN THREE OTHER PLACES (LABELS, JOURNAL, and the `state =
    // this.arrival(…)` note itself). I walked straight into it anyway.
    //
    // `state` is instance field #3 and `VPS` is field #11, so arrival() runs
    // DURING field initialisation, when this.VPS is still undefined.
    // _narrowNow() reads `this.VPS[st.vp][0]`, and DCLogic's constructor
    // pre-sets state to {} — so st.vp is undefined and the expression is
    // undefined[undefined]. That threw during construction, which is why the
    // whole site rendered blank on EVERY route and every width, desktop
    // included: nothing survived to paint.
    //
    // window.innerWidth is the only honest source here in any case. At first
    // paint there is no measured winW and no chosen vp yet — those are what
    // _narrowNow() prefers, and neither exists this early. 700 is the same
    // threshold _narrowNow() uses.
    //
    // ⚠ NOTHING CALLED FROM arrival() MAY TOUCH A FIELD DECLARED BELOW `state`.
    // The other eleven phoneNoMap() call sites are in ordinary methods that run
    // post-construction and are fine; arrival() is the one reached from the
    // field initialiser.
    if (arrivalNarrow) {
      // 'star' only. ath-map is ATH's LANDING PAGE, not a view mode — a route's
      // name is not its kind, and this arm used to delete the page (2026-09-15).
      if (s.route === 'star') s = Object.assign({}, s, { route: 'catalogue', view: 'releases', phoneMapNote: 1 });
    }
    // ── FIRST-PAINT TARGETS (2026-09-03) ───────────────────────────────────
    // __DC_RECORD = <PAIRS index>  → the record page, that record locked.
    // __DC_ARTIST = '<name>'       → the artist page for that name.
    // First-paint STATE, not post-paint toggles — starClick(rec) is the latter
    // and is wrong for a cold arrival, which is why these exist. Record wins if
    // both are set. Either may be a prop of the same name. Unknown values are
    // ignored: the arrival label's landing renders and nothing throws.
    const rec = p.record != null ? p.record : w.__DC_RECORD;
    const art = p.artist != null ? p.artist : w.__DC_ARTIST;
    // ── TWO MORE ARRIVAL KEYS (2026-09-04, D3) ─────────────────────────────
    // __DC_ARTICLE = '<slug>'  → the journal article page, that piece open.
    // __DC_TRACK   = 'v<vol>d<drop>t<idx>' or a plain track index on the
    //                current drop → the ATH track page, that track selected.
    // Old journal and track URLs could redirect to the section but not to the
    // item, so a shared link lost the thing it named. Same precedence as the
    // other two: a prop beats the global, and record still wins over all of
    // them because a locked record is the most specific arrival.
    const artcl = p.article != null ? p.article : w.__DC_ARTICLE;
    const trk = p.track != null ? p.track : w.__DC_TRACK;
    // __DC_CARD = '<card slug>' → the ATH deck, that card open (2026-09-11,
    // Tom). The refresh half of the same ask: goToNav handles a card that
    // arrives through the channel, but a COLD load of /curator-map has no
    // channel yet, and without this it would land on the deck's default card
    // and read as a broken link. Least specific of the five, so a record, an
    // artist, an article or a track still wins.
    const card = p.card != null ? p.card : w.__DC_CARD;
    // Most specific first: a locked record, then an artist, then a journal
    // article, then a track. An unknown value is ignored rather than guessed —
    // a bad deep link should land on the section, never on the wrong item.
    if (rec != null && Number.isInteger(+rec) && +rec >= 0) {
      s = Object.assign({}, s, { route: 'record', recIdx: +rec, lock: +rec, prev: 'catalogue', open: true });
    } else if (typeof art === 'string' && art) {
      s = Object.assign({}, s, { route: 'artist', artist: art, prev: 'landing' });
    } else if (typeof artcl === 'string' && artcl) {
      // ⚠ A SLUG CANNOT BE RESOLVED HERE (2026-09-09, code audit part 1).
      // JOURNAL is a class field declared BELOW `state = this.arrival(…)`, so
      // this read `this['DEFAULT_JOURNAL']` as undefined and `|| []` turned that
      // into an empty list: every slug missed and every slug link landed on the
      // journal index instead of the piece it named — silently, which is worse
      // than throwing. An index needs no table and still resolves here; a slug
      // rides on state and is resolved in componentDidMount, where data() can
      // see the table. The route is right immediately; only the index waits.
      const idx = Number.isInteger(+artcl) ? +artcl : -1;
      s = Object.assign({}, s, { route: 'ath-article', theme: 'light' },
        idx > -1 ? { article: idx } : { artSlug: String(artcl) });
    } else if (card != null && String(card) && this.athCardIndex(card) != null) {
      // ── ?card= IS A RETARGET ON A PHONE, NOT A DELETION (2026-09-15) ───────
      // The desktop target here is the STAR route (the ATH column, view
      // 'ath-curator', addressed by athCard) — and 'star' genuinely has no
      // phone. So this one cannot just drop its narrow arm the way the other
      // doors can: that would leave an unconditional route:'star' and put a
      // phone ON the star map, which is the opposite failure.
      //
      // It retargets instead, to the same card on ATH's own landing. The two
      // orders line up for the first seven — the-idea, the-core-idea,
      // curator-map, the-drops, broadcast, journal, subscribe — so the slug's
      // index IS the landing's panel. The eighth slug, 'releases', has no
      // landing panel and is the releases page by name, so it goes there.
      //
      // No phoneMapNote on the card arm any more: nothing was taken away, the
      // card the link named is what opens.
      const cardIdx = this.athCardIndex(card);
      s = Object.assign({}, s, arrivalNarrow
        ? (cardIdx >= 7
            ? { route: 'ath-all-releases', theme: 'light', labelKey: 'across-the-horizon', phoneMapNote: 1 }
            : { route: 'ath-map', panel: cardIdx, theme: 'light', labelKey: 'across-the-horizon' })
        : {
          route: 'star', view: 'ath-curator', athCard: cardIdx,
          theme: 'light', labelKey: 'across-the-horizon'
        });
    } else if (trk != null && String(trk)) {
      // 'v2d3t1' names volume, drop and track; a bare number is a track index
      // on whatever drop the deck already holds.
      const m = String(trk).match(/^v(\d+)d(\d+)t(\d+)$/i);
      s = m
        ? Object.assign({}, s, { route: 'ath-track', dropKey: 'd' + (+m[1] - 1) + '-' + (+m[2] - 1), trackIdx: +m[3], theme: 'light' })
        : (Number.isInteger(+trk)
          ? Object.assign({}, s, { route: 'ath-track', trackIdx: +trk, theme: 'light' })
          : Object.assign({}, s, { route: 'ath-all-releases', theme: 'light' }));
    }
    // ── §1 · THE LABEL IS RESOLVED HERE, ONCE, AND NEVER AGAIN ──────────────
    // ⚠ THIS RUNS LAST ON PURPOSE, and the ordering is the whole correctness
    // argument. Everything above may still change the route: the label branch
    // sets ATH's default landing, __DC_ROUTE overrides it, and the four
    // deep-link openers override that. Resolving the label any earlier would
    // read a route that had not settled — a host publishing `ath-journal` with
    // no label key would resolve to Northern Spy and then STICK, because from
    // here on labelKey is authoritative and nothing re-derives it.
    //
    // WHY IT IS RESOLVED AT ALL rather than left null: `dropLabelKey.ts`
    // returns null for ATH today, so in production ATH arrives with no key. If
    // labelKey stayed null, labelKeyOf() would fall back to the route prefix at
    // every read — and the moment any navigator left an `ath-` route the answer
    // would flip to Northern Spy. That is the reported blocker, and it would
    // have survived behind pinLabel(): pinLabel only covers the navigators that
    // call it, and eight route-setters do not (openAlbum, the four browse
    // commits, the ⋮ menu's artistPage / artistMap, the ath-track artist link).
    //
    // Resolved once here, labelKey is non-null from first paint, so setState's
    // merge carries it through every one of those setters whether they know
    // about any of this or not. That is the difference between fixing a class
    // and fixing the instances of it you happened to find.
    if (!s.labelKey) {
      s = Object.assign({}, s, {
        labelKey: key
          || (typeof s.route === 'string' && s.route.indexOf('ath-') === 0 ? 'across-the-horizon' : null)
          || (this.labelKeys()[s.label == null ? 0 : s.label])
          || 'northern-spy'
      });
    }
    return s;
  }

  /* ══ DC-DEFAULTS:START run=01 drop=QUEUE ══ */
  // [title, artist, seconds, hasAudio, recIdx, trackIdx, cued?]
  //
  // ⚠ THE MOCK WAS FOUR WIDE AND THE RUNTIME TUPLE IS SEVEN (2026-09-11).
  // That gap is why the queue row's unconditional hatch survived: qOnIdx reads
  // r[4]/r[5] and cued reads q[6], but nothing in this file's own data ever
  // supplied them, so every row fell to the no-artwork fallback and the bug
  // looked like the design. A mock narrower than the tuple it stands in for
  // does not just under-test — it actively hides the thing it is meant to show.
  //
  // Rows 0–8 carry their real PAIRS index, so the tool paints real sleeves.
  // The last three are DELIBERATELY without one: those titles have no PAIRS
  // row, so they exercise the hatch as the genuine fallback it is, and both
  // branches are visible on one screen.
  DEFAULT_QUEUE = [
    ['Passing Fires', 'Jeremy Cunningham', 254, true, 0, 0],
    ['Love Song', 'Clarice Jensen', 372, true, 1, 0],
    ['Disquiet', 'The Necks', 1284, true, 2, 0],
    ['Inscape', 'Balmorhea', 298, false, 3, 0],
    ['Waltz for Sarah', 'Rachel Grimes', 221, true, 4, 0],
    ['Around the Lake', 'marine eyes', 186, true, 5, 0],
    ['Par', 'Jessica Roch', 243, true, 6, 0],
    ['Metal Flower', 'Adjuster66', 331, true, 7, 0],
    ['Kites', 'Jeremy Cunningham', 267, true, 8, 0],
    ['Second Sight', 'Clarice Jensen', 402, true],
    ['Low Ceiling', 'The Necks', 918, false],
    ['Cardinal', 'Balmorhea', 275, true]
  ];

  /* ══ DC-DEFAULTS:END run=01 drop=QUEUE ══ */
  // THE PAINTED WINDOW. In the tool the mock catalogue paints 12 (the drawer's
  // designed resting depth). ON THE SITE THERE IS NO WINDOW: the drawer and the
  // grid render every row of PAIRS and scroll (Tom/build, 2026-09-03: "the
  // Releases list stops at 12"). CAP stays as a constellation limit only.
  CAP = 12;
  paintCap() {
    const site = !!(this.props.site || (typeof window !== 'undefined' && window.__DC_SITE));
    return site ? Infinity : (this.CAP || 12);
  }

  // The journal's four adapted zine pieces, ONE source of truth: the article
  // rail, the deck's teaser card and the expanded journal route all read this.
  // [slug, kind, title, dek, byline, image, where]
  // ── THE ARTICLE BODY, IN BLOCKS (2026-09-05) ─────────────────────────────
  // ARTICLE_BODY[slug] = [[kind, ...fields], …]. The frame drew a specimen —
  // grey bars and "Body copy drops in here" — which meant the four pieces we
  // hold could not render at all. Six kinds, because those are the six the
  // pieces actually use and no more:
  //
  //   ['p',     text]                          a paragraph
  //   ['speech', speaker, text]                a quoted answer, speaker named
  //   ['aside',  text]                         a bracketed aside
  //   ['entry',  name, place, blurb, photoUrl] a photographed list entry
  //   ['note',   text]                         a closing note
  //   ['signoff', text]                        the signoff
  //
  // Empty here on purpose: ARTICLE_BODY is editorial-class, so the body is
  // whatever the site sends and never something this file wrote.
  /* ══ DC-DEFAULTS:START run=02 keep=ARTICLE_BODY,JOURNAL ══ */
  DEFAULT_ARTICLE_BODY = {};
  DEFAULT_JOURNAL = [
    ['mutation-stimulates-evolution-yuka-honda', 'Interview', 'Mutation Stimulates Evolution: Yuka Honda In Conversation',
     'Yuka Honda talks with Jason Woodbury about imaginal language, sampling as intuition, and how her Volume One piece came together.',
     'Jason Woodbury, in conversation with Yuka Honda', 'public/ath/journal-honda.jpg', 'Volume One · Drop 3'],
    ['100-proof-steel-aged-15-years-tommy-white', 'Review', '100 Proof Steel, Aged 15 Years',
     'JR Bohannon on the teenage pedal steel record that got there decades before the words “ambient country” existed.',
     'Review by JR Bohannon', 'public/ath/journal-tommy-white.jpg', 'Zine Vol. 1'],
    ['stelth-ulvang-top-five-notable-libraries', 'List', 'Stelth Ulvang’s Top Five Notable Libraries',
     'Touring the world with the Lumineers, Ulvang found his reset in the quietest rooms he could find.',
     'Stelth Ulvang · five photographs', 'public/ath/journal-stelth-ulvang.jpg', 'Zine Vol. 1'],
    ['cowboy-sadness', 'Interview', 'Cowboy Sadness',
     'Seltzer brand loyalties, class divides, microplastics, and the unmatched joy of cracking a can.',
     'Cowboy Sadness is Peter Silberman, David Moore & Nicholas Principe', 'public/ath/journal-cowboy-sadness.jpg', 'Zine Vol. 1']
  ];

  /* ══ DC-DEFAULTS:END run=02 keep=ARTICLE_BODY,JOURNAL ══ */
  // Per-drop "Get the music" state: the popup was one shared boolean, so
  // opening it on one drop opened it on every drop in the run at once. Keyed
  // by the drop's own title instead.
  getMusicOn(st, key) {
    return {
      getOpen: st.getMusic === key,
      getClick: (e) => { if (e && e.stopPropagation) e.stopPropagation(); this.setState({ getMusic: st.getMusic === key ? null : key }); }
    };
  }

  // ── ONE artist mosaic, used by BOTH indexes ───────────────────────────────
  // The ATH artists index and the four labels' artists index are the same wall:
  // N independent columns, shortest-column-first packing, every photo keeping
  // its own measured ratio, the same caption, hover and ⋮ behaviour. Only the
  // ◆ Curator tag is ATH-specific, and it rides on the item as `curator`.
  // Both call this, so a change to one IS a change to the other.
  //
  // items: [{ name, src, ar, focus, curator }]  ·  o: { narrow, W, pad, mhover }
  buildMosaic(items, o) {
    const narrow = o.narrow;
    const gap = narrow ? 8 : 14;
    const colW = o.W - (narrow ? 28 : (o.pad || 48));
    const n = narrow ? 2 : (colW > 1300 ? 4 : 3);
    const tileW = (colW - gap * (n - 1)) / n;
    const cols = [];
    for (let i = 0; i < n; i++) cols.push({ items: [], h: 0 });
    items.forEach(it => {
      // A missing ratio must not collapse a tile to nothing: 1.0 is the square
      // default the repo's own photos mostly are.
      const ar = it.ar == null || !it.ar ? 1 : it.ar;
      let best = 0;
      for (let i = 1; i < n; i++) if (cols[i].h < cols[best].h) best = i;
      cols[best].items.push({ it: it, ar: ar });
      cols[best].h += tileW / ar + gap;
    });
    return cols.map((c, ci) => ({
      key: 'mc' + ci,
      style: { flex: '1 1 0', minWidth: 0, display: 'flex', flexDirection: 'column', gap: gap + 'px' },
      items: c.items.map((row, ai) => {
        const a = row.it;
        const hov = o.mhover === a.name;
      // The tile's record for hover audio: this artist's newest record in PAIRS.
      const firstRec = this.recFor(a.name);
        const has = !!a.src;
        return {
          key: 'm' + ci + '-' + ai,
          name: a.name,
          // §1a · an artist tile is addressable and now says so. /artist/<slug>
          // is the singular form your table measures at 200 — never /artists/.
          href: this.addr('artist', a.name),
          meta: '',
          // The photograph is filled by tileRef as the tile approaches (C1).
          // The panel colour underneath is what a not-yet-filled tile shows, so
          // the wall's geometry is complete from the first paint and nothing
          // reflows as the images arrive.
          tile: {
            position: 'relative', width: '100%', aspectRatio: String(this.arOf(row.ar)), minHeight: '120px',
            borderRadius: '7px', overflow: 'hidden', boxSizing: 'border-box',
            border: '1px solid ' + (hov ? 'var(--goldsoft)' : 'var(--hair20)'),
            cursor: 'pointer',
            background: has
              ? 'var(--tile)'
              : 'repeating-linear-gradient(' + (125 + ai * 17) + 'deg, var(--tile) 0 8px, transparent 8px 16px)',
            display: 'flex', alignItems: 'flex-end'
          },
          tileRef: this.lazyRef(a.src, a.focus),
          scrim: {
            position: 'absolute', left: 0, right: 0, bottom: 0, height: '40%',
            background: has ? 'linear-gradient(rgba(0,0,0,0), rgba(0,0,0,' + (hov ? 0.7 : 0.34) + '))' : 'none',
            pointerEvents: 'none'
          },
          // The photo is the content, not the decoration. The name sits at
          // normal weight and partial strength and only comes fully up on
          // hover — reading in is a second step, not the first thing.
          caption: {
            position: 'relative', display: 'flex', flexDirection: 'column',
            padding: narrow ? '6px 8px' : '10px 12px', minWidth: 0
          },
          nameStyle: {
            fontSize: narrow ? '9.5px' : '12px', fontWeight: 400, letterSpacing: '0.01em',
            lineHeight: 1.3,
            color: has ? (hov ? '#f3f1ea' : 'rgba(243,241,234,0.78)') : (hov ? 'var(--ink)' : 'var(--ink70)'),
            textShadow: has ? '0 1px 6px rgba(0,0,0,0.5)' : 'none',
            transition: 'color 160ms ease',
            overflow: 'hidden', textOverflow: 'ellipsis', whiteSpace: 'normal'
          },
          metaStyle: { display: 'none' },
          // RULING 1 — the pending slot is retired. Both kept as no-ops so the
          // template's two holes still resolve; a photoless artist never
          // reaches this packer now, so neither can render.
          slotNote: { display: 'none' },
          slotLabel: '',
          // ATH only: curators are marked, not sectioned. The by-drop grouping
          // is the curator map's job.
          curatorTag: a.curator
            ? {
                position: 'absolute', left: narrow ? '8px' : '10px', top: narrow ? '8px' : '10px',
                display: 'flex', alignItems: 'center', gap: '5px',
                height: narrow ? '20px' : '22px', padding: narrow ? '0 7px' : '0 9px',
                boxSizing: 'border-box', borderRadius: '4px',
                background: 'rgba(6,7,10,0.62)', border: '1px solid rgba(219,191,61,0.55)',
                fontFamily: "'JetBrains Mono', monospace", fontSize: narrow ? '7.5px' : '8.5px',
                letterSpacing: '0.16em', textTransform: 'uppercase', color: '#DBBF3D',
                whiteSpace: 'nowrap', pointerEvents: 'none'
              }
            : { display: 'none' },
          // Bottom-right of the photo, opposite the caption so it never sits on
          // the name. Appears on hover; on a phone it is always there, since
          // there is no hover to reveal it with.
          dotsStyle: {
            position: 'absolute', right: '8px', bottom: '8px',
            display: 'flex', alignItems: 'center', justifyContent: 'center',
            width: '30px', height: '30px', boxSizing: 'border-box', borderRadius: '5px',
            background: 'rgba(6,7,10,0.55)', border: '1px solid rgba(243,241,234,0.22)',
            color: '#f3f1ea', fontSize: '15px', cursor: 'pointer',
            opacity: (narrow || hov) ? 1 : 0, transition: 'opacity 160ms ease'
          },
          // The menu needs to know WHOSE photo it opened on, and mhover clears
          // the moment the pointer leaves the tile for the menu — so the name is
          // stashed on click instead.
          dots: (e) => { this.setState({ ctxArtist: a.name }); return this.openCtx('artistTile', null)(e); },
          // Hover audio on a wall of artists: the tile is a handle on that artist's
          // newest record (firstRec), same chain as a grid cover.
              // ⚠ NO HOVER AUDIO ON AN ARTIST PHOTO (2026-09-09, Tom, revising
              // himself inside one message: "let's remove the hover audio on artist
              // photos altogether… that's a change just on album covers and track
              // listings"). The tile still lights and still carries its menu; what
              // goes is the sound, because a photograph is not a record and the wall
              // was previewing that artist's newest one as if it were.
              enter: () => this.hoverEnter({ mhover: a.name }),
              leave: () => this.setState({ mhover: null }),
          // ATH tiles go to the ATH artist page, label tiles to the label one:
          // same wall, different destination.
          // §1a · preventDefault keeps the in-page behaviour with no host at
          // all, so the design tool still works and your intercept has nothing
          // to race. The href above is the destination; this is the transition.
          click: this.navTo(() => this.setState(this.pinLabel({ route: o.route || 'artist', artist: a.name })))
        };
      })
    }));
  }

  // The drop behind the release page: whichever the user clicked, defaulting to
  // the newest. Returns the raw record plus the ATH-specific copy — the
  // curator's note on the three tracks — where that copy exists.
  // See athDrop's note. Returns [text, attribution] or null — never invented,
  // only found. The order is specificity, not preference.
  // ── THE QUOTE FOR ONE SINGLE (2026-09-06, Tom) ───────────────────────────
  // ⚠ FOR CLAUDE CODE — THIS IS A DATA PULL, NOT A PENDING FEATURE.
  //
  // Every ATH single has a quoted passage FROM THE ARTIST in the "About this
  // album" text on that single's own Bandcamp page, in quotation marks. That
  // is the source. There is nothing to write and nothing to wait for: the
  // pages are already crawled, the text is already there, and it is the
  // series' signature — the artist saying what they made.
  //
  // Feed it as ATH_QUOTES, keyed by whichever you have:
  //   'v<vol>d<drop>t<idx>'  the single itself — PREFERRED, it is exact
  //   '<artist name>'        that artist's words
  // Each entry [text, attribution]; attribution defaults to the key's name.
  //
  // ⚠ AND THE SAME GOES FOR ATH_STATEMENTS AND THE TRACK CREDITS. Every ATH
  // artist page reads "statement pending" and shows no credits on the live
  // site. That is not the design waiting to be designed — it is these two
  // tables never having been fed. The statement is the same Bandcamp passage
  // (athStatementFor already falls back to ATH_QUOTES for exactly this
  // reason), and the credits are the written/performed lines from the same
  // block. A page that says "pending" over text we have crawled is worse than
  // one that says nothing.
  athQuoteForTrack(r, ci, name) {
    const Q = this.data('ATH_QUOTES') || {};
    const keys = ['v' + (r.vi + 1) + 'd' + r.num + 't' + (ci + 1), name];
    for (let i = 0; i < keys.length; i++) {
      const hit = Q[keys[i]];
      if (hit && hit[0]) return hit[0];
    }
    // The artist's statement is the same passage by another name.
    return this.athStatementFor(name) || '';
  }
  athQuoteFor(r) {
    const Q = this.data('ATH_QUOTES') || {};
    const keys = ['d' + r.vi + '-' + r.di, r.curator].concat(r.guests || []);
    for (let i = 0; i < keys.length; i++) {
      const hit = Q[keys[i]];
      if (hit && hit[0]) return [hit[0], hit[1] || keys[i]];
    }
    return null;
  }
  // ── AN ARTIST'S STATEMENT IS THEIR OWN WORDS, WHEREVER THEY ARE ──────────
  // (2026-09-05.) ATH_STATEMENTS is empty, so every ATH artist page showed the
  // pending card. But a curator's ATH_QUOTES entry IS a statement — it is that
  // person writing about the drop they made — so the artist page reads it
  // rather than leaving the series' signature feature blank next to text we
  // already hold. STATEMENTS still wins where it exists, because it is written
  // for this surface.
  athStatementFor(name) {
    if (!name) return null;
    const S = this.data('ATH_STATEMENTS') || {};
    if (S[name]) return S[name];
    const Q = this.data('ATH_QUOTES') || {};
    return (Q[name] && Q[name][0]) ? Q[name][0] : null;
  }
  athDrop(st) {
    const all = [];
    this.data('VOLS').forEach((v, vi) => (v.drops || []).forEach((d, di) => {
      all.push({ vi: vi, di: di, num: d[0], curator: d[1], track: d[2], guests: d[3] || [] });
    }));
    all.reverse();
    const key = st.dropKey;
    const r = (key ? all.filter(x => ('d' + x.vi + '-' + x.di) === key)[0] : null) || all[0] || { vi: 1, di: 0, num: 1, curator: 'Curator pending', track: 'Title pending', guests: [] };
    // ── THE QUOTE IS THE SERIES' FEATURE, SO LOOK EVERYWHERE FOR IT ─────────
    // (2026-09-05, Tom: "I want to push it to show up.") This read
    // ATH_QUOTES[curator] and nothing else, and only two curators have an
    // entry — so fifteen of sixteen drops showed the pending line, which reads
    // as "the feature is broken" rather than "this drop's words aren't in yet".
    //
    // Three keys now, most specific first:
    //   ATH_QUOTES['d<vol>-<drop>'] — a quote written for THIS drop
    //   ATH_QUOTES[curator]         — the curator's words, as before
    //   ATH_QUOTES[track artist]    — an invited artist's, where they wrote one
    // Each entry is [text, attribution]; attribution falls back to the name the
    // entry was found under, so a quote can never appear unattributed.
    const q = this.athQuoteFor(r);
    // Bandcamp's "about this release" text, keyed 'd<vol>-<drop>'. EMPTY on
    // purpose: this is the scraper's landing spot, one entry per drop, pulled
    // verbatim from the release page. Nothing is invented to fill it — an unset
    // key renders the pending line, so a real pull is always visibly different
    // from a placeholder.
    const ABOUT = {};
    const aboutKey = 'd' + r.vi + '-' + r.di;
    return { r: r, quote: q ? q[0] : null, attr: q ? q[1] : null, about: ABOUT[aboutKey] || null };
  }

  // the press feed renders, and whether the "what we do" band is the full
  // service list (parent only) or the long-form version of that one service.

  // Which drop an ATH artist belongs to, their role in it, and who else is on
  // it. An ATH artist is defined by the drop that invited them — that is the
  // whole premise of the series — so the page is built around it.
  athArtistCtx(name) {
    let found = null;
    (this.data('VOLS') || []).forEach((v, vi) => (v.drops || []).forEach((d, di) => {
      if (found) return;
      const curator = d[1], guests = d[3] || [];
      const all = [curator].concat(guests);
      if (all.some(n => this.slug(String(n)) === this.slug(name))) {
        found = { vi: vi, di: di, num: d[0], track: d[2], curator: curator, guests: guests, isCurator: this.slug(curator) === this.slug(name) };
      }
    }));
    return found;
  }

  // ── Artist statements ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────
  // Keyed by artist name. Each is the QUOTED PASSAGE lifted out of that artist's
  // own Bandcamp single-page description — them speaking in the first person
  // about the series — not the whole description, which belongs to "About this
  // release" on the release page.
  //
  // EMPTY ON PURPOSE: nothing here is invented. An unset name renders the
  // pending card at the same size, so a real pull is always visibly different
  // from a placeholder and the layout does not shift when one lands.
  /* ══ DC-DEFAULTS:START run=04 keep=ATH_STATEMENTS ══ */
  DEFAULT_ATH_STATEMENTS = {};

  /* ══ DC-DEFAULTS:END run=04 keep=ATH_STATEMENTS ══ */
  // ── Artist notes ───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
  // The one-line note per ATH artist, from the curator map's Drop 3 lineup —
  // the only documented one. INDEX[4] is the artist's HOMETOWN, not a bio, and
  // reading it into a bio slot printed "Austin, Texas" as a two-word biography.
  // Keyed by name; an unset name says the bio is pending rather than showing a
  // location dressed as prose.
  /* ══ DC-DEFAULTS:START run=05 keep=ATH_BIOS ══ */
  DEFAULT_ATH_BIOS = {
    'Balmorhea': 'Curated this drop and plays it — Rob Lowe and Michael A. Muller, from Austin.',
    'Rachel Grimes': 'Pianist and composer, Louisville. An improvisation in the spirit of the traditional dance form.',
    'Takeshi Nishimoto': 'Guitarist, Berlin. Recorded in a two-day session in Tokyo, March 2025.'
  };

  /* ══ DC-DEFAULTS:END run=05 keep=ATH_BIOS ══ */
  artistBio(name) { return this.data('ATH_BIOS')[name] || null; }
  // INDEX[4] belongs here instead: a hometown line, labelled as one.
  artistHome(name) {
    const row = this.data('INDEX').filter(x => x[0] === name)[0];
    return (row && row[4]) ? row[4] : '';
  }

  mmss(s) { return Math.floor(s / 60) + ':' + String(s % 60).padStart(2, '0'); }

  /* ══ DC-DEFAULTS:START run=06 drop=PAIRS ══ */
  DEFAULT_PAIRS = [
    // [artist, title, meta, cover, altCover, releaseState?, recordId?, address?]
  //
  // ⚠ INDEX 5 CARRIES TWO FACTS AND THAT IS A KNOWN DEFECT (2026-09-09, code
  // audit part 1). It is documented here as releaseState — 'preorder' drives
  // recFormatChips — and it is ALSO read as a hasAudio boolean in three places
  // (`p[5] === false`), which is why a second, contradictory tuple line exists
  // further down the file. Both readers are live, so neither can be deleted
  // today.
  //
  // THE RESOLUTION, agreed with code the same day: REC_TRACKS[4] becomes the
  // audio state, PER TRACK ('full' | 'sample' | 'none'). When it lands, every
  // `p[5] === false` read retires and index 5 is releaseState alone. Until then
  // treat a boolean at [5] as legacy and never write a new reader of it.
    // ── §2 of r47 · INDEX 7 IS THE RECORD'S OWN ADDRESS ───────────────────
    // A stored string, e.g. '/album/suss-counting-sunsets'. addr('album') reads
    // it and never derives when it is present. Fill index 7 and 118 of your 199
    // 404s go with it; index 6 (Album.id) is read as a second choice and yields
    // '/album/<id>'. With neither, the SITE emits no anchor at all rather than a
    // guess — see addr('album') for why that is the safer failure.
    //
    // The mock rows below carry no index 7 on purpose: opened on its own this
    // file derives (tool only), so the design tool keeps working links while
    // the site never ships one that was invented.
    // meta is 'Label · CAT# · Date' — LABEL FIRST (2026-09-04, ledger 32),
    // because that is how a record identifies itself on its sleeve, and the
    // label is the fixed thing on a page that changes record to record. The
    // file renders the string verbatim; absent parts take their separator with
    // them. Say the word and the order moves — it is one array on the build's
    // side and this comment on mine.
    // recordId (2026-09-04, ledger 20) is OPTIONAL and is the join key for
    // REC_TRACKS / REC_NOTES / REC_COVERS / REC_CONTRIBUTORS. Supply it: four
    // live records are titled "S/T" and two "Split", so a display title is not
    // a unique key. Absent → the title is used, as before.
    // releaseState (2026-09-04) is OPTIONAL: 'preorder' where the site's
    // preorder.isPreorder(Album.releaseTs) is true; absent or anything else
    // means released. The album page's kicker is the only reader.
    ['Jeremy Cunningham', 'Passing Fires', 'Northern Spy · NS-118 · 18 Mar 2026', 'remote/bcbits/a2652378460_10.jpg', 'public/ath/vol1-lp-jacket.jpg'],
    ['Clarice Jensen', 'Love Song', 'Northern Spy · NS-117 · 06 Feb 2026', 'remote/bcbits/a0481107880_16.jpg', 'public/ath/vol1-vinyl.jpg'],

  /* ══ DC-DEFAULTS:END run=06 drop=PAIRS ══ */
  // Placements, so "In constellation with" can be DERIVED rather than faked.
  // [scene cluster, genre clusters, label] — the three similarity axes the
  // real atlas actually stores per album (galaxyId/clusterId pairs); mood and

  // Scenes come from CATS['Scenes']; labels from CATS['Labels'].

  // (2026-09-05). Every mock row had said 'Northern Spy', so a set could never
  // span more than one label here and the caption correctly hid itself — which

  // applied to my own mock: a fixture that only shows the happy path hides the
  // behaviour you are trying to check.
  /* ══ DC-DEFAULTS:START run=07 drop=PLACE ══ */

  /* ══ DC-DEFAULTS:END run=07 drop=PLACE ══ */
  // Ported from lib/atlas/deriveSimilarArtists.ts (the album page's chip reads
  // the same function through ConstellationWithButton). Four picks, never five;
  // the target is always excluded; tie-broken by discography size then
  // alphabetically so the list is stable across reloads.
  //
  // TIERS: scene first, then genre. Tom's 2026-08-06 note moved scene up from
  // tier 3 and label down from tier 1 — "label-mates are a RETAIL relationship".
  // Label is not a tier here at all: a label-only match carries no shared genre
  // or scene, and evaluatePick's gate drops exactly those, so the tier could
  // never produce a surviving pick.
  // ── F3 · THE CONSTELLATION ARRIVES CURATED (2026-09-04) ──────────────────
  // The live constellation is staff-curated links PLUS a machine layer MINUS
  // editorial hides. similarArtists() below could only ever compute the third
  // tier — the live site's own FALLBACK — from artist tags, so the design's
  // neighbours were the weakest of the three and never honoured a hide. It is
  // also data logic in the file, which rule 2 forbids outright.
  //
  // CONSTELLATION[name] arrives as the resolved list, in its curated order.
  // similarArtists() survives ONLY as the pre-catalogue fallback and retires
  // with the table; nothing new may call it.
  constellationFor(name) {
    const supplied = (this.data('CONSTELLATION') || {})[name];
    if (supplied) return supplied;
    return this.similarArtists(name);
  }
  similarArtists(target) {
    const me = this.data('PLACE')[target];
    if (!me) return [];
    const albums = {};
    this.data('INDEX').forEach(a => { albums[a[0]] = a[3] == null ? 1 : a[3]; });
    const picks = [];
    Object.keys(this.data('PLACE')).forEach(name => {
      if (name === target) return;
      const p = this.data('PLACE')[name];
      const sameScene = p[0] === me[0];
      const sameGenre = p[1].some(g => me[1].indexOf(g) > -1);
      if (!sameScene && !sameGenre) return;
      picks.push({ name: name, tier: sameScene ? 0 : 1, count: albums[name] == null ? 1 : albums[name] });
    });
    picks.sort((a, b) => (a.tier - b.tier) || (b.count - a.count) || a.name.localeCompare(b.name));
    return picks.slice(0, 4).map(p => p.name);
  }

  // THE BROWSE TAXONOMY SPLITS IN TWO (§63). CATS is DESIGN: the groups, their
  // order and their colours. CAT_VALUES is DATA: what is in each group and how
  // many. The site injects __DC_DATA.CAT_VALUES only and never re-types a group
  // name or a colour:
  //
  //     CAT_VALUES = { Genres: [['Ambient', 41], ['Drone', 17], …], Scenes: [...], … }
  //
  // Each entry is [name, count]; count is optional. A group absent from
  // CAT_VALUES falls back to the file's own list for that group (below).
  // catRows() is the ONLY reader — it merges [group, count, values, colour] per
  // row, so the panel never sees two authorities.
  /* ══ DC-DEFAULTS:START run=08 drop=CAT_VALUES ══ */
  DEFAULT_CAT_VALUES = {};
  /* ══ DC-DEFAULTS:END run=08 drop=CAT_VALUES ══ */
  // ══ RULING (Tom, 2026-09-03): THE BROWSE MENU IS LABEL-AGNOSTIC. It shows the
  // whole atlas — every group, every value, every label — regardless of which
  // label's chrome you arrived under. Do not scope CATS or CAT_VALUES to the
  // arrival label; do not hide Labels rows for "other" labels. The results a
  // pick produces are the ATLAS's results. (The label scoping that DOES exist —
  // INDEX for the artists wall — is a different surface and a different rule.)
  catRows() {
    const groups = this.data('CATS');
    const vals = this.data('CAT_VALUES') || {};
    // ── ⚠ THE COLUMN DRAWS `CATS`, NOT `CAT_VALUES` (2026-09-06) ────────────
    // Reported as "one row from eight populated categories". The cause is that
    // this maps CATS — the design's ordering and colours — and looks values up
    // BY GROUP NAME. So a group only appears if CATS names it: eight populated
    // CAT_VALUES keys with a one-row CATS renders one row, exactly as seen.
    //
    // CAT_VALUES is the data and CATS is presentation, so the data should be
    // able to introduce a group the design has not met. Any CAT_VALUES key with
    // no CATS row is appended here, after the design's own order, in a neutral
    // ink. Nothing is dropped for want of a colour.
    const known = groups.map(g => g[0]);
    const extra = Object.keys(vals)
      .filter(k => known.indexOf(k) < 0 && (vals[k] || []).length)
      .map(k => [k, String(vals[k].length), vals[k].map(x => Array.isArray(x) ? x[0] : x), 'var(--ink55)']);
    return groups.concat(extra).map(g => {
      const v = vals[g[0]];
      if (!v || !v.length) {
        // No real values for this group. In the tool the file's own list shows
        // (real for Labels/Formats/Scenes, "Pending — …" for the rest). On the
        // site a pending placeholder is fiction: the group stays, empty.
        // On the site, only DESIGN-KNOWN groups keep the file's own list:
        // Labels (the five marks) and Formats (LP/CS/CD/DL). Every other group's
        // values are catalogue — Artists, Genres, Moods, Scenes, Instruments,
        // Featured — and render EMPTY until CAT_VALUES supplies them. The
        // Artists group listed eight mock names on the live site (caught in
        // review); "pending" placeholders were already suppressed, real-looking
        // mock lists were not. This is the rule, not a placeholder check.
        const site = !!(this.props.site || (typeof window !== 'undefined' && window.__DC_SITE));
        const designKnown = g[0] === 'Labels' || g[0] === 'Formats';
        return (site && !designKnown) ? [g[0], '', [], g[3]] : g;
      }
      const names = v.map(x => Array.isArray(x) ? x[0] : x);
      const count = v.length;
      return [g[0], String(count), names, g[3]];
    });
  }
  /* ══ DC-DEFAULTS:START run=09 keep=CATS,NEWS,TITLES ══ */
  DEFAULT_CATS = [
    ['Labels', '5', ['Northern Spy', 'NNA Tapes', 'Egghunt', 'Across the Horizon', 'Whited Sepulchre'], 'var(--ink55)'],
    ['Genres', '37', ['Pending — from the genre table'], '#7ac0a8'],
    ['Moods', '17', ['Pending — from the mood table'], '#4d6fa3'],

    ['Instruments', '15', ['Pending — from the instrument table'], '#5fb8c8'],
    ['Formats', '4', ['LP', 'Cassette', 'CD', 'Digital'], 'var(--ink55)'],

    // ⚠ NOTHING ELSE READ IT. catRows builds every group from this table and
    // keys on the name, so the row was its only definition — no gate, no colour
    // reference, no CAT_VALUES key. Removing the row removes the group.
    //
    // If a curated set is wanted later it is a CONSTELLATION, which this file
    // already has: a named set with an anchor and an address, rather than a
    // browse facet.
    ['Artists', '', ['Jeremy Cunningham', 'Clarice Jensen', 'The Necks', 'Balmorhea', 'Rachel Grimes', 'marine eyes', 'Jessica Roch', 'Adjuster66'], 'var(--ink55)']
  ];

  // 7l · the News feed. [kind, media, ratio, body, stamp, action, extra]
  // kind: photo | carousel | video | post | press
  DEFAULT_NEWS = [
    ['photo', 'remote/bcbits/a0481107880_10.jpg', '1', 'Passing Fires — Jeremy Cunningham. Out today on LP, cassette and download.', 'Today · 09:12', 'play', ''],

  DEFAULT_TITLES = {
    // filtered: the count line after a Browse pick. Absent → falls back to releases.
    filtered: ['Releases', '179', '179 albums'],
    // [title, desktop sub, PHONE sub]. The phone drops "12 of": the list lazy
    // loads, so a window count says nothing. What matters on one line is what
    // you are looking at and how much of it there is — the label's whole
    // catalogue, or the filter that got you here.

    artists: ['Artists', '8 with records', '8 artists'],
    tour: ['On tour', 'dates pending', 'dates pending'],

    about: ['About', 'the label', 'the label'],
    // ── radio (2026-09-09) ─────────────────────────────────────────────────

    // `|| T.releases` and the Radio column headed itself "Releases · 9 of 179" —
    // a missing row is indistinguishable from a deliberate one, which is why it

    // /^\d+$/ test) rather than counting rows. Northern Spy's alone, like the
    // menu item.
    radio: ['Radio', 'the residency', 'the residency'],
    // ── ATH's ONE COLUMN VIEW (2026-09-09, revised the same day) ────────────
    // 'ath-curator' is the deck, and its header does NOT come from here: each
    // card names itself from ATH_COL. The row is kept because titlesFor() is
    // called before the card index is read, and its fallback would otherwise
    // head the column "Releases · 9 of 179".
    //
    // ⚠ The three rows that stood here — ath-idea, ath-podcast, ath-journal —
    // are DELETED with the views they headed. Nothing can reach them: no
    // view === test, no go() target, no menu item.
    // ── (lineage) ───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
    // Without a row, titlesFor() falls through to `|| T.releases` and each of
    // these headed itself "Releases · 9 of 179" over the idea's copy, the
    // podcast strands or the journal list — the same fallback that mis-headed
    // Radio, and the same reason: a missing row is indistinguishable from a
    // deliberate one. The subs are WORDS, so countLine prints them verbatim
