    'ath-curator': ['The Curator Map', 'sixteen drops, two volumes', 'sixteen drops'],
    album: ['', '', '']
  };

  /* ══ DC-DEFAULTS:END run=09 keep=CATS,NEWS,TITLES ══ */
  rnd(seed) {
    let a = seed;
    return () => {
      a |= 0; a = a + 0x6D2B79F5 | 0;
      let t = Math.imul(a ^ a >>> 15, 1 | a);
      t = t + Math.imul(t ^ t >>> 7, 61 | t) ^ t;
      return ((t ^ t >>> 14) >>> 0) / 4294967296;
    };
  }

  // ── THE STAR ARRAY ────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
  // TWO SOURCES, ONE SHAPE. When the real WebGL field is mounted it owns where
  // every record sits, and it hands back identity and position only:
  //
  //     window.__DC_STARS = [ { rec, x, y }, … ]     // x, y NORMALISED 0..1
  //
  //   · rec  — the record's index in PAIRS. The join key, and the only identity
  //            the host supplies; `id` is derived here as 's' + rec so the
  //            existing invariant (id IS the catalogue index) survives intact.
  //   · x, y — 0..1 of the field's box, top-left origin. NOT the 0..1000 `w`
  //            space below: that number is an internal artefact of generating
  //            dots, and a host projecting a 3D scene already has a normalised
  //            viewport. This file converts.
  //
  // Everything visual — size, alpha, drift animation, tier — stays here and is
  // IGNORED when the real field paints, per the protected-paths rule. The host
  // supplies no appearance and should not be asked to.
  //
  // WHY (a) AND NOT (b): field() keeps building the array rather than being
  // bypassed, so there is one code path and one shape. Same pattern as
  // __DC_DATA — the drop's own dots are the fallback, so the design file still
  // opens and behaves standalone, which is the property this depends on.
  //
  // THE ARRAY IS CATALOGUE-LENGTH when the host supplies it. 130 is the size of
  // a plausible-looking drawn sky, never a statement about how many records the
  // field holds — which is why hover was silently limited to the first 130.
  field(h) {
    const key = 'f' + h;
    if (this[key]) return this[key];
    const host = (typeof window !== 'undefined' && window.__DC_STARS) || null;
    if (host && host.length) {
      const built = host.map((s) => {
        const rec = s.rec;
        const p = this.data('PAIRS')[rec];
        return {
          id: 's' + rec, rec: rec,
          // fx is the percentage the chrome positions with; x and y are pixels
          // in the field's own box, which the proximity test and the lock ring
          // both measure in.
          fx: s.x, x: s.x * 1000, y: s.y * h,
          size: 3, alpha: 0.36, anim: 'sa-drift-1', dur: '14.0', delay: '0.0',
          artist: p ? p[0] : 'Record ' + String(rec + 1).padStart(3, '0'),
          title: p ? p[1] : 'artist · title from catalogue',
          ph: !p, hosted: true
        };
      });
      this[key] = built;
      return built;
    }
    // ══ THE DRAWN SKY IS THE TOOL'S, AND MUST NEVER REACH THE SITE ══════════
    // (2026-09-11, Tom, with screenshots of whitedsepulchre.music and
    // nnatapes.music: "your design Starmap, which is just representative and
    // shouldn't be carried over, is showing. Literally, I can click on a star
    // and see the fake dummy data… there are black stars behind, which are
    // from your design. It's literally superimposed.")
    //
    // He is right, and this is the RING BUG ONE LAYER DOWN. r49 deleted the
    // two freeze rings from the markup because engineering said a null VALUE
    // is a weaker guarantee than absent MARKUP. The dots kept exactly the
    // weaker guarantee: starVals suppressed itself on stars[0].hosted, a flag
    // only path (a) above ever sets. No __DC_STARS, no flag, and 489 invented
    // dots paint over the host's WebGL field.
    //
    // ⚠ AND IT IS NOT ONLY COSMETIC — IT IS THREE OF THE FOUR MAP FAULTS:
    //   · the fake names. Seats past PAIRS are labelled 'Record 086' and
    //     'artist · title from catalogue'. Those strings exist ONLY here.
    //   · the black dots behind the light-mode field. Path (b)'s alpha ink on
    //     a white ground, under the host's coloured stars.
    //   · NO HOVER AUDIO ON THE MAP. These dots sit at rnd(19) positions with
    //     no relation to where the host's stars actually are, so a press on a
    //     visible star lands on whatever invented dot is under that pixel —
    //     usually one with ph:true and nothing to play.
    //
    // So the whole map interaction on the site was running on the tool's
    // stand-in geometry. In site mode this returns EMPTY: the host's field is
    // the only field, and if __DC_STARS is absent the honest render is no
    // dots rather than 489 invented records. The design file still opens and
    // behaves standalone, which is the property this path exists for.
    // ⚠⚠ RETURNED, NOT CACHED (2026-09-11, engineering Q1 — and they are
    // right; r74 as shipped would have taken the field from wrong to EMPTY).
    //
    // Their point: __DC_STARS cannot exist at first render because the WebGL
    // field PRODUCES those coordinates, on the client, after it mounts. So the
    // first field(h) call always misses — and `if (this[key]) return this[key]`
    // would have frozen that miss for the life of the component, per height,
    // for good. An empty array is truthy, so the cache would have held it.
    //
    // CACHING AN ABSENCE IS THE BUG, not the timing. A cache is a promise that
    // the answer cannot have changed; "no stars yet" is precisely an answer
    // that will change. So site mode with no host returns [] and remembers
    // nothing, and the call after the stars land takes path (a) naturally.
    //
    // This is their option 2. It is necessary and NOT sufficient on its own —
    // something still has to ask again. That is _dcStars below, their option 1.
    if (this.siteMode()) return [];
    const r = this.rnd(19), w = 1000, out = [];
    let guard = 0;
    while (out.length < 130 && guard < 40000) {
      guard++;
      const x = 18 + r() * (w - 36), y = 16 + r() * (h - 32);
      let ok = true;
      for (let i = 0; i < out.length; i++) if (Math.hypot(out[i].x - x, out[i].y - y) < 30) { ok = false; break; }
      if (!ok) continue;
      const t = r();
      const tier = t > 0.9 ? 2 : t > 0.62 ? 1 : 0;
      out.push({
        id: 's' + out.length, x, y, fx: x / w,
        size: tier === 2 ? 6 : tier === 1 ? 4 : 3,
        alpha: tier === 2 ? 0.62 + r() * 0.14 : tier === 1 ? 0.36 + r() * 0.12 : 0.2 + r() * 0.08,
        anim: 'sa-drift-' + (1 + Math.floor(r() * 4)),
        dur: (12 + r() * 9).toFixed(1),
        delay: (-r() * 18).toFixed(1)
      });
    }
    out.forEach((s, i) => {
      if (i < this.data('PAIRS').length) { s.rec = i; s.artist = this.data('PAIRS')[i][0]; s.title = this.data('PAIRS')[i][1]; }
      else { s.artist = 'Record ' + String(i + 1).padStart(3, '0'); s.title = 'artist · title from catalogue'; s.ph = true; }
    });
    this[key] = out;
    return out;
  }

  // ── THE STAR FIELD BRIDGE ─────────────────────────────────────────────────
  // Four methods and one callback. That is the whole integration; if it is
  // taking more than that, something is being done twice.
  //
  // The problem these solve: when the real field is mounted, starVals is empty
  // (no CSS dots), so there are no per-star `enter`/`click` closures for the
  // host to call. These are the same behaviours addressed by RECORD INDEX
  // instead — which is the only identity the host has anyway.
  //
  //   FIELD → DESIGN (call these):
  //     atlas.starEnter(rec)   pointer over a star
  //     atlas.starLeave()      pointer off it
  //     atlas.starClick(rec)   click — holds it, or lets go if already held
  //     atlas.starClear()      click on empty space
  //
  //   DESIGN → FIELD (implement this):
  //     window.__DC_ON_STAR_STATE = ({ lock, hover }) => …
  //   Called whenever either changes, with catalogue indices or null. The field
  //   needs it because the design can move the selection WITHOUT the pointer
  //   ever touching the field — hovering a row in the release list sets
  //   hoverRow, Browse locks a record, the album panel's close clears it. A
  //   field that only listens to its own canvas will fall out of step with the
  //   column beside it.
  //
  // Reach them as `window.__DC_ATLAS` (set on mount).
  starEnter(rec) {
    const st = this.state;
    if (st.starLock != null) return;
    if (st.view !== 'album' || st.lock == null) {
      this.hoverEnter({ hoverStar: 's' + rec, hoverRow: rec, prev: st.view === 'album' ? st.prev : st.view });
    }
  }
  starLeave() {
    if (this.state.starLock == null) this.setState({ hoverStar: null, hoverRow: null });
  }
  // Mirrors the CSS dot's click exactly — including the phone branch, where the
  // hold stays on starLock alone because st.view === 'album' would swap the
  // compact card for the full album panel.
  starClick(rec) {
    const st = this.state, id = 's' + rec;
    const narrow = (st.winW || (this.VPS[st.vp] && this.VPS[st.vp][0]) || 1180) < 700;
    if (st.starLock === id) {
      this.setState(narrow
        ? { starLock: null, hoverStar: null }
        : { starLock: null, hoverStar: null, lock: null, view: st.prev || 'releases' });
      return;
    }
    const base = { starLock: id, hoverStar: id, started: true, prev: st.view === 'album' ? st.prev : st.view };
    this.setState(narrow ? base : Object.assign({}, base, { lock: rec, view: 'album' }));
  }
  starClear() {
    // ── A HOST RELEASE IS THE SAME RELEASE (2026-09-05, item 5) ─────────────
    // This cleared starLock and hoverStar but left `lock` and `view`, so a
    // host-initiated release left the album panel standing over a field with
    // nothing held — the seam the toggle-off path does not have. It now does
    // what releaseStar does: the same teardown whoever asks for it.
    this.setState({
      starLock: null, hoverStar: null, constellation: false,
      lock: null, view: this.state.prev || 'releases'
    });
  }
  // Push selection out to the field. Called from componentDidUpdate so it fires
  // for EVERY cause — a row hover, a Browse pick, the album panel closing — not
  // only for pointer events the field already saw.
  _pushStarState() {
    const fn = typeof window !== 'undefined' && window.__DC_ON_STAR_STATE;
    if (typeof fn !== 'function') return;
    const st = this.state;
    const idToRec = (id) => (id == null ? null : parseInt(String(id).slice(1), 10));
    const next = {
      lock: st.lock != null ? st.lock : idToRec(st.starLock),
      hover: st.hoverStar != null ? idToRec(st.hoverStar) : (st.hoverRow != null ? st.hoverRow : null)
    };
    if (this._lastStarState && this._lastStarState.lock === next.lock && this._lastStarState.hover === next.hover) return;
    this._lastStarState = next;
    fn(next);
  }
  // `athCat` records whose catalogue the star map is showing. ATH is a label
  // like any other here — Releases → Map gives it the same star field and the
  // same column as Northern Spy or NNA. It has nothing to do with the curator
  // map, which is a separate browse toy on the ATH landing page.
  go(v, athCat, asLabel) {
    // ⚠ DOOR 1 OF 6 — AND THIS COMMENT USED TO SAY 4 AND "every internal call
    // lands here", which was false (2026-09-14 audit). Five other doors wrote
    // route:'star' or route:'ath-map' by direct setState and never reached this
    // branch: arrival as ATH, a ?card= deep link, goAth(), goCard(), and the
    // ⋮ menu's Open in the map. All six now go through phoneMapPatch or carry
    // their own guard; the grep that proves it is route: 'star'|'ath-map'.
    // This branch covers Explore, the label picker, the route picker and the nav.
    // A phone gets the catalogue — the same records, one surface simpler.
    if (this.phoneNoMap()) {
      this.setState(this.pinLabel({ route: 'catalogue', view: 'releases', athCat: !!athCat, lock: null, hoverStar: null, hoverRow: null, prev: null, open: true, menuOpen: false, browseOpen: false, explore: false, cart: false }, asLabel));
      return;
    }
    this.setState(this.pinLabel({ route: 'star', view: v, athCat: !!athCat, lock: null, hoverStar: null, hoverRow: null, prev: null, open: true, menuOpen: false, browseOpen: false, explore: false, cart: false }, asLabel));
  }

  // Across the Horizon is light by default (15b) — entering its site sets the
  // theme the way picking a label in Explore does for the other four.
  // THE ATH ENTRY POINT (2026-09-04). Every arrival at Across the Horizon
  // lands on THE IDEA — card 0 of the curator-map deck — per Tom's ruling that
  // a site's landing comes first and the star map is a secondary choice. It is
  // one method so Explore, the label row and Home cannot drift apart, and so
  // the deleted 'ath-home' route can never be reached by accident again.
  // It is goCard(0) plus closing whatever menu you came through: setting
  // label: null here broke the chrome, because LABELS is indexed by it — ATH
  // keeps the arriving label's index and is marked separate by route, not by a
  // null label.
  goAthIdea() {
    this._mapPass = 0;
    // The phone guard is GONE (2026-09-15) — ATH's landing is a phone page. Its
    // desktop target was already `ath-map`, so removing the redirect leaves one
    // destination for both widths rather than exposing a 'star' write.
    this.setState({
      route: 'ath-map', theme: 'light', panel: 0,
      // §1 · states ATH, same reason as goAth below.
      labelKey: 'across-the-horizon',
      menuOpen: false, browseOpen: false, explore: false,
      cart: false, queue: false, hoverStar: null
    });
  }
  // goAth STATES its label rather than carrying one: it is ATH's navigator, and
  // the dev picker's ATH rows come through here from whatever site was last on
  // screen. Carrying would pin Northern Spy and then outrank ATH's own route
  // prefix — the §1 bug with the sign flipped.
  goAth(r) {
    this.setState(this.pinLabel({ route: r, theme: 'light', menuOpen: false, browseOpen: false, explore: false, cart: false, queue: false, hoverStar: null }, 'across-the-horizon'));
  }

  // Land on the ATH deck and show one card. Card order is fixed: the idea,
  // the core idea, the curator map, the drops, the podcast, the journal, the
  // membership offer.
  goCard(i) {
    this._mapPass = 0;
    // Guard removed with goAthIdea's, and for the same reason: a card is one of
    // ATH's own cards, the destination is `ath-map` at both widths, and Tom's
    // ruling is explicit — "Do not suppress curator-map."
    this.setState({ route: 'ath-map', theme: 'light', panel: i, labelKey: 'across-the-horizon', menuOpen: false, browseOpen: false, explore: false, cart: false, queue: false, hoverStar: null });
  }

  // ── Phone curator map · ported from Map Phone Sandbox ──────────────────
  // Geometry authored at the phone width and never used by the desktop path.
  phGeo(W) {
    const C = 42, I = 30, CY = 46, NAME_H = 20, LABEL_H = 26, GUEST_DY = 44, CLEAR = 10;
    const trioBottom = CY + GUEST_DY + I / 2 + LABEL_H;
    const nextTop = CY - C / 2 - NAME_H;
    return {
      W: W, PAD: 10, COLS: 4, C: C, I: I, CY: CY, NAME_H: NAME_H, LABEL_H: LABEL_H,
      HEAD: 26, READER: 0, BAND: 124,
      ROW: Math.ceil(trioBottom - nextTop + CLEAR)
    };
  }

  phLayoutClump(keys, leftRoom, rightRoom, nameLines, bandRanks) {
    const GAP = 4, D_MIN = 11, D_MAX = 15;
    const WIDTH = Math.max(40, leftRoom + rightRoom);
    // The label wraps, so its height follows its line count rather than a
    // constant: one line ≈ 9px, two ≈ 18px.
    const NAME_H = (nameLines > 1 ? 18 : 9) + 2;
    // How many dots one rank can hold at the smallest acceptable size; the rest
    // wrap to a second rank within the same column.
    const perRank = Math.max(1, Math.floor((WIDTH + GAP) / (D_MIN + GAP)));
    // ONE rank, always — the row shows what it can hold.
    const ranks = 1;
    const BOX_H = NAME_H + D_MAX + 2;
    // A stable hash per connection, so a dot keeps its size and level between
    // renders and between sessions.
    const h = (k, salt) => {
      let x = 2166136261 ^ salt;
      const str = String(k);
      for (let i = 0; i < str.length; i++) { x ^= str.charCodeAt(i); x = (x * 16777619) >>> 0; }
      return (x >>> 8) / 0xffffff;
    };
    const shown = keys.slice(0, perRank);
    const n = shown.length;
    const pts = shown.map((k, i) => ({
      d: Math.round(D_MIN + h(k, 1) * (D_MAX - D_MIN)),
      lane: h(k, 2)
    }));
    // Lay out left to right with a varying advance; the span is centred later.
    const rowSize = n;
    let x = 0;
    pts.forEach((p, i) => {
      p.rank = Math.floor(i / rowSize);
      // Each rank starts its own run.
      if (i % rowSize === 0) x = 0;
      const prev = (i % rowSize === 0) ? null : pts[i - 1];
      if (prev) {
        const want = (prev.d + p.d) / 2 + GAP;
        x += want * (0.82 + h(shown[i], 3) * 0.5);
      }
      p.x = x;
      // Levels are continuous rather than two rungs, so nothing lines up. A dot
      // under the name is held below its band.
      // Every dot sits below the name — never over it — inside its own rank.
      const rTop = NAME_H + p.rank * (D_MAX + GAP);
      const rBottom = Math.min(BOX_H - p.d, rTop + (D_MAX - p.d));
      p.y = Math.round(rTop + p.lane * Math.max(0, rBottom - rTop));
    });
    // Relaxation: push overlapping pairs apart along x until each has a gap.
    for (let pass = 0; pass < 6; pass++) {
      let moved = false;
      // EVERY pair, not just neighbours in one rank: a cross-rank pair whose
      // x-ranges overlap is exactly the case that was slipping through.
      for (let i = 0; i < n; i++) {
        for (let j = i + 1; j < n; j++) {
          const a2 = pts[i], b2 = pts[j];
          const dx = b2.x - a2.x, dy = b2.y - a2.y;
          const need = (a2.d + b2.d) / 2 + GAP;
          const dist = Math.sqrt(dx * dx + dy * dy);
          if (dist < need) {
            const push = need - Math.abs(dx) + 0.5;
            if (push > 0) for (let k = j; k < n; k++) pts[k].x += push;
            moved = true;
          }
        }
      }
      if (!moved) break;
    }
    const measure = () => {
      const first = pts[0], last = pts[n - 1];
      return pts.length ? (last.x + last.d / 2) - (first.x - first.d / 2) : 0;
    };
    // THE INVARIANT: the span may not exceed the column plus a bounded borrow
    // into the gutter that sits free between clumps. If relaxation overshot it,
    // the dots shrink and the advances compress until it holds — separation is
    // preserved because both terms scale together.
    // The clump may occupy its own room in each direction, and no more.
    const LIMIT = WIDTH;
    for (let guard = 0; guard < 8; guard++) {
      const span0 = measure();
      if (span0 <= LIMIT || span0 <= 0) break;
      const k = LIMIT / span0;
      const x0 = pts[0].x;
      pts.forEach(p => {
        // Advances compress; the dot itself does not.
        p.x = x0 + (p.x - x0) * k;
      });
    }
    const span = measure();
    const start = pts.length ? (pts[0].x - pts[0].d / 2) : 0;
    let shift = -start - span / 2;
    pts.forEach(p => { p.cx = p.x + shift; });
    // Slide the finished span so it lies inside [-leftRoom, +rightRoom].
    const lo = Math.min.apply(null, pts.map(p => p.cx - p.d / 2));
    const hi = Math.max.apply(null, pts.map(p => p.cx + p.d / 2));
    let slide = 0;
    if (lo < -leftRoom) slide = -leftRoom - lo;
    else if (hi > rightRoom) slide = rightRoom - hi;
    if (slide) pts.forEach(p => { p.cx += slide; });
    return { pts: pts, span: span, boxH: BOX_H };
  }

  phBare(n) { return String(n).split(':')[0]; }

  // layoutClump DELETED 2026-09-09 (code audit part 6). It was a byte-identical
  // twin of phLayoutClump with ZERO callers — 98 lines, differing only in the
  // `ph` prefix. The unprefixed name is the one a reader takes for canonical,
  // so a clump-geometry fix would have landed in the dead copy and changed
  // nothing on screen. One expression, one name.

  // Members are the first three columns, empties dropped; the note is always
  // index 3, never "the last non-empty".
  phMembersOf(c) { return c.slice(0, 3).filter(Boolean); }
  phNoteOf(c) { return c[3] || ''; }

  // ── THE LANDING DECK IN THE COLUMN · FLIPPING (2026-09-09, Tom) ──────────
  // "It should be cards that I can flip through with the arrow, boom, boom,
  // boom, just like the phone." The phone's deck is a scroll-snap stack and so
  // is this one — one mechanism, so a card cannot end up half-shown on one
  // surface and snapped on the other. The arrows and the menu both move it by
  // scrolling, and the card index is read back from the scroll position rather
  // than kept as a second copy of where we are.
  // The column deck, in order, with the heading each card gives the drawer.
  // The menu's numbered items index straight into this (see the hamburger's
  // handler), and the header reads its title from here rather than from the
  // view — a card is a page and names itself.
  // ── THE COLUMN'S RELEASE ROWS ───────────────────────────────────────────
  // One builder behind two cards: the drops card asks for three groups, the
  // releases card for all of them. A group is a drop — its heading over its
  // three tracks in the column's release-row form — and every row's click,
  // hover and ⋮ are the drop's OWN handlers, so a row here does exactly what
  // the same row does on the landing page.
  // ── ONE GATE FOR EVERY RELEASE SURFACE (2026-09-10, Tom) ─────────────────
  // Methods, not locals, because three surfaces ask the same question — the
  // Starmap column's list, the volumes list and the drops card — and the live
  // fault was precisely that they answered it differently. A predicate that
  // lives in one builder is a predicate the next surface will re-invent.
  // ══ ONE RATIO GATE FOR EVERY PHOTOGRAPH (2026-09-11, Tom: "one of the
  // artist photos is just a tiny cropped vertical or horizontal line") ══════
  // SIX call sites put a raw data value straight into aspectRatio. Absent or
  // unparseable yields the string "undefined", CSS drops the declaration, and
  // a box with no other height collapses onto its caption — the sliver. A
  // method, not six copies, for the same reason athDropIsPublic is one: a
  // predicate living in one builder is one the next builder re-invents.
  //
  // CLAMPED, not merely defaulted. 0.08 and 12 are as destructive as undefined
  // and are what a bad EXIF read actually produces. 0.5–2.0 is 1:2 through
  // 2:1, which holds every real portrait in the set including
  // dave-harrington.jpg at 928×1400 (0.663); outside it the photograph is
  // still real, so the box squares up and the image fills by cover rather
  // than the row breaking.
  arOf(v) {
    const n = typeof v === 'number' ? v : parseFloat(v);
    if (!isFinite(n) || n <= 0) return 1;
    return Math.max(0.5, Math.min(2, n));
  }
  // ══ THE PHONE HAS NO MAP (2026-09-14, Tom: "I'm considering limiting mobile
  // to grid and player view: grid view with player audio on it, no map and
  // hover, just to make it simpler to display and more consistent") ═════════
  //
  // ONE PREDICATE, FIVE CALLERS. The map is not removed from the file — it is
  // made UNREACHABLE on a narrow viewport, which is a different and much safer
  // thing. phoneMap() is 558 lines, the star card is 27 keys and the drawer is
  // 17 more; ripping those out in one pass is how this file gets broken. Gate
  // the four doors instead and every one of them goes quiet by itself.
  //
  // ⚠ AND `route === 'star'` CANNOT BE THE GATE. It is load-bearing for four
  // views beyond the field — album, results, artists and ath-curator — and two
  // of those are things a phone must keep: opening a record and browsing by
  // category. Blocking the route would have taken both out. The DOORS are the
  // gate: go(), openAlbum(), arrival() and the View toggle.
  //
  // Everything downstream then follows for free, because the field, the star
  // card and the sheet all mount behind isStar / starHoverNarrow.
  phoneNoMap() { return this._narrowNow(); }
  // ── AND ONE PATCH EVERY MAP-BOUND DOOR USES (2026-09-14, audit) ──────────
  // goStarFn's note claimed "DOOR 1 OF 4 · every internal 'show me the map'
  // call lands here". It was wrong, and the audit found SIX doors, five of
  // them writing route:'star' or route:'ath-map' by direct setState and so
  // walking straight past that branch:
  //
  //   · arrival with __DC_LABEL = across-the-horizon   (ATH lands ON the map)
  //   · a ?card= deep link into a curator card
  //   · goAth() and goCard(), the two internal ATH map entries
  //   · the ⋮ menu's "Open in the map" — see CTX, now withheld on a phone
  //
  // A claim about how many doors exist is worth nothing; a PATCH every door
  // must use is worth something. This is that patch: it clears every map-only
  // piece of state so nothing survives the redirect half-set.
  //
  // ⚠ AND THE CENSUS ABOVE IS STILL NOT THE WHOLE LIST. Engineering found two
  // more on 09-14 — goToNav's route write, which is the door THEY drive, and
  // the stored viewMode restore, which opens by itself on load with no link
  // and no tap. Both gated now, at eight. The lesson is that counting doors is
  // the wrong instinct: what makes the rule hold is that every write of
  // route: 'star' passes a phoneNoMap() test, and the only way to keep that
  // true is to grep for the write rather than trust a number in a comment.
  // ══ THE NOTE IS THE DOOR'S TO RAISE, NOT THIS PATCH'S (2026-09-14,
  // engineering §4) ════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
  // ⚠ phoneMapNote: 1 WAS HARD-CODED HERE, so every door raised it — including
  // goAthIdea() and goCard(), which are IN-APP TAPS that were never going to
  // the map. A visitor tapping a curator card got "the star map needs a wider
  // screen", explaining a redirect from a place they had not asked for.
  //
  // The note answers exactly one question: WHY DID THIS LINK NOT LAND WHERE IT
  // POINTED. Only an arrival-class door can be asked that — a shared link, a
  // ?card= deep link, an ATH label arrival. An in-app tap has no link to
  // explain, so it redirects silently.
  //
  // Each caller now passes phoneMapNote: 1 or does not, and this stops
  // deciding. arrival()'s paths pass it; goAthIdea and goCard do not.
  phoneMapPatch(extra) {
    return Object.assign({
      route: 'catalogue', view: 'releases',
      lock: null, hoverStar: null, hoverRow: null, prev: null, open: true,
      menuOpen: false, browseOpen: false, explore: false, cart: false
    }, extra || {});
  }
  // ── THE ATH EXT ID, AND WHY IT IS NOT POSITIONAL (2026-09-15, engineering) ─
  // It was 'ath-d<vi>-<di>-<ti>', where di indexes the drops array buildDrops
  // walks. That array comes from VOLS_RELEASED — a list the HOST builds by
  // filtering unreleased drops out and RE-INDEXING. So the id's coordinates
  // were positions in a filtered lineup, not identities.
  //
  // Engineering caught what that costs: the ids "happen to line up today only
  // because the unreleased drops form a contiguous tail; one drop held back
  // mid-series and every id after it silently points at the wrong track."
  // Silently is the word that matters — nothing throws, the wrong track plays.
  //
  // The drop's NUMBER is data, not a position: d[0] in VOLS, the same value
  // every title in this file is built from ('Vol. 2 Drop 5'). Volume and drop
  // number address a drop whatever is filtered out around it.
  //
  // ⚠ Fixed NOW, before the namespace is resolved rather than after, because
  // the flaw survives both of the choices in front of Tom: if those rows revert
  // to requiring a PAIRS record the id disappears, and if the host resolves the
  // namespace it would otherwise be resolving a broken key.
  athExtId(r, ti) {
    return 'ath-v' + (r.vi + 1) + '-d' + r.num + '-t' + (ti || 0);
  }
  athTrackIsPublic(t) {
    return !!(t && t.titleText
      && !/pending|^tba$|^untitled$|^t\.?b\.?[ad]\.?$/i.test(String(t.titleText).trim()));
  }
  athDropIsPublic(d) {
    if (!d) return false;
    const row = this.athDropRow(d.title);
    if (row && row.live === false) return false;
    if (!d.released) return false;
    const trk = d.tracks || [];
    // No tracks at all is a drop with nothing to withhold — the old behaviour,
    // kept. Any tracks, and at least one of them has to be public.
    return trk.length ? trk.some((t) => this.athTrackIsPublic(t)) : true;
  }
  athColGroups(st, limit) {
        // ── "THE LATEST DROPS" MEANS THE LATEST RELEASED ONES ────────────────────
    // (2026-09-09, Tom, on the live site: the card was heading Vol. 2 Drops 8, 7
    // and 6, and only 6 is out.)
    //
    // THREE TESTS, because no single one of them is answerable from the data as
    // it ships today. A drop is out only if all three agree:
    //
    //   1. VOLS[…].drops[…][4], the released flag. ⚠ ABSENT MEANS 1 by
    //      construction, so older data behaves as before — and an unreleased
    //      drop carrying no flag reads as OUT. That is what put Drop 8 first.
    //   2. ATH_DROPS.live, joined on the drop title. Explicit where it exists;
    //      a drop with no row falls back rather than disappearing, because
    //      absence is not a denial.
    //   3. A REAL TITLE — at least one of the three tracks is named. A drop
    //      whose every row reads "Title pending" has nothing to name and
    //      nothing to play, so it is not a release whatever a flag says. This
    //      is the only one of the three that is true of the data TODAY, which
    //      is why the card is right on the live site before engineering send
    //      anything.
    //
    // ⚠ ENGINEERING: (3) is a GUARD, not the contract. Set
    // VOLS[…].drops[…][4] = 0 on every unreleased drop — or give every drop an
    // ATH_DROPS row with live — and this card follows the flag alone, which is
    // what it should do: a drop can legitimately be announced before its titles
    // land, and only you can say which of those it is.
    // ── AND THE GATE IS PER TRACK, NOT PER DROP (2026-09-10, Tom: "unreleased
    // ones, ones that aren't public, shouldn't be out… these sidebars and any
    // release schedule should show newest public tracks on top always") ──────
    // ⚠ AND ALL THREE TESTS ARE NOW A FALLBACK. VOLS_RELEASED (read at the top
    // of this builder) is the host stating which drops are public, so the tests
    // below only decide when that table is absent. Test (3) asked whether ANY
    // track in the drop was named, so one
    // real title carried its two unnamed siblings onto the page — which is
    // exactly what the live list showed: Vol. 2 Drop 8 heading the column with
    // "My Big Tummy As Your Pillow" and then two rows reading "Title pending"
    // over placeholder art. A drop is a container; PUBLIC is a fact about each
    // record in it, so the test belongs one level down.
    //
    // SELF-SETTING, which was the actual ask. Nothing here is a list of what to
    // hide: a track appears when it has a real title, and the newest drop with
    // at least one such track is therefore the top of the list by arithmetic
    // rather than by anyone remembering to move it. When Drop 8's titles land
    // it rises on its own; until then Drop 7 leads and no one edits anything.
    const isPublicTrack = (t) => this.athTrackIsPublic(t);
    const all = this.buildDrops(st).shown.filter((d) => this.athDropIsPublic(d));
        const drops = limit ? all.slice(0, limit) : all;
        return drops.map((d, gi) => ({
          key: 'acdg' + gi,
          label: d.title,
          meta: gi === 0 ? 'Newest' : '',
          headStyle: {
            display: 'flex', alignItems: 'baseline', justifyContent: 'space-between', gap: '10px',
            padding: gi === 0 ? '0 20px 8px' : '18px 20px 8px',
            fontFamily: "'JetBrains Mono', monospace", fontSize: '9px', letterSpacing: '0.18em',
            textTransform: 'uppercase', color: 'var(--ink)'
          },
          metaStyle: gi === 0
            ? { fontSize: '8px', letterSpacing: '0.16em', color: 'var(--gold)' }
            : { display: 'none' },
          // ⚠ THE ORIGINAL INDEX TRAVELS WITH THE TRACK. athDropTrack(title, ti)
          // is positional against the drop's own tracks[], so filtering the
          // array first would have re-based ti and handed every surviving row
          // its neighbour's cover, handlers and address.
          rows: (d.tracks || []).map((t, ti) => [t, ti]).filter((p) => isPublicTrack(p[0])).map((p) => {
            const t = p[0], ti = p[1];
            const trk = this.athDropTrack(d.title, ti);
            const cover = trk ? trk[4] : '';
            // Lit on the same fact the deck's row uses — the drop's own row
            // style already resolved it, so the two cannot disagree.
            const on = /138,106,18,0\.14/.test(String((t.rowStyle || {}).background));
            return {
              key: 'acr' + gi + '-' + ti,
              box: {
                position: 'relative', display: 'grid', gridTemplateColumns: '44px 1fr 1.2fr 22px',
                alignItems: 'center', gap: '10px', padding: '7px 20px', minHeight: '58px',
                boxSizing: 'border-box', cursor: 'pointer', overflow: 'hidden',
                borderBottom: '1px solid var(--hair)',
                background: on ? 'rgba(138,106,18,0.14)' : 'transparent',
                transition: 'background 120ms linear',
                fontFamily: "'JetBrains Mono', monospace", fontSize: '11.5px'
              },
              cover: {
                width: '44px', height: '44px', borderRadius: '5px', boxSizing: 'border-box',
                border: '1px solid ' + (on ? 'var(--gold)' : 'var(--hair20)'),
                background: cover
                  ? 'var(--tile)'
                  : 'repeating-linear-gradient(135deg, var(--tile) 0 6px, transparent 6px 12px)'
              },
              coverRef: this.lazyRef(cover),
              who: String(t.name || '').split('  ·')[0],
              // ── THE ARTIST LEADS (2026-09-10, Tom) ────────────────────────
              // This row had the artist at ink55 and the title at full ink, so
              // the faint half was the one the project's own rule puts first:
              // "Never an album title without its artist", and on the map
              // "Artist on top, title beneath — on the map the byline leads".
              // Side by side the same hierarchy has to hold in WEIGHT, since
              // there is no above and below to carry it. Artist takes the ink,
              // the title takes ink70, and gold still marks what is sounding.
              whoStyle: { color: 'var(--ink)', overflow: 'hidden', textOverflow: 'ellipsis', whiteSpace: 'nowrap' },
              ttl: t.titleText,
              // ── ⚠ THE ROW IS A LINK NOW (2026-09-10) ──────────────────────
              // Engineering: acrossthehorizon.music/releases rendered TWO
              // anchors where the other four labels render one per record
              // (206/142/71/55), and across all fifteen ATH pages there was not
              // a single /album/ href. All 42 records resolve — there was simply
              // no way to reach one by clicking.
              //
              // ⚠ AND THE CRAWL LAYER WAS EMITTING THEM ANYWAY: 42 correct
              // anchors in the served HTML, removed from the DOM at hydration,
              // so crawlers could see what people could not. That is the worst
              // version of this bug rather than a mitigation of it. A row that
              // LOOKS like the other labels' catalogue rows has to behave like
              // one.
              //
              // ⚠ AND THE ADDRESS IS NOT DERIVED — that is the r49 ruling and I
              // very nearly broke it here. My first pass built the handle from
              // the artist and title, which is exactly what engineering
              // measured as making r47/r48 WORSE than r45 (199 dead links
              // instead of 118). addr('album') takes the PAIRS ROW and returns
              // row[7] or nothing; with no row[7] the site emits NO ANCHOR,
              // which both sides agreed is the safe failure.
              //
              // So the row links exactly when its seat resolves a record AND
              // that record carries an address. The other four labels render
              // one anchor per record because their rows ARE PAIRS rows; an ATH
              // seat is a person on a drop, and recFor() is the only bridge
              // from a name to a record.
              //
              // ⚠ recFor() FLATTENS, so this closes the resolvable seats and
              // not the rest: 9 of 48 today, the last of Volume 2. seats[] is
              // what closes the other 39 — a seat is a PERSON ON A DROP, so two
              // seats of one artist are two different tracks, and recFor cannot
              // say that. Until it lands, an unresolved seat renders no anchor
              // rather than a guess.
              href: (() => {
                const rec = this.recFor(String(t.name || '').split('  ·')[0]);
                if (rec == null) return null;
                return this.addr('album', (this.data('PAIRS') || [])[rec]);
              })(),
              // Stops the row's play handler, so the title navigates and does
              // not also start the track.
              ttlClick: (e) => { if (e && e.stopPropagation) e.stopPropagation(); },
              ttlStyle: {
                minWidth: 0, paddingRight: '6px', overflow: 'hidden', textOverflow: 'ellipsis',
                whiteSpace: 'nowrap', color: on ? 'var(--gold)' : 'var(--ink70)',
                textDecoration: 'none'
              },
              dotsStyle: t.dotsStyle,
              dots: t.dots,
              click: t.click,
              enter: t.enter,
              leave: t.leave
            };
          })
        }));
  }

  ATH_COL = [
    ['The idea', 'the series'],
    ['The core idea', 'discovery through curation'],
    ['The Curator Map', 'sixteen drops, two volumes'],
    ['The drops', 'the latest release'],
    ['Broadcast', 'podcast and radio'],
    ['The Journal', 'writing and interviews'],
    ['Membership', 'the subscription'],
    ['Releases', 'every drop, newest first']
  ];
  ATH_COL_N = 8;
  // ── EVERY CARD HAS AN ADDRESS (2026-09-11, Tom) ──────────────────────────
  // "When I go to the Idea, Curator Map, the Podcast, and Subscribe, it needs
  // to have a URL that can be copied and seen. Refreshed and uses a back
  // button."
  //
  // Those four were deck CARDS — panel indices behind athColOpen(n) — so they
  // had no href, never reached the nav tuple, and the host had nothing to
  // write. Copy gave you the page you arrived on, refresh lost the card, and
  // Back skipped every one of them. Not a missing feature so much as four
  // pages that were never addressable.
  //
  // ⚠ ALL EIGHT ARE LISTED, not just Tom's four. A half-filled table is the
  // trap here: the next card to be linked would look supported, resolve to
  // undefined and land on card 0 in silence.
  //
  // ⚠ AND THE KEY IS THE NAME, NOT THE INDEX — the same ruling as conAnchor
  // and the article slug. Card order has already changed once; an index in a
  // URL is a promise this file cannot keep across a reorder, and a stale
  // bookmark would open a different page rather than fail.
  ATH_CARD_SLUG = ['the-idea', 'the-core-idea', 'curator-map', 'the-drops', 'broadcast', 'journal', 'subscribe', 'releases'];
  athCardSlug(i) { return this.ATH_CARD_SLUG[Math.max(0, Math.min(this.ATH_COL_N - 1, i || 0))]; }
  // ⚠ SECOND INSTANCE OF THE SAME FAMILY (2026-09-14, engineering §3).
  // ATH_CARD_SLUG is declared BELOW state, and arrival()'s ?card= branch calls
  // this — so it threw on .indexOf before phoneNoMap was even reached. Guarded
  // rather than moved: the field's position is load-bearing elsewhere, and a
  // method that cannot answer during construction should say so by returning
  // null, which is exactly what its callers already handle.
  athCardIndex(slug) {
    const table = this.ATH_CARD_SLUG;
    if (!table || !table.indexOf) return null;
    const k = table.indexOf(String(slug || '').replace(/^.*\//, ''));
    return k > -1 ? k : null;
  }
  athColEl() { return (typeof document === 'undefined') ? null : document.querySelector('[data-ath-col]'); }
  athColGo(i) {
    const k = Math.max(0, Math.min(this.ATH_COL_N - 1, i));
    if (this.state.athCard !== k) this.setState({ athCard: k });
  }
  // From a menu item: the column has to exist before it can be scrolled, so the
  // scroll waits a frame on the view change rather than racing it.
  // One step per gesture, locked for the length of the transition — a trackpad
  // sends a burst of small deltas and an unlocked handler would run the whole
  // deck off the end. Same 620ms lock the landing deck's wheel uses.
  athColStep(dy) {
    const now = Date.now();
    if (this._athColLock && now - this._athColLock < 420) return;
    if (Math.abs(dy) < 6) return;
    this._athColLock = now;
    this.athColGo((this.state.athCard || 0) + (dy > 0 ? 1 : -1));
  }
  athColKey = (e) => {
    if (!e || this.state.view !== 'ath-curator' || this.state.route !== 'star') return;
    const t = e.target, tag = t && t.tagName;
    if (tag === 'INPUT' || tag === 'TEXTAREA') return;
    if (e.key === 'ArrowDown' || e.key === 'PageDown') { e.preventDefault(); this.athColStep(1); }
    else if (e.key === 'ArrowUp' || e.key === 'PageUp') { e.preventDefault(); this.athColStep(-1); }
  };
  athColOpen(i) {
    this.setState({ athCard: Math.max(0, Math.min(this.ATH_COL_N - 1, i)) });
    this.go('ath-curator', true);
  }

  // ── The curator map IN THE DRAWER (2026-09-09) ──────────────────────────
  // ONE phoneMap call at the column's width, with the band panel exposed under
  // athDrawer* names.
  //
  // ⚠ Why this method exists. phoneMap returns the bands AND the Connections
  // panel that opens under them (bandStyle / bandTitleStyle / bandRowStyle /
  // story / storyStyle / clumps). Those reach the PHONE template through the
  // Object.assign at the foot of renderVals — `if (narrow && route ===
  // 'ath-map')` — which by construction never runs on a desktop route. The
  // drawer took only `.bands`, so its panel's six holes resolved to nothing:
  // the bands drew, a drop selected, and the panel stayed a 0-height nothing.
  // Tom: "the connection section isn't opening up." It was opening; it had no
  // styles and no clumps to open with.
  //
  // Prefixed rather than bare because the phone's Object.assign runs LAST and
  // would otherwise silently win over the drawer's values on any route where
  // both were present. Same fact, one expression, two named readers.
  athDrawerVals(st, H) {
    const m = this.phoneMap(st, 380, H);
    return {
      athDrawerBands: m.bands,
      athDrawerBandStyle: m.bandStyle,
      athDrawerBandTitleStyle: m.bandTitleStyle,
      athDrawerBandRowStyle: m.bandRowStyle,
      athDrawerStory: m.story,
      athDrawerStoryStyle: m.storyStyle,
      athDrawerClumps: m.clumps
    };
  }

  // ── Phone curator map · render values ──────────────────────────────────
  // The sandbox's own render body, verbatim but for its device picker: the
  // frame is the card's window and the width is the phone's.
  // ── AUTHORED LIGHT-ONLY, NOW THEMED (2026-09-11, Tom: "I don't know what
  // else is not visible in light mode or dark mode… there could be a menu item
  // that's dark on light or black on black") ───────────────────────────────
  // This whole builder carried eleven hardcoded light-mode values and not one
  // token — white sheets, #17191b-family ink, light hairlines — so in dark
  // mode the sheets stayed white and every label on the sky was near-black ink
  // on a near-black ground. Invisible, not merely wrong.
  //
  // It reads the same tokens as the rest of the file now, so it flips with the
  // theme instead of describing one of them. The scroll-fade is the exception
  // that still branches: a gradient has to fade to the GROUND colour, and
  // "transparent" in a gradient resolves to transparent-black, which greys the
  // ramp — so each theme states its own start and end.
  phoneMap(st, W, H) {
    // 'half' on arrival, 'full' once pulled up.
    const sheet = st.sheet || 'half';
    // The card gives the map its window. Half-open that is the pane below the
    // 44px bar, less the floating transport. PULLED UP the drawer is the whole
    // screen — over the header, no bottom margin — so the map gets every pixel
    // and scales up instead of down. That is the point of pulling it up.
    const frameH = sheet === 'full' ? Math.max(320, H - 20) : Math.max(320, H - 70 - 40);
    const G = this.phGeo(W);
    const trioExt = G.CY + 44 + G.I / 2 + 18;
    // Closed: the band is a 10px spacer. Open: title + story + two dot ranks.
    // STORY_H is declared here because BOTH the band's open height and the
    // story's own reserved box read it — a fixed 30px box was masking the third
    // line of every longer note. Four lines at 9.5px/1.4 clears the corpus.
    const STORY_H = 54;
    const BAND_CLOSED = 10, BAND_OPEN = 12 + 10 + 8 + STORY_H + 8 + 30 + 10;
    // Half-open the drawer starts 210px down the pane, so its inner height is
    // nothing like the full-screen frame. Whether the composition FITS decides
    // if the field centres it or flows it from the top.
    // 70 is the phone pane's top edge under the Safari-sized chrome (14 + 44 + 12).
    const sheetInnerH = sheet === 'full' ? frameH : Math.max(120, (H - 70) - 210);
    // The fit pass has to reckon with the field's OWN padding, or it under-
    // shrinks by exactly that much and the composition then reads as "too tall
    // to centre" — which is why every phone except 390×844 fell back to
    // top-alignment and left all its slack at the bottom.
    // A little more air above than below: the grab handle lives up there.
    // Clearance under the drawer's open/close BUTTON, which is 48px tall now
    //
